Bringing Back Dignity: Identification of the Missing in the Homeland War

Izloženi osobni predmeti korišteni u identifikaciji žrtava u obdukcijskoj sali Kliničkoga bolničkog centra Split (izvor: arhiv Kliničkoga zavoda za patologiju, sudsku medicinu i citologiju KBC-a Split).

Personal belongings used in the identification of victims displayed in the autopsy room of the University Hospital of Split (source: Archive of the Clinical Department of Pathology, Forensic Medicine, and Cytology, University Hospital of Split).

In one case, a dentist came with the family and identified the body because she remembered the work she had done on the person’s tooth just before the war. We usually didn’t have dental records, which is something American teams rely on heavily. Once the family told us who they believed it was, we would proceed with verification. We established anthropological characteristics such as height, age, and sex, and tried to determine the cause of death as far as possible. After that, we would take DNA samples.

Leskur Staničić: Is there any case that has particularly stayed with you?

PDF page 29

nismo mogli identificirati njenog brata. Brat joj se često pojavljivao u snovima. U jednom od njih ona je sanjala da je on ubijen u automobilu. Možda je već i deseti put došla k nama kad nam je ispričala svoj san, nakon čega smo pregledali zapisnike jer smo imali bilješku da je u jednoj vreći sakupljeno automobilsko staklo. I onda smo DNA analizom utvrdili da je to stvarno njezin brat! Njezin san je bio vjerodostojan. Ona nije odustajala dok nije to riješila, dolazila je s grupom ili sama, ali nije imala mira dok ga nije identificirala. Tu njenu upornost ne mogu zaboraviti. Kao što mi je ostala u sjećanju sudbina kupreške novinarke koju su ušutkali da ne širi istinu. Njezini roditelji zabi-

Identifikacija žrtava u obdukcijskoj sali Kliničkoga bolničkog centra Split (foto: Phil Farnsworth, uz dopuštenje).

Identification of victims in the autopsy room of the University Hospital of Split (photo: Phil Farnsworth, used with permission).

Anđelinović: It was during the war that I came to realize that the strongest bond is often the one between a brother and a sister. There was one woman who kept coming to the autopsy room. We couldn’t identify her brother. He kept appearing in her dreams. In one of them, she dreamt that he had been killed in a car. Maybe it was the tenth time she came to see us when she told us about it. After that, we went back through the records and found a note that in one of the bags, there had been fragments of car glass. Then, through DNA analysis, we confirmed that it really was her brother! Her dream turned out to be true. She never gave up. She kept coming, sometimes with a group, sometimes alone, but she had no peace until he was identified. I will never forget her persistence. And I also remember the fate of a journalist from Kupres who was silenced so she would not spread the truth. Her parents were captured on that footage. She was not afraid. She refused to leave Kupres. It was deeply moving!

Leskur Staničić: I imagine the hardest part was facing the parents and having to tell them the results of your analyses.

Anđelinović: Certainly. The handover of the body to the family followed a protocol. We would first go through the standard anthropological findings, describing the characteristics of the remains. Then we would explain the DNA results, showing that the DNA from their blood matched the DNA from the bones,

PDF page 30

lježeni su na snimkama. Nije se bojala, nije htjela otići iz Kupresa. Potresno!

Leskur Staničić: Vjerojatno najteži dio je bio onaj kad ste suočeni s roditeljima kojima treba reći rezultate vaših analiza?

Anđelinović: Sigurno. Predaja tijela rodbini išla je po protokolu u kojem bismo im prvo čitali klasične antropološke nalaze koji govore o njima, nakon toga ih upoznali s rezultatima DNA koji su pokazali da se DNA iz njihove krvi i DNA iz kosti pokojnika podudaraju i da se s visokom vjerojatnosti radi o njihovu sinu. U tome trenu bi zastali, vidite majku koja se suzdržava od plača. Netko od njih nešto pita, imaju neka dodatna pitanja... i nakon toga im izrazimo sućut. U tome trenu prilaze mi, najčešće majke, zagrle me i na ramenu zaplaču. U tome plaču je sve – priznanje da se to dogodilo, pomirenje i bol koja nije prošla. Ta majka, rodbina, prošli su kroz period u kojem su dolazili lažni svjedoci koji su joj govorili da su im vidjeli sina živa, ili da on nije bio s njima u ratnoj akciji, da imaju spoznaje da je živ, ili su dolazili s nekim fotografijama na kojima ipak nije bio on... Rodbina je s tako s vremenom doživjela puno frustracija. Tako, kad bi sjeli s nama s druge strane stola, nisu znali jesam li ja još jedan od tih. To im se vidi u očima, oni još ne vjeruju. Tek u tih 10, 15 minuta sve se promijeni i majka, roditelji, shvate „to je to“. Povjerenje u nas i znanost pomoglo je da majka dobije ono što je tražila - mir. Spoznaju da je ON mrtav i da nema više traženja. Da može doći na njegov grob. Neke oprečne emocije, imaš najtužniju vijest da si ga stvarno izgubio i najdražu da si ga našao. Taj težak spoj emocija ona mora na nekoga prenijeti, i to sam u tom trenu bio ja. Jedini i najbliži njoj, a nikad se prije nismo vidjeli! U tom trenu znanstvena titula više nije bitna, važna je ljudska duša. To je trenutak

Povratak dostojanstva: identifikacija nestalih u Domovinskome ratu /

Bringing Back Dignity: Identification of the Missing in the Homeland War

and that, with a very high degree of probability, it was their son. At that moment, we would pause. You could see the mother holding back her tears. Someone would ask a question, there were always a few more things they needed to understand… and then we would express our condolences. That’s when they would come up to me, most often the mothers, embrace me, and break down on my shoulder. In that moment of grief, everything was there. The acceptance that it had happened, a kind of reconciliation, and a pain that never really goes away. These families had already gone through so much. False witnesses had come to them, telling them they had seen their son alive, or that he had not been there during the fighting, that they had information he was still alive. Some even brought photographs, but it was never him… Over time, the families carried a lot of frustration. So, when they sat across from us, they didn’t know whether I was just another one of them. You could see it in their eyes; they still didn’t believe. And then, within ten or fifteen minutes, everything would change. The mother, the parents, would realize… this is it. Trust in us and in science meant that a mother could finally find what she had been searching for - peace. The certainty that he is gone, that there is no more searching. That she can visit his grave. It’s a strange mix of emotions. You receive the worst news: that you have truly lost him, and at the same time, the most important one, that you have finally found him. That heavy combination has to go somewhere, and in that moment, it came to me. I was the only one there, the closest person to her, even though we had never met before! In that moment, academic titles no longer matter. What matters is the human side. That is when dignity is restored. It is no longer an un-

PDF page 31

kada se vraća dignitet te osobe. To više nije mrtva, nepoznata osoba, to je osoba s imenom i prezimenom, a to je cilj svake obitelji.

Leskur Staničić: Koliko je ljudi, nesretnih članova obitelji, prošlo kroz obdukcijsku salu u tom procesu identifikacije?

Anđelinović: Više od 500 ljudi, ni ne znam koliko sam zagrljaja primio.

Leskur Staničić: Kako je na Vas djelovalo to iskustvo?

Anđelinović: Za mene nije bilo upitno treba li to sve napraviti, treba li upotrijebiti najbolju opremu, nisam imao nikakvu dvojbu. A to je bilo tada pitanje struke – hoćete li koristiti skupu metodu pri identifikacijama, treba li u masovnim grobnicama raditi DNA, to su stručna pitanja. Toliki je broj mrtvih da je bilo pitanje kad sve to napraviti, gdje s tijelima, radi li se to uopće? Mi bismo na nekim kostima radili izolaciju deset puta i tek deseti put bismo dobili profil. A smatralo se da je i pet puta dovoljno. Gledao sam te nesretne žene pred sobom, nije mi bilo važno koliko to košta. To je neki pokretač jači od tebe, radiš više nego što možeš. Ne mogu objasniti, to je ta duhovna povezanost ljudi. Odnos koji smo imali dr. Primorac, dr. Lee i ja bio je izuzetan, radili smo kao tim. Naš profesor, dr. Bogdan Krstulović, koji je došao na ispomoć iz Zagreba u Split, u jednoj je prigodi nazvao Primorca i mene „dvojcem bez kormilara“. Bio je u pravu, ali dodao bih da on nije vidio kormilara. Kormilar je bio Henry Lee koji je naš prijatelj i otac u smislu moderne forenzike. Pionir koji je prvi radio DNA analize iz kosti. Naišli smo na čovjeka koji je senzibilan za ljudske probleme, spreman ići s nama u nove izazove i – biti prijatelj. Izraziti prijatelj Hrvatske; njegova knjiga ‘Pucanj u Hrvatskoj’ jedna je od rijetkih

known, lifeless body. It is a person with a name and a surname. And that is what every family is searching for.

Leskur Staničić: How many people, how many grieving family members, passed through the autopsy room during the identification process?

Anđelinović: More than 500 people. I don’t even know how many people I embraced.

Leskur Staničić: How did that experience affect you?

Anđelinović: For me, there was never any question about whether this should be done or whether we should use the best available methods. I had no doubts at all. At the time, though, these were real professional questions. Should you use such an expensive method for identification? Should DNA even be used in mass graves? There were so many bodies that the question was also practical. When to get all that done? Where do you keep the remains? Is it even feasible at all? We would sometimes try to isolate DNA from the same bone ten times, and only on the tenth attempt would we finally get a usable profile. At the time, it was considered that even five attempts were more than enough. I was looking at those grieving women in front of me, and I didn’t care what it cost. It was something that drove you - stronger than yourself; you did more than you thought possible. I can’t really explain it; it was a kind of spiritual connection between people. The relationship between Primorac, Dr Lee, and me was exceptional; we worked as a true team. Our professor, Dr Bogdan Krstulović, who came from Zagreb to help in Split, once called Primorac and me “the rudderless duo.” He was right. But I would add that he simply didn’t see the helmsman. The helmsman was Dr Henry C. Lee, our friend and, in many

PDF page 32

knjiga koja opisuje ratna zbivanja kod nas iz pozicije stranca znanstvenika i pokazuje njegovo razumijevanje što se u Hrvatskoj događalo. I upravo smo zahvaljujući njegovoj predanosti uspjeli postići sve to. Tu su još i Mitchell M. Holland, Michael M. Baden, Barbara Wolf, Moses Schanfield, svi koji su nam se pridružili. Njihovo iskustvo i naš entuzijazam, spremnost da to usvojimo, bili su ključni. Bez njihove pomoći ne bismo mogli, a bez našeg izazova, oni ne bi ni znali da sve to postoji. Uz nas su bili i dr. Matko i Ana Marušić, u znanstvenom smislu, Ivan Damjanov u patologiji, svi su oni dio tog znanstvenog tima. Tu su bili brojni inženjeri i tehničari, uprava bolnice. Sam čovjek ne može ništa.

Leskur Staničić: Nakon što je objavljen članak dr. Primorca i suradnika, cijeli znanstveni svijet saznao je o doprinosu splitskih forenzičara. Kako ste to doživjeli?

Anđelinović: Postavili smo jedan novi standard, model koji smo inaugurirali u Vukovaru i u svim drugim hrvatskim masovnim grobnicama. Poslije se pokazao dobrim i u Srebrenici, kao i u svijetu. Bio je to pionirski rad, odgovor na potrebe rodbine poginulih. S vremenom smo napravili tri ovlaštena laboratorija u Hrvatskoj, u Osijeku, Zagrebu i Splitu koja imaju potencijal odgovoriti tim zahtjevima. Nismo uspjeli identificirati samo one čije kosti nisu mogle dati DNA ni nakon deset pokušaja. Ti ljudi su proglašeni nestalim ili su pokopani bez imena. Svi oni koje smo uspjeli identificirati dobili su svoj novi dignitet. Pokopani su dostojno. Iako su nas profesori Matko i Ana Marušić poticali da u tim ratnim situacijama više pišemo, uz sve obaveze to je bilo teško. Zato danas ima tako malo znanstvenih radova o tome. A ono što nije zapisano, to ne postoji. Da to nismo objavili, ta istina

Povratak dostojanstva: identifikacija nestalih u Domovinskome ratu /

Bringing Back Dignity: Identification of the Missing in the Homeland War

ways, a father figure of modern forensics. We met someone who was deeply sensitive to human suffering, willing to step into new challenges with us, and above all, a friend. A true friend of Croatia. His book “Gunshot in Croatia” is one of the rare accounts that describes the war here from the perspective of an outsider, a scientist, and shows how well he understood what was happening in this country. And it was largely through his commitment that we were able to achieve all of this. There were also Drs Mitchell Holland, Michael M. Baden, Barbara Wolf, Moses Schanfield, and all those who joined us. Their experience, our enthusiasm, and our readiness to learn and adopt it were the key. Without their help, we could not have done it. And without our challenge, they might never have known that something like this even existed. On our side, we also had Dr Matko Marušić and Dr Ana Marušić on the scientific side, Ivan Damjanov in pathology; they were all part of that scientific team. There were also many of our engineers and technicians, as well as the hospital administration. No one could have done this alone.

Leskur Staničić: After Dr Primorac et al. published the paper and the wider scientific community became aware of the contribution of the Split forensic team, how did you experience that?

Anđelinović: We set a new standard, a model we first introduced in Vukovar and later applied to mass graves across Croatia. It proved its value in Srebrenica as well, and eventually around the world. It was pioneering work, a response to the needs of the families of those who had been killed. Later, we established three accredited laboratories in Croatia, in Osijek, Zagreb, and Split, capable of meeting those demands.

PDF page 33

se nikad ne bi doznala. Da dr. Barbara Wolf nije bila ovdje, nitko u Americi ne bi znao istinu. To je ključ. Veličina svega toga nam je promicala među prstima u tom svakodnevnom poslu. Kad je stiglo 37 ubijenih iz Uzdola, u obdukcijsku salu ušli smo u 9 sati ujutro i radili do deset i pol navečer. Na Haškom sudu me kao svjedoka pitao jedan odvjetnik: „Pa kako ste mogli tih 37 zapisnika tako detaljno opisati?“, a ja mu na to kažem: „I ja se pitam!”. Trebalo je odgovoriti na zahtjeve vremena. Bili smo tada jedina dostupna patologija na ovom području, sve je drugo bilo blokirano. U tom nepredvidivom poslu, u tom vremenu, nastojali smo napraviti sve da postignemo najviši znanstveni standard u zadanim uvjetima. Odradili smo puno toga čega smo tek kasnije postali svjesni. I za Muslimane i za Hrvate, a kasnije i za Srbe, sve je zapisano, ne može se izbrisati. To je ključno, žrtvama dati dignitet, istini prostor, a svima nama zadovoljstvo da smo u tome sudjelovali.

In the end, the only ones we could not identify were those whose bones did not yield DNA, even after ten attempts. Those people were declared missing or buried without a name. Everyone we were able to identify regained their dignity. They were laid to rest with dignity. Although Professors Matko and Ana Marušić encouraged us to write more during those wartime years, with everything we were dealing with, it was simply very difficult. That is why so little has been published scientifically on this topic. And what is not published does not exist. If we had not did it, the truth would never have come out. If Dr Barbara Wolf had not been here, no one in the United States would have known what was really happening. That was crucial. The magnitude of it all was slipping through our fingers amid the daily work. When 37 victims from Uzdol arrived, we entered the autopsy room at nine in the morning and worked until half past ten at night. At the Hague, one of the lawyers asked me, as a witness, “How were you able to document all 37 cases in such detail?” And I told him, “I ask myself the same question!” We had to respond to the demands of the time. Back then, we were the only functioning pathology service available in this region; everything else was blocked. In that unpredictable work, in those circumstances, we did everything we could to meet the highest possible scientific standards. We carried out things we only fully understood later. For Muslims, for Croats, and later for Serbs as well, everything was documented; it cannot be erased. And that is what matters most: to restore dignity to the victims, to give space to the truth, and for all of us, a sense of fulfillment in having been part of it.

Identification of War Victims from Mass Graves in Croatia, Bosnia, and Herzegovina by the Use of Standard Forensic Methods and DNA Typing

Dragan Primorac1; Šimun Anđelinović2; Marija Definis-Gojanović2; Irena Drmić2; Boja Režić2; Michael M. Baden3; Mitchell A. Kennedy4; Moses S. Schanfield5; Stephen B. Skakel6; and Henry C. Lee7.

1) DNA Laboratory at the Department of Pathology and Forensic Medicine, Zagreb University School of Medicine, Split Branch, Clinical Hospital Split, Croatia; presently, instructor, University of Connecticut Health Center, Department of Pediatrics, Farmington, CT. 2) Associate professor, assistant professor, research assistant, and technician, respectively, Department of Pathology and Forensic Medicine, Clinical Hospital Split, Split, Croatia. 3) Director, Forensic Science Unit, New York State Police, Albany, NY. 4) Technical support manager, Affymetrix, Santa Clara, CA. 5) Laboratory director, Analytical Genetic Testing Center, Denver, CO. 6) Project director, AmeriCares Foundation, New Canaan, CT. 7) Director, Forensic Science Laboratory, Connecticut State Police, Meriden, CT. SAŽETAK: Posmrtni ostatci šezdeset i jedne žrtve rata ekshumirani su iz šest masovnih grobnica u Bosni i Hercegovini, godinu i pol nakon ukopa. Standardnim metodama identifikacije, uključujući usporedbu medicinske i stomatološke dokumentacije, identifikaciju na temelju razlikovnih obilježja poput odjeće i osobnih predmeta, te fotografskom superimpozicijom, identificirano je 35 osoba. Nastavlja se predan rad na identifikaciji preostalih 26 osoba. Tipizacija DNK-a provedena je na lokusu HLA DQA1 i pet lokusa PM sustava. Rezultati tipizacije DNK-a potvrđeni su drugim metodama. Profili DNK-a članova obitelji 150 nestalih osoba sada se izrađuju uporabom navedenih šest lokusa. Ti profili DNK-a zatim će se usporediti s DNK profilima iz koštanih i zubnih ostataka neidentificiranih žrtava.

KLJUČNE RIJEČI: forenzičke znanosti, patologija i biologija, identifikacija osoba, DNK, genska tipizacija, rat, masovne grobnice, PCR

Rukopis je predan 12. lipnja 1995.; revidirani rukopis zaprimljen 1. prosinca 1995. i 19. siječnja 1996.; prihvaćen za objavu 22. siječnja 1996.

U ovom se izvješću opisuje naš rad na identifikaciji posmrtnih ostataka 61 osobe primjenom različitih metoda. Tijekom 1993. i 1994. godine, posmrtni ostatci 61 osobe koje su poginule na kupreškome bojištu u Bosni i Hercegovini, prevezeni su na Zavod za patologiju i sudsku medicinu Kliničke bolnice u Splitu. Ekshumirani godinu i pol nakon smrti, posmrtni ostatci iz šest zajedničkih grobnica bili su u uznapredovalome stadiju raspadanja. Istodobno su prikupljeni i dokumentirani podatci o nestalim osobama s toga područja (ukupno 150), koje su ili nestale ili su ubijene tijekom travanjske agresije srpskih snaga 1992. godine. U svim slučajevima forenzički stručnjaci proveli su detaljan pregled odjeće i osobnih predmeta pokojnika, opisali posebna obilježja, analizirali

ABSTRACT: The postmortem remains of sixty-one war victims were excavated from 6 mass graves in Bosnia and Herzegovina one and a half years after interment. Using standard identification methods, including the matching of medical and dental records, the recognition of distinguishing characteristics such as the use of clothing and belongings, and video superimposition, 35 persons were identified. For the remaining 26 persons identification efforts continue. DNA typing was performed at the HLA DQA1 locus and five PM system loci. Results from DNA typing were confirmed by other methods. DNA profiles of family members of 150 missing persons are now being developed using the 6 loci. These DNA profiles will then be compared with those generated from the bone and teeth remains of the unidentified victims.

KEYWORDS: forensic science, pathology and biology, human identification, DNA, genetic typing, war, mass graves, PCR

Received for publication 12 June 1995; revised manuscript received 1 Dec. 1995 and 19 Jan. 1996; accepted for publication 22 Jan. 1996.

This report describes our work on the identification of the postmortem remains of 61 persons using a variety of applicable methods. During 1993 through 1994, the postmortem remains of 61 persons who died on the Kupres battlefield of Bosnia and Herzegovina (1) were transported to the Department of Pathology and Forensic Medicine, Clinical Hospital in Split, Croatia. Excavated one and half years after death, the remains from six common graves were in an advanced stage of decomposition. At the same time, data on missing persons from that region (totaling 150), who had either disappeared or had been killed during the April 1992 Serbian aggression were collected and registered. In all cases, forensic examiners performed a detailed examination of the clothing and belongings of the dead, described special features,

Identifikacija žrtava rata iz masovnih grobnica u Hrvatskoj, Bosni i Hercegovini standardnim forenzičkim metodama i tipizacijom DNK-a / Identification of War Victims from Mass Graves in Croatia, Bosnia, and Herzegovina by the Use of Standard Forensic Methods and DNA Typing

koštane ostatke radi procjene spola i tjelesne visine te usporedili zaživotnu stomatološku dokumentaciju s posmrtnim stomatološkim nalazima. Uz to, provedene su i rendgenske usporedbe morfologije kostiju. Identifikacija više osoba obavljena je superimpozicijom snimaka lubanja i fotografija. Prikupljeni zaživotni podatci uspoređeni su s nalazima obdukcije.

Zbog uznapredovaloga stupnja razgradnje mnogih tijela, razmatrane su mogućnosti djelomične identifikacije žrtava rata tipizacijom DNK-a iz kostiju i zuba (2). Lee i sur. (3) navode da se iz spužvaste kosti može dobiti do 10 μg DNK-a, a iz kompaktne kosti do 0,5 μg DNK-a po miligramu početnoga tkiva. Najbolji način za dobivanje genetičkih informacija iz uzoraka s degradiranim DNK-om test je utemeljen na PCR-u. Stoga je analiziran lokus HLA DQA1 i pet lokusa sustava AmpliType PM.

MATERIJAL I METODE

Izvori DNK-a Tijekom obdukcija prikupljeni su uzorci dugih kostiju i zuba za analizu DNK-a. Uzorci kostiju i zuba uzeti su od sljedećih osoba: osoba br. 4, bedrena kost; osoba br. 14, goljenična kost i zub; i osoba br. 44, bedrena kost. Količina kosti prikupljene za analizu DNK-a iznosila je od 5 do 20 g. Zubi za ekstrakciju DNK-a pripremljeni su prema prije opisanome postupku (4). Iz uzoraka kostiju dobiveno je prosječno 100 ng ljudskoga DNK-a po gramu usitnjene kosti. Uzorci kostiju i zuba pohranjeni su na −20°C. Istodobno su za tipizaciju DNK-a prikupljeni uzorci krvi (po 5 ml) i uzorci kose od članova svake obitelji.

Ekstrakcija DNK-a Nakon čišćenja kontaminiranih površina uzoraka kostiju i zuba, uzorci su usitnjeni u fini prah. Otprilike 1,0 g koštanoga praha dodano je u 450 μL pufera za ekstrakciju (10 mM tris-klo-

analyzed skeletal remains to estimate sex and height, and compared premortem dental records with postmortem dental records. In addition, X-ray comparisons were performed for bone morphology. Identification of several persons by the superimposition of skull and photographic images was performed. The compiled, premortem data were compared with the autopsy findings.

Because of the advanced decomposition of many bodies, we considered identifying war victims partly by DNA typing from bones and teeth (2). It was reported by Lee et al. (3) that the recovery of DNA can be up to 10 μg from spongy bone or up to 0.5 μg from compact bone per milligram of starting tissue. The best way to obtain genetic information from samples with degraded DNA is a PCR-based test. We therefore analyzed the HLA DQA1 locus and the five loci of the AmpliType PM typing kit.

MATERIAL AND METHODS

DNA Sources Samples of long bones and teeth were collected for DNA analysis at the time of the autopsies. Bone and teeth specimens were collected from the following individuals: No. 4, femur; No. 14, tibia and tooth; and No. 44, femur. The amount of bone collected for DNA analysis varied from 5 to 20 g. Teeth for DNA extraction were prepared as described (4). From the bone samples, an average of 100 ng of human DNA per gram of powdered bone were recovered. The bone and teeth samples were stored at −20°C. At the same time, blood samples of 5 mL each and hair samples were collected for DNA typing from members of each family.

DNA Extraction After cleaning the contaminated surfaces of the bone and tooth samples, the specimens were pulverized into a fine powder. Approximately 1.0 g

PDF page 39

rovodične kiseline [HCl], pH 7,5; 1 mM etilendiamintetraoctene kiseline [EDTA], pH 8; 100 mM natrijeva klorida [NaCl] i 2%-tni natrijev dodecil-sulfat [SDS]) u prisutnosti proteinaze K (200 μg/ml) (Gibco BRL). Ova je otopina inkubirana na 56°C tijekom 18 sati, a DNK je izoliran organskom ekstrakcijom (3,4).

Kvantifikacija DNK-a Količina ljudskoga DNK-a određena je slot blot analizom s pomoću kompleta za kvantifikaciju ljudskoga DNK-a QuantiBlot™ (Roche Molecular Systems, Inc., Branchburg, NJ).

Amplifikacija i tipizacija DNK-a Amplifikacija PCR-om provedena je na uređaju Perkin-Elmer GeneAmp PCR System 9600 s pomoću originalnih kompleta za amplifikaciju i tipizaciju Amplitype HLA DQA1 PCR te originalnog kompleta za amplifikaciju i tipizaciju AmpliType PM PCR. Hibridizacija i detekcija amplificiranih lokusa HLA-DQA1 i PM provedene su prema uputama proizvođača.

Analiza rezultata tipizacije Profili DNK-a dobiveni iz kostiju i zuba analizirani su i uspoređeni s profilima DNK-a živih srodnika. Relativna učestalost podudaranja u populaciji izračunata je prema metodama koje su opisali Schanfield i Stern (5). Na temelju tih vrijednosti izračunani su omjer vjerojatnosti u korist roditeljstvu ili srodstvu te Bayesova vjerojatnost roditeljstva ili srodstva, s pomoću formule P = 1 / (1 + relativna učestalost u populaciji), uz pretpostavku da prethodna vjerojatnost iznosi 0,5. Upotrijebljene su učestalosti alela dobivene iz združenoga uzorka hrvatske populacije.

of bone powder was added to 450 μL of extraction buffer (10 mM Tris-hydrochloric acid [HCl] pH 7.5, 1 mM ethylenediaminetetraacetate [EDTA] pH 8, 100 mM sodium chloride [NaCl], and 2% sodium dodecyl sulfate [SDS]) in the presence of proteinase K (200 μg/mL) (Gibco BRL). This solution was incubated at 56°C for 18 h, and DNA was isolated by organic extraction (3,4).

DNA Quantification The quantity of human DNA was determined by slot blot analysis using the QuantiBlot™ Human DNA Quantitation Kit (Roche Molecular Systems, Inc., Branchburg, NJ).

DNA Amplification and Typing PCR amplification was performed on the Perkin-Elmer GeneAmp PCR System 9600, using the original Amplitype HLA DQA1 PCR Amplification and Typing Kit, as well as the original AmpliType PM PCR Amplification and Typing Kit. Hybridization and detection of amplified HLA DQA1 and PM DNA were performed according to the manufacturer’s instructions.

Analysis of Typing Results DNA types from bone and teeth were analyzed and compared to the DNA types from living relatives. The relative population frequency of the matches were calculated using the methods described by Schanfield and Stern (5). From these values, the likelihood in favor of parentage, or relatedness, and Bayesian probability of parentage or relatedness as calculated using the formula P = 1 / 1 + relative population frequency, assuming a prior probability of 0.5. Allele frequencies used were a pooled sample of Croatians.

REZULTATI

Na temelju podataka dobivenih tijekom razmjene posmrtnih ostataka i iskaza očevidaca utvrđeno je da ostatci 61 osobe potječu iz šest masovnih grobnica. Svi posmrtni ostatci bili su u uznapredovalome stadiju raspadanja, dok su neki bili posve skeletizirani. Pedeset i osam pokojnika imalo je potpunu ili djelomičnu vojnu odoru, a dvoje je bilo odjeveno u civilnu odjeću. U jednome je slučaju pronađena samo odjeća, bez ijednoga dijela tijela. Sedam tijela bilo je djelomično ili potpuno saponificirano, a jedno je tijelo bilo karbonizirano. Budući da su vojne odore bile identične, nisu pružile razlikovna obilježja. Utvrđeno je da su svi posmrtni ostatci pripadali muškarcima. Trideset i pet osoba uspješno je identificirano

RESULTS

From the information given during the exchange of remains and from eyewitnesses, it was determined that the 61 of the remains came from 6 mass graves. All remains were in an advanced state of decomposition; some were only skeletal. Fifty-eight had full or partial military uniforms, and two were clothed as civilians. In one case, only the clothes, without any part of the body, were recovered. Seven bodies were partially or completely saponified, and one body was carbonized. Since military uniforms are identical, they provided no distinguishing clues. It was established that all remains were male. Thirty-five persons were successfully identified using combined techniques of identification. In every case a

kombiniranim tehnikama identifikacije. U svakome slučaju pozitivna identifikacija temeljila se na podatcima dobivenima iz dviju ili više tehnika. Međutim, detalji na odjeći, kao i civilno rublje i osobni predmeti, pridonijeli su identifikaciji u 31 slučaju. U 32 slučaja tjelesna visina procijenjena je na temelju duljine dugih kostiju i drugih koštanih ostataka. Zbog nedostatka dugih kostiju visina nije mogla biti utvrđena u gotovo pola slučajeva. Stomatološka dokumentacija potvrdila je identitet 15 osoba. Individualna obilježja, poput starih ožiljaka i prijeloma, bila su korisna u sedam slučajeva. Uz daktiloskopske i stomatološke analize, radiološki je pregled kostiju također bio koristan u određenim slučajevima (6).

U ovome je radu identitet četiriju osoba potvrđen rendgenskom usporedbom oblika i veličine kostiju te znakovima starih prijeloma. Samo je 19 osoba identificirano na temelju stomatološke i medicinske dokumentacije. Dva su glavna razloga za to: (1) nedostatak odgovarajuće zaživotne dokumentacije i (2) veliki broj izrazito oštećenih lubanja, bez gornje ili donje čeljusti, ili bez ijedne čeljusti. U pet slučajeva utvrđivanje identiteta provedeno je (kompozitnom) fotografskom superimpozicijom. Fotografska superimpozicija izvedena je usporedbom lubanja žrtava s njihovim fotografijama (7,8). Pri identifikaciji lubanje, uzimajući u obzir debljinu mekih tkiva te s pomoću suvremenih fotografskih ili elektroničkih postupaka, identifikacija je moguća na temelju brojnih podudarnih referentnih točaka u usporedivom materijalu (9). Ostale metode identifikacije uključivale su sedam slučajeva u kojima su pomogli podatci o okolnostima smrti (vrsta i lokalizacija ozljede, iskazi očevidaca i mjesto ukopa) te osam slučajeva u kojima su boja i duljina kose bile korisne. Vrsta ozljede pomogla je razjasniti okolnosti smrti u određenim slučajevima. Na pet lubanja u ovoj skupini utvrđene su strijelne rane. DNK je uspješno amplificiran i tipiziran na lokusima HLA DQA1 i PM.

positive identification was based on the data from two or more techniques. However, details on the clothes, as well as civilian underwear and personal belongings, aided in the identification of 31 cases. In 32 cases, the statures were estimated from the length of long bones and other bone remains. Because of the lack of long bones, height could not be established in almost half the cases. Dental records confirmed the identity of 15 persons. Special marks (such as old scars and old fractures) were useful in seven cases. In addition to fingerprints and dental examinations, in some circumstances, radiographic examination of the bone was also helpful in identification of the remains (6).

In our work, the identities of four individuals were confirmed by X-ray comparison of bone shape, size, or indications of old fractures. Only 19 persons were identified by dental and medical records. The two main reasons for that were: (1) the lack of adequate antemortem records and (2) a great number of extremely damaged skulls, without upper or lower jaws, or both. Identity determination was established by means of the image superimposition technique in five cases. Video superimposition was done comparing skulls of victims and their photographs (7,8). In skull identification, by taking soft tissue thickness into consideration and with the help of modern photographic or electronic super projection processes, identification is possible as a result of numerous reference point matches in comparable material (9). Other methods of identification included seven cases in which we were aided by data indicating the circumstances of death (type and location of wound, eyewitness accounts, and place of burial) and eight cases in which hair color and length have been useful. Wound types helped clarify conditions of death in some cases. Gunshot wounds were found on five skulls in this category.

The DNA was successfully amplified and typed at the HLA DQA1 and PM loci. Figure 1 shows

Na slici 1 prikazani su rezultati podudaranja profila DNK-a živih srodnika i triju nestalih osoba. Nakon primjene ove metode, identitet triju osoba potvrđen je dodatnom usporedbom zaživotnih podataka s posmrtnim nalazima.

RASPRAVA

U nedostatku zaživotnih podataka za usporedbu, identifikacija ljudskih posmrtnih ostataka izuzetno je otežana (10). Zbog raspadanja tijela i opsežnih posmrtnih promjena, čak ni uzrok smrti često nije bio očit. Budući da su tijela bila nepažljivo ukopana i ekshumirana, identifikacija je bila dodatno otežana. Okolnosti ukopa i ekshumacije varirale su. Tipizacija DNK-a omogućila je dobivanje podataka iz ljudskih koštanih ostataka što je bila dodatna metoda za uspješnu identifikaciju.

Mnogi autori ističu poteškoće forenzičke analize DNK-a iz koštanih uzoraka (10,11). Velik broj uzoraka sadržavao je nedovoljnu količinu DNK-a ili je DNK bio degradiran ili kontaminiran. Budući da primjena polimorfizama dužine restrikcijskih fragmenata (RFLP) nije moguća u slučajevima izrazite degradacije DNK-a (12), PCR omogućuje amplifikaciju relativno kratke ciljane sekvencije DNK-a i naknadnu tipizaciju DNK-a iz kostiju izloženih ekstremnim utjecajima okoliša (13). U ovome su radu rezultati tipizacije DNK-a bili u skladu s profilima potomaka poznatih roditelja ili srodnika. Koristeći učestalosti alela koje su nedavno dobivene u hrvatskoj populaciji na lokusima LDLR, GYPA, HBGG, D7S8, Gc i HLA-DQA1, izračunata vjerojatnost roditeljstva u sljedećim slučajevima iznosila je: 99,617 % za slučaj 4, 99,446 % za slučaj 14 te 98,393 % za slučaj 44.

Za obradu koštanih uzoraka korištene su metode ekstrakcije fenolom i Chelexom. U ovome radu metoda ekstrakcije fenolom pokazala se učinkovitijom. Rezultati nisu dobiveni kad je DNK

the DNA typing results of matches between living relatives and three missing persons. Using this method, three identities were consistent in conjunction with additional antemortem and postmortem data.

DISCUSSION

In the absence of antemortem data for comparison, identification of human remains is exceedingly difficult (10). Because of advanced decomposition and extensive postmortem changes, even the cause of death was often not apparent. Since the bodies were buried and excavated carelessly, identification was further impaired. The circumstances of burying and excavation varied. DNA typing offered the possibility of obtaining information from human skeletal remains as an additional method for successful identification.

Many authors emphasize the difficulties in forensic DNA analysis from bone specimens (10,11). A large number of specimens contained insufficient quantities of DNA, or the DNA from these specimens was degraded or contaminated. Since the use of restriction length fragment polymorphisms (RFLPs) is not applicable in cases involving extensive degradation of DNA (12), PCR permits the amplification of a relatively small DNA target sequence and subsequent DNA typing from bone exposed to extreme environmental conditions (13). In our work, results of DNA typing are consistent with those of the offspring of known parents or relatives. Using allele frequencies recently obtained from the Croatian population at loci LDLR, GYPA, HBGG, D7S8, Gc, and HLA-DQA1,8 we calculated the probability of parenthood in the following cases: 99.617% for Case 4, 99.446% for Case 14, and a probability of relatedness of 98.393% for Case 44.

PDF page 43

iz kostiju ekstrahiran s pomoću smole Chelex (8). Međutim, ekstrakcija uzoraka krvi s pomoću Chelexa bila je korisna. Također je utvrđeno da korak dekalcifikacije nije nuždan za dobivanje dovoljne količine DNK-a iz koštanih ostataka, što je u skladu s nalazima Fishera i suradnika (12). Uz to, utvrđeno da je Centricon® mikropurifikacija korisna za pročišćavanje ekstrakta, osobito u slučajevima kada je nuklearni DNK bio izrazito degradiran.

Trenutno smo jedini forenzički laboratorij za analizu DNK-a na području gdje je broj žrtava rata iz Hrvatske i Bosne i Hercegovine između 1991. i 1992. godine premašio 150.000 (14). Laboratorij je zaslužan za točnu identifikaciju mnogih od tih žrtava. Trenutačno testiramo nove metode, poput tipizacije mitohondrijskoga DNK-a radi identifikacije ljudskih ostataka (15).

Važnost identifikacije žrtava rata obuhvaća humanitarne, medicinske i pravne aspekte. Posmrtno utvrđivanje identiteta može biti znatno otežano. Tipizacija DNK-a stoga može biti korisna metoda u slučajevima kad forenzički patolozi nemaju dovoljno podataka za identifikaciju drugim sredstvima.

Zahvale Zahvaljujemo dr. Mitchellu M. Hollandu, dr. Bruceu Budowleu, dr. Barbari C. Wolf i dr. Olgi J. Schamraj na korisnim prijedlozima. Također zahvaljujemo gđi Mary Louise Stover, Galeu B. Gardineru te dr. Davidu W. Roweu, dr. Alexanderu C. Lichtleru i dr. Marku S. Kronenbergu na pomoći u pripremi rukopisa. Isto tako, zahvaljujemo Perkin-Elmeru i AmeriCaresu na sponzoriranju ovoga projekta.

Both phenol and Chelex extraction methods have been used for the processing of bone samples. In this work, the phenol extraction method was found to be more effective. No results were obtained when DNA was extracted from bone in the presence of Chelex resin (8). In contrast, Chelex extraction of blood samples was useful. We also found that the decalcification step was not required for sufficient DNA yield from skeletal remains confirming the findings of Fisher et al. (12). In addition, we found Centricon® micropurification to be useful for purifying the extract, particularly in cases in which nuclear DNA was highly degraded.

Currently, we are the only DNA forensic laboratory in the area in which war victims from Croatia and Bosnia and Herzegovina totaled more than 150,000 between 1991 and 1992 (14). The laboratory has the responsibility for accurate identification for many of these victims. We are in the process of testing new methods such as mitochondrial DNA typing for identification of human remains (15).

The importance of identifying victims from a war situation involves humanitarian as well as medical and legal concerns. Establishing postmortem identity can be very difficult. DNA typing, therefore, represents a potentially useful method in cases in which forensic pathologists have too little data for identification by other means.

Croatian contributions to the first systematic DNA-based large-scale identification of mass graves war victims

SAŽETAK

Početkom 1990-ih započinje opsežna DNK identifikacija ratnih žrtava, kao odgovor na dotad nezabilježene forenzičke izazove povezane s masovnim grobnicama i izrazito degradiranim ljudskim ostacima, otkrivenima tijekom i nakon ratova u Hrvatskoj i Bosni i Hercegovini. Ovaj rad iz povijesne perspektive preispituje početke i dugoročni utjecaj prvih sustavnih primjena metoda analize DNK-a u identifikaciji žrtava iz masovnih grobnica nastalih u ratnome kontekstu. Te su aktivnosti započele između 1993. i 1994. godine na današnjemu Kliničkom zavodu za patologiju, sudsku medicinu i citologiju Kliničkoga bolničkog centra Split. Integracijom klasičnih forenzičkih metoda s ranim metodama tipiziranja DNK-a utemeljenih na PCR-u te uspostavom referentne baze podataka srodnika, hrvatski su znanstvenici, u suradnji s vodećim američkim stručnjacima, razvili metodološki okvir koji je omogućio razrješavanje složenih slučajeva identifikacije žrtava iz masovnih grobnica u ratnim uvjetima. Njihov je rad prepoznat i na razini Kongresa Sjedinjenih Američkih Država 1995. godine, čime je dana rijetka institucijska potvrda forenzičkomu programu koji nije nastao u vodećim zapadnim znanstvenim središtima. Tijekom narednih triju desetljeća pristupi razvijeni u tome kontekstu prerasli su u standardiziranu forenzičku praksu koja obuhvaća analizu STR-ova, mitohondrijskoga DNK-a i forenzičku genomiku. Njihova se primjena proširila daleko izvan regionalnoga konteksta te je pridonijela oblikovanju globalnih pristupa identifikaciji žrtava katastrofa i poslijeratnoj obnovi. Hrvatsko iskustvo tako označuje važnu prekretnicu u prijelazu od primjene analize DNK-a u pojedinačnim slučajevima do njezine sustavne i široke primjene u identifikaciji osoba, s trajnim znanstvenim, institucijskim i humanitarnim učincima.

Ključne riječi: DNK identifikacija; žrtve rata; masovne grobnice; forenzička genetika; Hrvatska; međunarodna suradnja; identifikacija žrtava katastrofa

ABSTRACT

The large-scale DNA identification of war victims emerged in the early 1990s as a response to unprecedented forensic challenges posed by mass graves and highly degraded human remains during and after the wars in Croatia and Bosnia and Herzegovina. This historical perspective revisits the origins and long-term impact of the first systematic applications of DNA-based technology during the massive identification of mass grave victims during wartime, which started between 1993 and 1994 at the Clinical Department of Pathology, Forensic Medicine and Cytology, University Hospital of Split. Through the integration of classical forensic methods with early PCR-based DNA typing and the establishment of a kinship reference database, Croatian scientists, in collaboration with leading American experts, developed a methodological framework capable of resolving complex mass-fatality cases under wartime conditions. This work was formally recognized in 1995 before the United States Congress, providing rare institutional validation of a forensic program operating outside established Western infrastructures. Over the following three decades, the approaches developed in this context evolved into a standardized forensic practice that incorporates STR analysis, mitochondrial DNA, and forensic genomics. Their application has extended far beyond the regional context and has contributed to the global approach to disaster victim identification and post-conflict recovery. The Croatian experience thus represents an important moment in the transition of DNA analysis from isolated forensic use to a systematic, large-scale tool for human identification, with continuous scientific, institutional, and humanitarian implications.

Keywords: DNA identification; war victims; mass graves; forensic genetics; Croatia; international collaboration; disaster victim identification

Hrvatski doprinos razvoju prve sustavne identifikacije žrtava iz masovnih grobnica analizom DNK-a / Croatian contributions to the first systematic DNA-based large-scale identification of mass graves war victims

UVOD

Identifikacija nestalih osoba ključan je korak ne samo za osobna razrješenja obiteljima, nego i za ostvarenje poslijeratne pravde, pri čemu često predstavlja jedini neosporni dokaz (1). U posljednjim desetljećima 20. stoljeća uobičajeni forenzički postupci identifikacije bili su analiza zuba i otisaka prstiju te usporedba zaživotnih i posmrtnih podataka (2). Analiza DNK-a postala je najpouzdanija metoda identifikacije tek krajem 20. stoljeća (3, 4), a u ranoj primjeni koristila se povremeno, uglavnom samo za pojedinačne forenzičke slučajeve i masovne katastrofe zatvorenoga tipa (5–8).

Nakon izbijanja rata u Hrvatskoj 1991. godine, a potom i u Bosni i Hercegovini (9), do 1992. godine više je od 150 000 osoba poginulo ili se vodilo nestalima (10, 11). U takvim okolnostima hrvatski su znanstvenici, djelujući u ratnim uvjetima i bez uspostavljenoga institucijskog okvira, u bliskoj suradnji s vodećim američkim forenzičkim stručnjacima, razvili jedan od najranijih, sustavnih i opsežnih programa identifikacije žrtava masovnih stradanja koji je utemeljen na analizi DNK-a.

FORENZIČKE METODE ANALIZE DNK-A PRIJE HRVATSKE INICIJATIVE

Forenzička primjena DNK-a u identifikaciji osoba potječe iz razvoja otiskivanja DNK-a koje su 1985. godine na Sveučilištu u Leicesteru uveli Alec Jeffreys, Victoria Wilson i Samuel Thein (3, 4). Forenzičke mogućnosti DNK-a ubrzo su potvrđene u kriminalističkim postupcima, osobito u slučaju Colina Pitchforka (12), u kojemu je analiza DNK-a omogućila isključenje lažnoga priznanja te identifikaciju stvarnoga počinitelja masovnim testiranjem. Tijekom kasnih osamdesetih i ranih devedesetih godina

INTRODUCTION

The identification of missing persons is an essential step not only for achieving personal closure for families, but also for achieving post-conflict justice, where it often represents the only undisputed evidence (1). In the final decades of the 20th century, established forensic tools included dental records, fingerprint analysis, and ante- and post-mortem data comparison (2). DNA emerged as the most reliable method of identification only in the late 20th century (3, 4), and its early application remained sporadic, largely limited to individual forensic cases and closed mass disasters (5–8). In Croatia and Bosnia and Herzegovina, following the outbreak of the Homeland War in 1991 in the context of the aggression against Croatia and the subsequent conflict in Bosnia and Herzegovina (9), more than 150,000 individuals had died or gone missing by 1992 (10, 11). In this context, Croatian scientists, working under war conditions and without an established institutional framework, in close collaboration with leading American forensic experts, developed one of the earliest systematic, large-scale DNA-based identification programs for mass-casualty victims.

DNA-BASED METHODS IN FORENSICS BEFORE THE CROATIAN INITIATIVE

The forensic use of DNA for human identification traces to Jeffreys, Wilson and Thein’s development of DNA fingerprinting at the University of Leicester in 1985 (3, 4). Its forensic potential was rapidly demonstrated in criminal investigations, most notably in the Colin Pitchfork case (12), where DNA evidence allowed both exclusion of a false confessor and identification of the true perpetrator through mass screening. Through the late 1980s and into the early 1990s, its forensic application remained

PDF page 48

20. stoljeća njezina je primjena u forenzici bila pretežito ograničena na kaznene predmete i pojedinačne, medijski izložene slučajeve, (8), poput identifikacije žrtve ubojstva na temelju koštanih ostataka primjenom sekvenciranja mitohondrijskoga DNK-a (13). Prve primjene u kontekstu masovnih katastrofa pojavljuju se početkom devedesetih godina 20. stoljeća, u istragama zrakoplovnih nesreća i požara, no u to je vrijeme njihova uporaba bila ograničena u opsegu i metodološkoj integraciji (5–8, 14–18).

U to su se vrijeme međunarodni protokoli za identifikaciju žrtava katastrofa većinom oslanjali na otiske prstiju, stomatološke kartone i antropološku usporedbu (2), jer metode zasnovane na analizi DNK-a još nisu bile u sustavnoj uporabi. Većinom je bilo riječ o pojedinačnim postignućima, pri čemu su mnoga ovisila o uzorcima koji nisu bili degradirani ili su bili tek minimalno degradirani (5–8, 14–16, 18–20). Nijedna institucija dotad nije razvila sustav koji je istodobno mogao obuhvatiti ekshumaciju posmrtnih ostataka iz više masovnih grobnica, izgradnju opsežne referentne baze podataka srodnika te integraciju analize DNK-a s cjelokupnim sustavom klasičnih forenzičkih disciplina unutar jedinstvenoga identifikacijskog protokola. Taj je metodološki izazov sustavno riješen u Kliničkome bolničkom centru Split, gdje je 1993. i 1994. godine provedena jedna od prvih sustavnih identifikacija žrtava iz masovnih grobnica primjenom analize DNK-a, u vrijeme kada drugdje nije postojao usporedivi program (21).

U široj međunarodnoj perspektivi, rane devedesete godine 20. stoljeća dio su prijelaznoga razdoblja u kojemu forenzička analiza DNK-a počinje izlaziti iz okvira kriminalističkih istraživanja i počinje se primjenjivati u identifikaciji žrtava masovnih stradanja. U tim su se početcima takve analize primjenjivale u identifikaciji osoba stradalih u požaru na trajektu Scandinavian Star (1990) (14,

essentially confined to criminal casework and isolated high-profile identity cases (8), such as the recovery of a murder victim from skeletal remains through mitochondrial sequencing (13). Early applications in mass-fatality contexts emerged in the early 1990s, including aircraft disasters and fire-related incidents, but these were limited in scale and methodological integration (5–8, 14–18).

At the time, international disaster victim identification protocols relied primarily on fingerprints, dental records, and anthropological comparison (2), as DNA-based methods were not yet systematically incorporated. These were primarily singular achievements, many of them relying on non-degraded or minimally degraded samples (5–8, 14–16, 18–20). No institution had yet designed a framework capable of simultaneously recovering remains from multiple mass graves, constructing a kinship-based reference database at scale, and integrating DNA with the full framework of classical forensic disciplines within a unified identification protocol. This design challenge was systematically addressed at the University Hospital of Split, where the first systematic DNA-based identification of war mass-grave victims was carried out in 1993–1994, at a time when no comparable program had yet been implemented elsewhere (21).

From a broader international perspective, the early 1990s represent a transitional phase in which forensic DNA analysis was beginning to move beyond criminal casework into mass-fatality identification. Initial applications were reported in incidents such as the Scandinavian Star ferry fire in 1990 (14, 15), the Airbus A320 crash at Mont Ste-Odile in 1992 (16), and the Waco disaster in 1993 (6), where DNA was used selectively alongside conventional identification methods.

PDF page 49

Hrvatski doprinos razvoju prve sustavne identifikacije žrtava iz masovnih grobnica analizom DNK-a / Croatian contributions to the first systematic DNA-based large-scale identification of mass graves war victims

15), zrakoplovnoj nesreći Airbusa A320 na Mont Sainte-Odileu (1992) (16) te opsadi u Wacou (1993) (6), pri čemu se analiza DNK-a primjenjivala selektivno, uz konvencionalne metode identifikacije.

Kasniji su događaji, poput bombaškoga napada na AMIA-u, 1994. godine, i zrakoplovne nesreće na Spitsbergenu, iz 1996. godine, pokazali sve veću integraciju i pouzdanost metoda analize DNK-a u identifikacijskim postupcima (5, 18). U tome kontekstu, hrvatsko iskustvo jedan je od najranijih dokumentiranih primjera sustavne identifikacije visoko degradiranih koštanih ostataka žrtava iz masovnih grobnica u ratnim uvjetima primjenom analize DNK-a. Takav je pristup objedinio terensko prikupljanje ostataka, uzorkovanje referentnih uzoraka srodnika te laboratorijsku analizu u jedinstveni identifikacijski okvir (21, 22).

IZVORI I PRISTUP

Ovaj se rad temelji na narativnoj i povijesnoj rekonstrukciji ranoga razvoja identifikacije žrtava rata analizom DNK-a u Hrvatskoj. Analiza obuhvaća više kategorija izvora, odabranih prema njihovoj izravnoj relevantnosti, vremenskoj bliskosti događajima te dokaznoj vrijednosti u dokumentiranju znanstvenih i operativnih vidova procesa identifikacije. Ključni izvori na kojima se temelji ova rekonstrukcija uključuju sljedeće reprezentativne materijale, prikazane približno kronološkim i dokaznim redoslijedom:

- Perkin-Elmer Corporation. Early Development of DNA Identification in Croatia (1993) – dokumentarni zapis koji prikazuje uspostavu kapaciteta za forenzičku analizu DNK-a u Splitu te prve sustavne identifikacije žrtava iz masovnih grobnica analizom DNK-a u ratnim uvjetima (23).

Subsequent events, including the AMIA bombing in 1994 and the Spitsbergen aircraft disaster in 1996, demonstrated increasing methodological integration and the growing reliability of DNA-based approaches (5, 18). Within this, the Croatian experience represents one of the earliest documented examples of systematic DNA-based identification of highly degraded skeletal remains from mass graves in a wartime context. This approach combined field recovery, kinship-based reference sampling, and laboratory analysis into a unified identification framework (21, 22).

SOURCES AND APPROACH

This article is based on a narrative and historical reconstruction of the early development of DNA-based identification of war victims in Croatia. The analysis integrates multiple categories of sources, selected for their direct relevance, contemporaneity, and evidentiary value in documenting both scientific and operational aspects of the identification process. Key sources underpinning this reconstruction include the following representative materials, presented in approximate chronological and evidentiary order:

– Perkin-Elmer Corporation. Early Development of DNA Identification in Croatia (1993) – documentary laboratory record showing the establishment of forensic DNA capability in Split and the first systematic DNA-based identification of war mass-grave victims, under wartime conditions (23).

- Gunby P. Medical Team Seeks to Identify Human Remains From Mass Graves of War in Former Yugoslavia. JAMA (1994) – jedan od najranijih međunarodnih medicinskih izvještaja koji opisuje uvođenje metoda analize DNK-a u Hrvatskoj te suradnju s američkim stručnjacima (25).

PRISTUP I OGRANIČENJA

Ovaj rad donosi povijesnu rekonstrukciju dijelom utemeljenu na iskustvima i doprinosima pojedinaca koji su bili izravno uključeni u opisane događaje. Kao takav, odražava perspektivu oblikovanu neposrednim sudjelovanjem, potkrijepljenu suvremenim izvorima, znanstvenim publikacijama i neovisnim institucijskim zapisima. Iako je uložena posebna pozornost pri integraciji različitih vrsta izvora i uključivanju vanjskih potvrda (npr. recenzirane literature, međunarodnih izvještaja i kongresnih svjedočenja), narativ može isticati one aspekte koji su bili uočljiviji ili bolje dokumentirani unutar ovoga specifičnog okvira suradnje. Sukladno tomu, ovaj prikaz treba tumačiti kao dokumentiranu perspektivu ranoga razvoja forenzičke identifikacije primjenom analize DNK-a, a ne kao iscrpnu globalnu povijest navedenoga polja.

KUPREŠKE IDENTIFIKACIJE I USPOSTAVA HRVATSKO-AMERIČKOGA PARTNERSTVA

Dana 4. travnja 1992. snage bosanskih Srba napale su područje Kupresa u Bosni i Hercegovini, pri čemu su ubijeni hrvatski civili i vojnici, a mnogi od njih pokopani su u masovnim grobnicama (24, 33). Poslije su u grobnicama pronađeni znatno degradirani posmrtni ostatci, u nekim slučajevima s tragovima vezivanja na zapešćima i/ili gležnjevima, a katkad i pomiješane ljudske i životinjske kosti, što je dodatno otežavalo identifikaciju (22, 24). Obitelji su mjesecima i godinama bile bez pouzdanih informacija, dok su neke namjerno dovodili u zabludu da su njihovi bližnji još uvijek živi. Kako je poslije opisano u dokumentarnim materijalima, živjele su u „lažnoj nadi“ (24).

PERSPECTIVE AND LIMITATIONS

This article presents a historical reconstruction based in part on the experiences and contributions of individuals directly involved in the described events. As such, it reflects a perspective informed by first-hand participation, supported by contemporaneous documentation, scientific publications, and independent institutional records. While care has been taken to integrate multiple source types and include external validations (e.g., peer-reviewed literature, international reports, and Congressional testimony), the narrative may emphasize aspects that were more visible or better documented within this specific collaborative framework. Accordingly, the account should be interpreted as a documented perspective on early developments in forensic DNA identification, rather than an exhaustive global history of the field.

THE KUPRES IDENTIFICATIONS AND THE EMERGENCE OF A CROATIAN- AMERICAN PARTNERSHIP

On 4 April 1992, Bosnian Serb forces attacked the Kupres region of Bosnia and Herzegovina, killing Croat civilians and soldiers and burying many of them in mass graves (24, 33). The graves were later found to contain highly decomposed remains, in some cases with ligatures on the wrists and/or ankles, and even commingled human and animal bones, which further complicated identification (22, 24). Families were left for months and years without reliable information, while some were deliberately misled into believing that their relatives were still alive. As later described in the documentary material, they had lived on “false hope” (24).

PDF page 53

Hrvatski doprinos razvoju prve sustavne identifikacije žrtava iz masovnih grobnica analizom DNK-a / Croatian contributions to the first systematic DNA-based large-scale identification of mass graves war victims

Terensko prikupljanje posmrtnih ostataka i ekshumacije, započeti 1993. godine, odvijali su se u iznimno opasnim uvjetima. Grobnice na području Kupresa morale su se iskopavati s posebnim oprezom jer su u njih srpske snage postavile eksplozivne naprave kako bi ozlijedile osobe koje provode ekshumacije i dodatno fragmentirale posmrtne ostatke. Posmrtni ostatci bili su izrazito krhki, zbog čega su se ručno iskopavali. Forenzički timovi suočavali su se s neposrednom opasnošću u uvjetima obilježenima izravnim dokazima pogubljenja i prikrivanja zločina (24) (Slika 1).

Tijekom toga razdoblja postalo je jasno to da se većinu posmrtnih ostataka ne će moći identificirati standardnim forenzičkim metodama. Uvidjevši ograničenja tradicionalnih postupaka, Šimun Anđelinović, tadašnji predstojnik Kliničkoga zavoda za patologiju, sudsku medicinu i citologiju, obratio se Draganu Primorcu, koji je u to vrijeme bio na poslijedoktorskome

Slika 1. Ekshumacije žrtava iz masovnih grobnica na području Kupresa u ranoj fazi forenzičkih istraživanja s prikazom postupaka iskopavanja posmrtnih ostataka i sudjelovanja multidisciplinarnoga tima.

Figure 1. Mass grave exhumations in the Kupres region during the early phase of forensic investigation, showing recovery procedures and multidisciplinary team involvement.

Field recovery and exhumation, starting in 1993, were conducted under dangerous conditions. Graves in the Kupres area had to be excavated carefully, as explosive devices had been placed inside them by Serbian forces to harm the excavators and further fragment the remains. The remains themselves were very fragile, so recovery was performed manually. The forensic teams thus confronted immediate danger in conditions marked by direct evidence of execution and concealment (24) (Figure 1).

During that period, it became evident that most human remains would not be identifiable using standard forensic methods. Recognizing the limitations of conventional pathology, Šimun Anđelinović, then Head of the Clinical Department of Pathology, Forensic Medicine and Cytology, reached out to Dragan Primorac, who at the time was a postdoctoral fellow at the University of Connecticut Health Center. Primorac was deeply engaged in molecular biology

PDF page 54

usavršavanju na Sveučilištu Connecticut Health Center. Dragan Primorac bio je intenzivno uključen u molekularnobiološka istraživanja usmjerena na molekularne mehanizme bolesti osteogenesis imperfecta i oblika osteohondrodistrofije, poznatoga kao nanomelija.

On je bio među odabranom skupinom iznimno perspektivnih mladih liječnika koju je Matko Marušić s Medicinskoga fakulteta u Zagrebu uputio na usavršavanje u inozemstvo, s jasnom misijom: stjecanja naprednih znanja i prijenosa vrhunskih znanstvenih spoznaja u Hrvatsku (34).

Razumjevši hitnost i povijesnu važnost trenutka, Dragan Primorac odmah je kontaktirao Henryja C. Leeja iz državne policije u Connecticutu. To je trebao biti kratak, desetominutni sastanak, ali prerastao je u višesatnu raspravu o znanstvenim, etičkim i logističkim aspektima primjene forenzičke genetike u identifikaciji osoba. Taj je razgovor postavio temelje prvoga posjeta Henryja C. Leeja Hrvatskoj te označio početak suradnje koja će duboko utjecati na razvoj identifikacije žrtava iz masovnih grobnica forenzičkom analizom DNK-a.

Slika 2. Henry C. Lee s novinarkom Kendrom Farn tijekom iskopavanja, uz improvizirani objekt koji se rabio kao privremeni prostor za obdukcije.

Figure 2. Henry C. Lee with journalist Kendra Farn during the investigation, with an improvised structure used as a temporary autopsy facility.

research, focusing on the molecular mechanisms underlying osteogenesis imperfecta and a form of osteochondrodystrophy called nanomelia. He was among a select group of exceptionally promising young physicians sent abroad by Matko Marušić of the Zagreb School of Medicine, with a clear mission: to acquire advanced knowledge and transfer cutting-edge scientific expertise back to Croatia (34).

Understanding the urgency and historical importance of the moment, Primorac immediately contacted Henry C. Lee from Connecticut State Police. What was initially intended to be a brief ten-minute meeting evolved into an intense and visionary discussion lasting more than two and a half hours, during which they explored the scientific, ethical, and logistical foundations of potential of using forensic genetics for human identification. That conversation laid the groundwork for Lee’s first visit to Croatia and marked the beginning of a transformative collaboration that would profoundly shape the future of mass grave victim identification through forensic DNA analysis.

The recovery was conducted by Croatian forensic teams, joined by American experts, including Henry C. Lee, and by other forensic specialists such as Michael Baden and Barbara Wolf, within a broader Croatian-American collaboration that combined field investigation, laboratory development, and international documentation of the events (24, 28). As part of this effort, a US television crew (WTNH News 8, Channel 8) was brought to Croatia and Bosnia and Herzegovina,

PDF page 55

Hrvatski doprinos razvoju prve sustavne identifikacije žrtava iz masovnih grobnica analizom DNK-a / Croatian contributions to the first systematic DNA-based large-scale identification of mass graves war victims

Posmrtne ostatke ekshumirali su hrvatski forenzički timovi, kojima su se pridružili američki stručnjaci, među njima Henry C. Lee i drugi, u okviru šire hrvatsko-američke suradnje koja je objedinjavala terenska istraživanja, razvoj laboratorijskih kapaciteta i međunarodno dokumentiranje događaja (24, 28). U sklopu tih aktivnosti u Hrvatsku i Bosnu i Hercegovinu dovedena je i američka televizijska ekipa (WTNH News 8, Channel 8), koja je po prvi put snimila i prikazala ekshumacije i njihov kontekst američkoj javnosti (24).

U jednome od tih dokumentaraca (Heroes of Hope, 1995) (24) izravno su zabilježeni uvjeti ekshumacija, uz izjavu „Moramo učiniti sve što možemo, i to na najbolji mogući način“, koja odražava operativna ograničenja u kojima se rad odvijao (24). Isti dokumentarni materijal pokazuje i ljudsku dimenziju terenskoga rada. Henry Lee istaknuo je kako bi „kao pojedinac želio učiniti mnogo više kako bih im pomogao“, dok je Kendra Farn susret s preživjelima opisala kao nešto što se „riječima ne može izraziti“ (24) (Slike 2 i 3).

U ožujku 1994. godine provedbenom aktivnošću u Kliničkome bolničkom centru Split uspostavljei su kapaciteti za identifikaciju analizom DNK-a. U tome trenutku nije postojao funkcionalan forenzički DNK laboratorij, a očekivalo se da će njegovo uvođenje trajati mjesecima (23). Umjesto toga, tim je laboratorij uspostavio „od nule – u manje od dva dana“; već trećega dana koštani uzorci bili su spremni za ekstrakciju, dok je petoga dana tim dobio profil DNK-a iz kontrolnoga slučaja povezanoga s djetetom te potvrdio rezultat usporedbom s uzorcima krvi roditelja (23). Taj je rezultat pokazao da se tipizacija DNK-a zasnovana na PCR-u može primijeniti na degradirane koštane ostatke iz ratnoga konteksta te unaprijediti proces identifikacije žrtava s područja Kupresa (21–23). Nakon prikupljanja, posmrtni su ostatci transportirani u Klinički bolnički centar Split, gdje se

where, for the first time, the exhumations and their context were recorded and broadcast to the American public (24).

In one of these documentary records, “Heroes of Hope” from 1995 (24), the conditions of exhumation were captured directly, including the statement that “we have to do whatever we can do the best”, reflecting the operational constraints under which the work was carried out (24). The same material documents the human dimension of fieldwork. Henry Lee noted that “as an individual, I wish I could do much more to help them”, while Kendra Farn described the encounter with survivors as something that “went beyond words” (24) (Figure 2, Figure 3).

In March 1994, the first operational mission established a DNA-based identification capacity at the University Hospital of Split. At that point, there was no functioning forensic DNA laboratory, and the expectation was that building one would take months (23). Instead, the team set up the laboratory “from scratch in less than two days”; by the third day, bone samples were ready for extraction, and by the fifth day, the team had obtained DNA from a control case involving a child and confirmed the result with parental blood samples (23). This result showed that PCR-based DNA typing could be applied to degraded skeletal remains from the war context and to advance the identification of victims from Kupres (21–23). Following recovery, the remains were transported to the University Hospital of Split, where identification continued in direct contact with families of the missing (Figure 4).

In this phase, the scientific process acquired its full humanitarian dimension. As Anđelinović stated, “our whole hospital is some sort of their last hope” (24). The first victims from the Kupres area were identified using AmpliType PM + HLA DQA1 PCR Amplification and Typing Kits (22, 23). This development was critical because con-

Figure 3. The forensic team meets community members at the local school in Kupres.

identifikacija nastavila u izravnome kontaktu s obiteljima nestalih (Slika 4).

U ovoj je fazi znanstveni proces dobio svoju punu humanitarnu dimenziju. Kako je istaknuo Anđelinović, „cijela je naša bolnica, na neki način, njihova posljednja nada” (24). Prve žrtve s područja Kupresa identificirane su s pomoću tipizacije AmpliType PM + HLA DQA1 PCR Amplification kitovima (22, 23). Taj je razvoj bio presudan jer konvencionalne metode same po sebi nisu bile dostatne. To je dodatno potvrđeno u kasnijim analizama koštanih ostataka iz masovnih grobnica, u kojima su multipleksni STR sustavi (AmpFlSTR® Profiler®, Profiler Plus™, COfiler™ i PowerPlex® 16) omogućili dobivanje multilokusnih profila DNK-a, čak i iz teško degradiranih uzoraka kostiju i zuba s minimalnim količinama DNK-a, kao i onima ispod granice detekcije (32).

U slučaju Kupresa, 35 od 61 žrtve moglo se identificirati primjenom standardnih forenzičkih metoda (Slika 5), dok je za preostale bila nuždna analiza DNK-a, koja je potom postala sastavni dio cjelokupnoga identifikacijskog postupka, a ne tek pomoćna metoda (21, 22). Istodobno su prikupljeni uzorci krvi i kose srodnika približno 150 nestalih osoba, čime je razvijen jedan od prvih forenzičkih referentnih skupova podataka DNK-a srodnika u kontekstu masovnoga sukoba (21, 22). Kako bi se pojasnio slijed događaja i brzina razvoja kapaciteta za identifikaciju analizom DNK-a, potaknutih analizom žrtava iz Kupresa, vremenska linija s ključnim aktivnostima prikazana je na Slici 6.

ventional methods alone were insufficient. This was further demonstrated in subsequent analyses of skeletal remains from mass graves, where multiplex short tandem repeat (STR) systems (AmpFlSTR® Profiler®, Profiler Plus™, COfiler™ and PowerPlex® 16) enabled the recovery of multilocus DNA profiles even from highly degraded bone and tooth samples with minimal or undetectable quantities of human DNA (32).

Uključivanje američkih forenzičkih stručnjaka potaknulo je i posredovanje organizacije AmeriCares te snažna podrška Barbare Bush (Slika 7), čija je uključenost bila ključna za provedbu humanitarne misije, donaciju Perkin-Elmerove PCR tehnologije i okupljanje Henryja C. Leeja, Michaela Badena, Barbare Wolf, Mosesa Schanfielda, Dragana Primorca, Stephana Skakela i drugih (23–25, 28). Ovi su znanstvenici doputovali u Hrvatsku kako bi surađivali sa splitskim timom, što potvrđuje i kasniji opis splitskoga programa kao jednog od najranijih sustavnih programa identifikacije ratnih žrtava analizom DNK-a (21, 28). Njihova suradnja nije bila samo tehničke prirode. Kako je Šimun Anđelinović rekao za Henryja C. Leeja, „imamo vrlo slične poglede na život“, a Dragan Primorac dodao: „ako postignem samo 30 % ili 40 % onoga što je on postigao, bit ću iznimno uspješan“, čime se istodobno oslikava bliskost suradnje i asimetrija iskustva koja je ubrzala prijenos znanja (24).

Nakon dobivanja rezultata analize DNK-a obiteljima su prikazani odjevni predmeti, osobne stvari, koštani ostatci te zaključci potkrijepljeni analizom DNK-a. Mnogi su u početku reagirali nepovjerenjem, jer su dugo bili izloženi proturječnim informacijama, kao i pokušajima

In the Kupres case, thirty-five of sixty-one victims could be identified by standard forensic methods (Figure 5), but the remainder required DNA analysis, which then became integral to the entire identification framework rather than a marginal supplement (21, 22). At the same time, blood and hair samples from relatives of approximately 150 missing persons were collected, and one of the first kinship-based forensic DNA reference datasets in a mass-conflict setting was created (21, 22). To clarify the temporal sequence of events and the rapid development of DNA-based identification capacity in relation to the Kupres case, a timeline of key activities is presented in Figure 6.

The involvement of American forensic experts facilitated by AmeriCares and strongly supported through the advocacy of Barbara Bush (Figure 7), whose engagement was instrumental in enabling the humanitarian mission, including the donation of Perkin-Elmer PCR technology, brought together Henry C. Lee, Michael Baden, Barbara Wolf, Moses Schanfield, Dragan Primorac, Stephan Skakel, and others (23–25, 28). These scientists travelled to Croatia to work collaboratively with the Split team, which is consistent with the later characterization of the Split program as one of the earliest systematic DNA-based identification frameworks for war victims (21, 28). Their cooperation was not merely technical. As Anđelinović said of Lee, “we have very similar points of view on life”, and Primorac added, “if I obtain only 30% or 40% of

iznude uz tvrdnje da su nestali još uvijek živi (23, 24). Nakon što su rezultati objašnjeni i prihvaćeni, pozitivne identifikacije omogućile su pogreb pokojnika te okončale dugotrajnu neizvjesnost za mnoge obitelji (22, 23).

Susreti s obiteljima utjecali su i na članove forenzičkih timova. Henry C. Lee prisjetio se kako je „susret s obitelji promijenio njegovu životnu perspektivu“, dodajući da život treba iskoristiti „za nešto smisleno i konstruktivno“ (24). Ove izjave ne treba tumačiti kao stilizaciju ili retoričko uljepšavanje. One označuju trenutak u kojemu su se znanstvena identifikacija, psihološko razrješenje za obitelji i dokazni okvir za kasnije postupke za ratne zločine spojili u jedinstvenu forenzičku misiju (24, 30).

Slika 5. Prikaz forenzičke identifikacije u praksi: zajednički rad hrvatsko-američkih stručnjaka, obdukcijska sala Kliničkoga bolničkog centra Split, žrtve ekshumirane iz masovnih grobnica na području Kupresa.

Figure 5. Forensic identification in practice: joint Croatian-American autopsy work at the University Hospital of Split on victims recovered from Kupres mass graves.

what he obtained, I’ll be extremely successful” – a formulation that captures both the closeness of the collaboration and the asymmetry of experience that accelerated knowledge transfer (24).

Upon obtaining the DNA results, families were shown clothing, belongings, skeletal remains, and DNA-supported conclusions. Many initially reacted with mistrust because they had long been exposed to contradictory information, including extortion schemes based on claims that the missing were still alive (23, 24). Once the results were explained and accepted, however, identifications allowed funerals to take place and ended prolonged uncertainty for many families (22, 23).

The encounter with relatives also changed the forensic teams themselves. Henry Lee recalled that “meeting with the family changed my life perspective”, adding that life should be used “to do something meaningful, constructive” (24). These statements should not be read as embellishment; they mark the point at which scientific identification, psychological closure for families, and the evidentiary basis for later war-crimes proceedings converged into a single forensic mission (24, 30).
