PREPOZNAVANJE ZNANSTVENOGA DOPRINOSA U AMERIČKOME KONGRESU (6. PROSINCA 1995.)

Najznačajnija vanjska potvrda hrvatsko-američke forenzičke suradnje nije prikazana u znanstvenom časopisu, nego na saslušanju u Kongresu Sjedinjenih Američkih Država. Dana 6. prosinca 1995. Komisija za sigurnost i suradnju u Europi (Helsinška komisija) održala je saslušanje 104. saziva Kongresa, 1. zasjedanja, pod nazivom „Mass Graves and Other Atrocities in Bosnia“, kojim je predsjedao zastupnik Christopher H. Smith, uz sudjelovanje zastupnika Stenyja H. Hoyera, Johna Edwarda Portera, Billa Zeliffa, Benjamina Gilmana, Jamesa Morana i Sheile Jackson-Lee. Treća svjedokinja bila je Barbara C. Wolf, forenzička patologinja iz Albanyja (New York), čije svjedočenje zauzima važno mjesto u povijesti forenzičkih znanosti: riječ je o neposrednoj stručnoj procjeni forenzičke infrastrukture u Splitu, unesenoj u trajni zapis Kongresa Sjedinjenih Američkih Država (30).

Barbara Wolf boravila je u Hrvatskoj i Bosni i Hercegovini između 8. i 13. listopada 1995. godine pod pokroviteljstvom organizacije AmeriCares. Delegacija kojoj se pridružila uključivala je neke od najistaknutijih forenzičkih znanstvenika u Sjedinjenim Američkim Državama: Michaela Badena, suvoditelja Jedinice za forenzičke znanosti Državne policije New Yorka; Henryja C. Leeja, glavnog kriminalista Državne policije Connecticuta; Mosesa Schanfielda, stručnjaka za DNK iz Denvera; Davida Rowea, profesora pedijatrije s institucije University of Connecticut Health Center te Dragana Primorca, tadašnjega poslijedoktorskog istraživača na toj instituciji. Kako je Barbara Wolf navela u službenome zapisniku, cilj njihova dolaska bio je nedvosmislen: „Došli smo u Hrvatsku i Bosnu kako bismo surađivali s forenzičkim timom Kliničkog bolničkog centra u Splitu i pružili mu podršku” (30). Činjenica da su američki

RECOGNITION AT THE UNITED STATES CONGRESS FOR SCIENTIFIC CONTRIBUTIONS (6 DECEMBER 1995)

The most consequential external validation of the Croatian-American forensic collaboration was delivered not in a journal, but in a hearing of the United States Congress. On 6 December 1995, the Commission on Security and Cooperation in Europe, the Helsinki Commission, convened a hearing of the One Hundred Fourth Congress, First Session, entitled “Mass Graves and Other Atrocities in Bosnia,” chaired by Representative Christopher H. Smith and attended by Representatives Steny H. Hoyer, John Edward Porter, Bill Zeliff, Benjamin Gilman, James Moran, and Sheila Jackson-Lee. The hearing’s third witness was Barbara C. Wolf, a forensic pathologist from Albany, New York, whose testimony occupies an important place in the history of forensic science: a firsthand peer assessment of Split’s forensic infrastructure, entered into the permanent record of the United States Congress (30).

Dr Wolf had travelled to Croatia and Bosnia between 8 and 13 October, 1995, under the sponsorship of AmeriCares. The delegation she joined comprised some of the most distinguished forensic scientists in the USA: Michael Baden, co-director of the Forensic Sciences Unit of the New York State Police; Henry Lee, chief criminalist for the Connecticut State Police; Moses Schanfield, a DNA specialist from Denver; and David Rowe, Professor of Pediatrics at the University of Connecticut Health Center, and Dragan Primorac, a postdoctoral fellow at the University of Connecticut Health Center. Their purpose, as Wolf stated on the record, was unambiguous: “We went to Croatia and Bosnia to work with and lend our support to the forensic team from the Clinical Hospital in Split, Croatia” (30). That American experts travelled to Croatia, rather than the reverse, reflects

PDF page 62

stručnjaci dolazili u Hrvatsku, a ne obratno, odražava razinu stručnosti koju je splitski tim već tada bio dosegnuo (31). Uz sudjelovanje Henryja C. Leeja, Michaela Badena, Mosesa Schanfielda i Mitchella Hollanda, hrvatski je tim preuzeo dotad nezabilježen zadatak prikupljanja DNK dokaza za Međunarodni kazneni sud u Haagu u kontekstu postupaka za ratne zločine i zločine protiv čovječnosti. Primorac je sažeo duh toga nastojanja jednostavnom izjavom: „Svi podatci u našim knjigama bit će dostupni tužiteljima, bez obzira na to tko je počinio zločine” (28).

Barbara Wolf opisala je tijekom svoga svjedočenja rad u Splitu s kliničkom preciznošću. Navela je da je posjetila dvije masovne grobnice u blizini Kupresa, jednu s 34 tijela, drugu s tri, te treću grobnicu u Petrinji s gotovo sto tijela. Kazala je kako je američki tim radio sa Šimunom Anđelinovićem i njegovim suradnicima u obdukcijskoj sali u Splitu, pri čemu je istodobno na stolovima bilo pet ili šest tijela, dok su članovi obitelji ulazili naizmjenično kako bi pokušali identificirati svoje bližnje. Do kraja jednoga radnog dana identificirano je 27 od 34 tijela iz prve kupreške grobnice te sva tri vojnika iz druge; „DNK testiranje”, kako je izjavila u Kongresu, „nastavlja se na preostalim neidentificiranim tijelima”. Također je istaknula da je laboratorij bio „uspostavljen prije godinu dana uz određene smjernice dr. sc. Henryja C. Leeja” te da se laboratorij već razvijao prema primjeni analize mitohondrijskoga DNK-a (30).

Najznačajniji dio ispitivanja zabilježen je tijekom pitanja zastupnika Hoyera. Na pitanje jesu li hrvatski patolozi kompetentni za kvalitetan forenzički rad, Barbara Wolf odgovorila je: „U potpunosti jesu, i mislim da je ovo doista primjer kako znanost nadilazi političke podjele. Tehnike koje se rabe u Sjedinjenim Američkim Državama uvelike su iste kao i one koje su se rabile ondje, u DNK laboratoriju u Splitu.” Nadalje je izvijestila Kongres da

the level of expertise already established by the Split team at that time (31). Joined by Henry C. Lee, Michael Baden, Moses Schanfield, and Mitchell Holland, the Croatian team undertook the unprecedented task of producing DNA evidence for the International Criminal Tribunal in The Hague as it confronted allegations of crimes against humanity. Primorac captured the ethos of the effort with a simple declaration: “All the data in our books will be available for prosecutors, no matter who committed the crimes” (28).

Dr Wolf’s testimony described the work at Split with clinical precision. She reported visiting two mass graves near Kupres, one containing 34 bodies, one containing 3, and a third grave at Petrinja containing nearly 100 bodies. She testified that the American team worked directly alongside Anđelinović and his colleagues in the Split autopsy room, with five or six bodies on tables simultaneously and family members rotating through to attempt identification. By the end of a single working day, 27 of the 34 bodies from the first Kupres grave and all 3 soldiers from the second had been identified; “DNA testing”, she told Congress, “is continuing on the remaining bodies yet unidentified”. She also noted that the laboratory had been “set up a year ago with some guidance from Dr Lee” and was already expanding beyond standard DNA typing into mitochondrial DNA techniques (30).

The exchange that carries the greatest historical weight came during questioning by Representative Hoyer. Asked whether Croatian pathologists were competent to do good forensic work, Dr Wolf replied: “Very much so, and I think this was really an example of science crossing political lines. The techniques that we use in the United States were very much the same techniques that they were using there, including the DNA laboratory in Split”. She further informed Congress that the exchange of expertise had been bidirectional: “Croa-

se suradnja odvijala u oba smjera: „Hrvatski su znanstvenici dolazili u Sjedinjene Američke Države radi usavršavanja u tim tehnologijama. Naši stručnjaci za analizu DNK-a, dr. sc. Henry C. Lee, dr. sc. Moses Schanfield i drugi, imali su uvid u njihov rad i tehnologiju. Kapaciteti su svakako postojali. Ono što je potrebno jesu resursi i ljudi.” Na pitanje o glavnim preprekama u identifikaciji bila je izričita: problem nije bio u stručnosti ili metodologiji, nego u broju nestalih osoba i sustavnome uništavanju zaživotnih podataka – „Budući da su cijeli gradovi uništeni, postoji vrlo malo podataka kojima se moguće koristiti” – što je referentnu DNK bazu podataka srodnika, uspostavljenu u Splitu, učinilo ne samo korisnom, nego i operativno nezamjenjivom (30).

Ovo je saslušanje u Kongresu, prema našim saznanjima, u Hrvatskoj gotovo nepoznato, a zaslužuje biti prepoznato. Ono predstavlja oblik institucijskoga priznanja koji se rijetko dodjeljuje znanstvenim institucijama koje ne pripadaju vodećim zapadnim znanstvenim središtima: riječ je o formalnoj, u zapisnik unesenoj i pod prisegom iznesenoj procjeni pred Kongresom Sjedinjenih Američkih Država, da je bolnički laboratorij u Hrvatskoj djelovao na samome vrhu svojega područja kao ravnopravan partner vodećim forenzičkim centrima u Sjedinjenim Američkim Državama.

tian scientists have come to this country and spent time working with the technologies. Our DNA experts, Dr Lee, Dr Schanfield, and others, have looked at their work and technology. The capabilities are certainly there. It is resources and people that are needed”. When asked about the primary obstacles to identification, she was explicit: the challenge was not competence or methodology, but the volume of missing persons and the systematic destruction of antemortem records “because the entire towns are destroyed, there is very little to use”, which made the kinship DNA database constructed at Split not merely useful but operationally indispensable (30).

This Congressional testimony is, to our knowledge, essentially unknown in Croatia. It deserves to be recognized. It constitutes a form of institutional recognition rarely accorded to science conducted outside the major Western powers: a formal assessment, on the record, under oath, before the United States Congress, that a Croatian hospital laboratory was operating at the frontier of its field, as a peer institution to leading forensic centers in the United States.

Hrvatski doprinos razvoju prve sustavne identifikacije žrtava iz masovnih grobnica analizom DNK-a / Croatian contributions to the first systematic DNA-based large-scale identification of mass graves war victims

NASTAVAK ZNANSTVENOGA NASLJEÐA

Stvaran doprinos hrvatske inicijative ne očituje se samo u onome što je postignuto 1993. i 1994. godine, nego i u onome što je time pokrenuto. Ono što je započelo kao HLA tipizacija utemeljena na PCR-u na trima uzorcima kostiju iz Kupresa, razvilo se u istoj hrvatsko-američkoj mreži u sustavan program metodološkoga razvoja. Prijelaz na multipleksnu analizu STR-ova u kombinaciji s optimiziranim protokolima ekstrakcije, koji su pokazali prednost kompaktne kosti dugih kostiju u odnosu na elemente lubanje i rebara, povećao je uspješnost identifikacije s 20 % – 25 % na približno 85 % (21). Podatci o učestalosti alela u populaciji, nuždni za statistički pouzdanu identifikaciju, prikupljeni su u prvome zajedničkom istraživanju hrvatskoga znanstvenika Dragana Primorca i Brucea Budowlea iz laboratorija FBI-ja. U slučajevima u kojima su nuklearni biljezi bili nedostatni zbog izrazite degradacije materijala, u okviru te suradnje razvijene su metode analize mitohondrijskoga DNK-u: analiza imobiliziranih SSO probnih sustava za hipervarijabilne regije I i II, ispitana na koštanim uzorcima u zajedničkim hrvatsko-američkim istraživanjima; mt HV+ HaploArray za kodirajuće i nekodirajuće polimorfne lokuse; te Y-STR i miniSTR analize primijenjene na žrtve iz masovnih grobnica iz razdoblja Drugoga svjetskog rata iz Slovenije, Hrvatske i Hercegovine, koje nije bilo moguće razriješiti standardnim metodama (21). Logičan nastavak toga razvoja čini forenzička genomika: primjena sekvenciranja nove generacije (NGS) na autosomne STR-ove, identifikacijske SNP panele i mitohondrijski DNK, primijenjena u postupku identifikacije časne sestre Marije Krucifikse Kozulić, koji izravno odražava metodološki pristup započet na Kupresu (35).

Doseg ovih metoda odavno nadilazi bilo koji pojedinačni nacionalni kontekst. Metodološki okvir primijenjen u Hrvatskoj, koji obuhvaća istodobne ekshumacije, izgradnju referentne

CONTINUING SCIENTIFIC LEGACY

The true measure of the Croatian initiative is not only what it accomplished in 1993–1994 but what it set in motion. What began as PCR-based HLA typing on three Kupres bone samples evolved through the same Croatian-American network, into a systematic program of methodological evolution. The shift to multiplex STR analysis, combined with optimized extraction protocols that established the superiority of long-bone cortical samples over skull and rib elements, raised identification success rates from 20–25% to approximately 85% (21). Population allele frequency data required for statistically rigorous identification were generated in the first collaborative study between Croatian scientist Dragan Primorac and Bruce Budowle from FBI Laboratory. Where nuclear markers failed on maximally degraded material, the network developed mitochondrial approaches: immobilized SSO probe analysis of hypervariable regions I and II, validated on bone samples in joint Croatian-American studies; the mt HV+ HaploArray for coding and non-coding polymorphic targets; and Y-STR and miniSTR analysis applied to World War II-era mass graves in Slovenia, Croatia, and Herzegovina that could not be resolved using standard methods (21). The logical extension to this path is forensic genomics: massively parallel next-generation sequencing of autosomal STR, identity SNP panels, and mitochondrial targets, deployed in the identification of Sister Marija Crucifiksa Kozulić, a case that directly reflected the methodological approach initiated at Kupres (35).

The practical reach of these methods has long transcended any single national context. The methodological framework applied in Croatia, simultaneous excavation, kinship-based reference database construction, multi-marker identification, shared key elements with a range of international contexts, including victims of the 11 September attacks in New York and Wash-

PDF page 66

baze podataka srodnika te identifikaciju s pomoću više genskih biljega, imao je zajednička ključna obilježja s nizom međunarodnih primjena, od identifikacije žrtava napada 11. rujna u New Yorku i Washingtonu, D.C., žrtava tsunamija, do masovnih grobnica u Iraku, Siriji i Libiji (21). Međunarodna komisija za nestale osobe, osnovana 1996. godine (36), na temelju metodoloških postavaka koje je razvio splitski tim, identificirala je više od 27 000 osoba s područja bivše Jugoslavije (37). Ono što je nastalo u kriznim uvjetima u jednoj hrvatskoj kliničkoj bolnici, tijekom jednoga desetljeća postalo je dio međunarodne standardne prakse.

Suradnja koju je Barbara Wolf 1995. godine u Kongresu opisala kao „znanost koja nadilazi političke podjele” pokazala se strukturno održivom. Ista mreža; Dragan Primorac, Šimun Anđelinović, Henry C. Lee, Michael Baden, Mitchell Holland, Bruce Budowle, Damir Marjanović i njihovi znanstveni suradnici i dalje zajednički objavljuju radove, razvijaju metode i obrazuju nove generacije znanstvenika. Pregledni rad objavljen 2024. godine u časopisu Croatian Medical Journal (21) uključuje mnoga ista imena kao i rad iz 1996. godine u časopisu Journal of Forensic Sciences (22). Međunarodno društvo za primijenjene biološke znanosti (ISABS) institucionaliziralo je navedeni interdisciplinarni pristup te povezalo forenzičku genetiku, antropologiju, kliničku medicinu i translacijsku genomiku, pristup koji je prvi put primijenjen u laboratorijima i obdukcijskoj sali Kliničkoga bolničkog centra Split (38). Put od HLA tipizacije na trima kupreškim žrtvama (22) do forenzičko-genetičke analize drevnih ostataka (39), koordiniran unutar iste međunarodne znanstvene zajednice, pokazuje da hrvatsko-američko partnerstvo, nastalo u ratnim uvjetima, već više od trideset godina sudjeluje u oblikovanju globalne prakse forenzičke identifikacije.

ington, D.C., to tsunami casualties, and to mass graves in Iraq, Syria, and Libya (21). The International Commission on Missing Persons, established in 1996 (36), eventually identified more than 27,000 individuals from the former Yugoslavia (37) based on methodological principles the Split team had pioneered. What was invented under crisis in a Croatian clinical hospital became, within a decade, part of international standard practice.

The collaboration that Dr Wolf described to Congress in 1995 as “science crossing political lines” has proven structurally durable. The same network, Primorac, Anđelinović, Lee, Baden, Holland, Budowle, Marjanović, and their scientific peers continue to publish, develop methods, and train scientists jointly. The 30-year review published in Croatian Medical Journal in 2024 (21) carries many of the same names as the 1996 paper in the Journal of Forensic Sciences (22). The International Society for Applied Biological Sciences (ISABS) has institutionalized the interdisciplinary ethos and united forensic genetics, anthropology, clinical medicine, and translational genomics, which was first employed in the identification rooms of University Hospital of Split (38). It was founded by Croatian (Dragan Primorac) and American (Moses Schanfield) scientists as an institutional framework for continuing interdisciplinary collaboration in forensic and applied genetics. Stanimir Vuk-Pavlović later joined ISABS, while Šimun Anđelinović served as the Chairman of the Organizing Committee in Croatia. The journey from HLA typing of three Kupres (22) victims to forensic genomic sequencing of ancient remains (39), coordinated by the same international scientific community, demonstrates that a Croatian-American partnership, born in wartime, has contributed to shaping the global practice of forensic identification for thirty years and counting.

PDF page 67

Hrvatski doprinos razvoju prve sustavne identifikacije žrtava iz masovnih grobnica analizom DNK-a / Croatian contributions to the first systematic DNA-based large-scale identification of mass graves war victims

ZAKLJUČAK

Trideset godina nakon kupreških ekshumacija činjenice su jasne i nedvojbeno potvrđene, među ostalim, i u zapisima Kongresa Sjedinjenih Američkih Država. Tim Kliničkoga bolničkog centra Split nije djelovao u sjeni bolje opremljenih institucija niti je čekao vanjske smjernice. Djelovao je kao ravnopravni partner, a u svojemu specifičnom području, i kao pokretač razvoja u ravnopravnoj suradnji s američkim znanstvenicima koji su dolazili u Hrvatsku. Svjedočenje Barbare Wolf pred Helsinškom komisijom 6. prosinca 1995., u Hrvatskoj donedavno uglavnom neprepoznato, dokumentira navedenu suradnju u trenutku njezina formalnog međunarodnog priznanja. Taj dokument treba prepoznati i uključiti u povijesno razumijevanje razvoja forenzičke genetike u Hrvatskoj te njezina doprinosa svjetskoj forenzičkoj praksi.

Uz znanstvene i metodološke doprinose, hrvatsko je iskustvo sredinom devedesetih godina 20. stoljeća privuklo neuobičajenu pozornost znanstvene, političke i medijske javnosti. Rezultati splitskoga tima istodobno su bili dokumentirani u vodećim znanstvenim časopisima (JAMA, Science), praćeni u velikim međunarodnim medijima (The New York Times, Chicago Tribune, People Weekly) te formalno prepoznati na saslušanju u Kongresu Sjedinjenih Američkih Država (6. prosinca 1995.) (Slika 8). Ta istodobna prisutnost u znanstvenome, institucijskome i javnome prostoru ne odražava samo novost primijenjenih metoda, nego i širi značaj forenzičke identifikacije kao humanitarnoga i dokaznoga postupka. U tome se smislu hrvatska inicijativa nije razvijala izolirano, nego je nastala na sjecištu znanosti, međunarodne politike i javne percepcije, čime je dodatno učvrstila svoju ulogu u oblikovanju globalnoga okvira identifikacije žrtava masovnih stradanja.

CONCLUSION

Thirty years after the excavations at Kupres, the record is clear and formally documented, including in the proceedings of the United States Congress. The team at the University Hospital of Split did not work in the shadow of better-resourced institutions, nor did they await external guidance. They worked as peers and, in their specific domain, as early contributors in this domain, alongside American scientists who came to Croatia to collaborate on equal terms. Dr Wolf’s testimony before the Helsinki Commission on 6 December 1995, largely unrecognized in Croatia until recently, captures that partnership at its moment of formal international recognition. This record should be acknowledged and integrated into the historical understanding of the development of forensic genetics in Croatia and its contribution to global forensic practice.

Beyond its scientific and methodological contributions, the Croatian experience attracted an unusual convergence of scientific, political, and media attention during the mid-1990s. The work conducted in Split was simultaneously documented in leading scientific journals (JAMA, Science), reported in major international media outlets (The New York Times, Chicago Tribune, People Weekly), and formally recognized in a hearing before the United States Congress (6 December 1995) (Figure 8). This parallel visibility across scientific, institutional, and public domains reflects not only the novelty of the methods employed, but also the broader significance of forensic identification as both a humanitarian and evidentiary practice. In this sense, the Croatian initiative did not evolve in isolation, but emerged at the intersection of science, international policy, and public awareness, further reinforcing its role in shaping the global framework of mass-fatality identification.

Zahvala: Ovaj rad posvećen je uspomeni na dr. sc. Henryja C. Leeja, iznimnoga čovjeka, često nazivanoga Sherlockom Holmesom našega vremena, jednoga od začetnika suvremenih forenzičkih znanosti i svima nama dragoga prijatelja. U vrijeme kada je forenzička analiza DNK-a bila u svojim začetcima, dr. sc. Henry C. Lee pristupao je znanosti s ingenioznošću i odlučnošću, razvijajući i primjenjujući metode u uvjetima koji bi se danas smatrali skromnima, no koji su bili temeljem njegova djelovanja. Njegov rad nije bio ograničen na renomirane laboratorije, već je vođen znatiželjom, upornošću i dubokim uvjerenjem da znanost mora služiti istini i humanosti. Dr. sc. Dragan Primorac i dr. sc. Šimun Anđelinović dijelili su duboko prijateljstvo s dr. sc. Henryjem C. Leejem i imali jedinstvenu priliku raditi „rame uz rame“, tijekom povijesnoga uvođenja forenzičke genetike u identifikaciju žrtava rata iz masovnih grobnica. U tim su začetcima oni dijelili interdisciplinarna znanja i napredne tehničke analize, ali isto tako i duboko razumijevanje vrijednosti koje su obilježile život i rad dr. sc. Henryja C. Leeja: metodološku strogost, intelektualnu otvorenost, poniznost i nepokolebljivu posvećenost pravdi. Njegov boravak u Hrvatskoj tijekom prvih pokušaja identifikacije žrtava rata (1991. – 1995.) bio je više od znanstvene suradnje. U trenutku kada je znanost nadilazila granice kako bi odgovorila na ljudsku patnju, to je bio čin solidarnosti. Onima koji su poznavali dr. sc. Henryja C. Leeja, on je bio ne samo jedna od najutjecajnijih osoba u razvoju moderne forenzičke znanosti nego i darežljiv kolega i istinski prijatelj.

Acknowledgements: This work is dedicated to the memory of Dr Henry C. Lee, an extraordinary man, often regarded as the Sherlock Holmes of our time, one of the pioneers of modern forensic science, and, to us, a dear friend. At a time when forensic DNA analysis was only beginning to emerge, Dr Lee approached science with ingenuity and determination, developing and applying methods under conditions that today would seem modest, yet were foundational for the field. His work was not confined to established laboratories; it was driven by curiosity, persistence, and a deep belief that science must serve truth and humanity. Dr Dragan Primorac and Dr Šimun Anđelinović shared a profound friendship with Dr Lee, having the rare privilege of working alongside him during the historic introduction of forensic genetics into the identification of war victims from mass graves. Through this pioneering collaboration, they shared interdisciplinary knowledge and advanced technical expertise but also a deep understanding of the values that defined Dr Lee’s life and work – scientific rigor, intellectual openness, humility, and an unwavering commitment to justice. His presence in Croatia during the early efforts to identify the victims of the 1991–1995 Homeland War reflected more than scientific collaboration. It was an act of solidarity, at a moment when science crossed borders to respond to human suffering. To those who knew Dr Henry C. Lee, he was not only one of the most influential figures in the development of modern forensic science, but also a generous colleague and a true friend.

Mass Graves and other Atrocities in Bosnia Masovne grobnice i drugi zločini u Bosni

Na hrvatski prevela Ana Mršić Zdilar.

srijeda, 6. prosinca 1995.

komisija za sigurnost i suradnju

u europi, washington, dc

Komisija se sastala u 14:06 sati, u sobi 628 zgrade Senata Dirksen, pod predsjedanjem uvaženoga Christophera H. Smitha, predsjednika Komisije.

Prisutni članovi Komisije: uvaženi Christopher H. Smith (predsjednik), uvaženi Steny. D. Hoyer i uvaženi John Edward Porter.

Prisutni članovi Zastupničkog doma: uvaženi Bill Zeliff, uvaženi Benjamin Gilman i uvaženi James Moran.

Prisutni svjedoci: Ivan Lupis, David Rohde i Barbara Wolf.

UVODNO OBRAĆANJE PREDSJEDNIKA CHRISTOPHERA H. SMITHA

Predsjednik SMITH. Komisija započinje sa sjednicom. Sukob na prostoru bivše Jugoslavije, posebno u Bosni, bio je stalna tema američke vanjske politike tijekom proteklih četiriju godina. U posljednjih nekoliko tjedana razrada mirovnoga sporazuma, uz obvezu sudjelovanja snaga NATO-a, bila je u središtu vanjskopolitičkoga djelovanja.

wednesday, december 6, 1995

commission on security and cooperation

in europe,washington, dc

The Commission met at 2:06 p.m., in room 628 of the Dirksen Senate Office Building, Honorable Christopher H. Smith, chairman of the Commission, presiding.

Commission members present: Hon. Christopher H. Smith, Chairman; Hon. Steny. D. Hoyer; and Hon. John Edward Porter.

House members present: Hon. Bill Zeliff; Hon. Benjamin Gilman; and Hon. James Moran.

Witnesses present: Ivan Lupis, David Rohde, and Barbara Wolf.

OPENING STATEMENT OF CHAIRMAN CHRISTOPHER H. SMITH

Chairman SMITH. The Commission will come to order. The conflict in the former Yugoslavia, and in Bosnia in particular, has been a constant issue of the U.S. foreign policy agenda during the past 4 years. In these past few weeks, the working-out of a peace agreement with its accompanying commitment for NATO forces has been the leading issue on that agenda.

PDF page 76

Današnje saslušanje o Bosni, međutim, bavi se potresnom i jezivom temom: zločinima nad djecom, ženama i muškarcima. Ti neopisivi zločini razlog su zbog kojega je Bosna zauzela tako važno mjesto u našoj vanjskoj politici.

Dok se međunarodna zajednica bavi formalnostima razmjene teritorija među zaraćenim stranama i stanovništvom, stvarnost Bosne jest da su ljudi, nedužni civili, bili silovani, mučeni, protjerivani iz svojih domova i pogubljivani. Dokumentiranje ratnih zločina, izvođenje dokaza pred Sudom za ratne zločine i traženje pravde putem Suda temelj su pomirenja i istinskoga mira.

Helsinška komisija obvezna je, među ostalim, dokumentirati povrede ljudskih prava. Ta je uloga ključna sama po sebi. Dugujemo to žrtvama i nadamo se da će pozornost posvećena povredama ljudskih prava u Bosni odvratiti druge od budućih sličnih povreda. Unošenje pojedinosti u službeni zapisnik Komisije pomaže i nama koji moramo razmatrati diplomatske i vojne odgovore kako bismo ostali usredotočenima na ozbiljnost situacije s kojom se suočavamo.

Danas je s nama troje stručnjaka koji su proveli određeno vrijeme u Bosni, i susreli se sa stvarnim žrtvama ovoga rata ili preživjelim članovima njihovih obitelji te dokumentirali njihove nezamislive patnje. Najprije ćemo saslušati Ivana Lupisa, istraživača iz organizacije Human Rights Watch/Helsinki, koji će opisati kako i zašto se Srebrenica, koja je proglašena sigurnom zonom UN-a, nije uspjela oduprijeti militantima i što se zbilo nakon toga.

Zatim ćemo saslušati Davida Rohdea iz organizacije Christian Science Monitor, koji je uspio posjetiti lokacije navodnih masovnih grobnica u istočnoj Bosni i potvrditi njihovo postojanje. Njega je, uz to, zadesilo i to da ga zarobe srpski militanti i desetak dana zadrže u zaro-

Today’s hearing on Bosnia, however, is about a gruesome and solemn subject matter: namely, the atrocities against children, against women, and against men. These indescribable atrocities are the realities which warranted giving Bosnia such a place of prominence in our foreign policy.

While the international community deals with the formalities of exchanging territories among the warring parties and populations, the reality of Bosnia has been a land in which people—innocent civilians—have been raped, tortured, expelled from their homes, and executed by thugs. Documenting the war crimes, bringing the evidence before the War Crimes Tribunal, and pursuing justice through the tribunal are the foundation stones for reconciliation and a genuine peace.

The Helsinki Commission has an obligation, among other things, to document the violation of human rights. The role is vital, in and of itself. We owe it to the victims, and it is our hope that the attention given to the violations in Bosnia may deter future violations. Making the details a part of the official record of the Commission also helps those of us who must consider diplomatic and military responses to stay grounded in the reality of the situation that we are facing.

We have today three expert witnesses who have spent time in Bosnia and who have met the real victims of this war or their surviving family members and documented their almost incomprehensible ordeals. We will first hear from Ivan Lupis, a researcher from Human Rights Watch/ Helsinki, who will describe how and why the U.N.-declared safe haven of Srebrenica failed to deter militants and generally what happened thereafter.

We will then hear from David Rohde of the Christian Science Monitor, who was able to

PDF page 77

Masovne grobnice i drugi zločini u Bosni / Mass Graves and other Atrocities in Bosnia

bljeništvu. Gospodin Rohde, dakle, uz izravna iskustva sa žrtvama, ima i osobno iskustvo s počiniteljima zločina protiv čovječnosti.

Na kraju ćemo saslušati iskaz Barbare Wolf, forenzičke patologinje iz Albanyja u saveznoj državi New York, koja je posjetila mnoge lokacije masovnih grobnica u Bosni i susjednoj Hrvatskoj pod okriljem organizacije AmeriCares kako bi pomogla u identifikaciji posmrtnih ostataka. Novinski izvještaji o tome pothvatu upućuju na važnost ovoga rada za obitelji koje možda naslućuju, ali ne znaju stvarnu sudbinu svojih najmilijih, a posebice za mogući kazneni progon pred Međunarodnim sudom u Haagu onih koji su iskapali te grobnice te u njih zakapali žrtve.

Želim pozdraviti naše sudionike i svjedoke u Komisiji i zahvaliti im na značajnome radu pri dokumentiranju ovih gnjusnih zločina počinjenih nad civilima. Također bih, u ovome trenutku, želio ustupiti riječ svojemu vrlo dobromu prijatelju, gospodinu Hoyeru, vodećemu članu oporbe u Komisiji.

UVODNO OBRAĆANJE UVAŽENOGA STENYJA H. HOYERA

G. HOYER. Hvala Vam, predsjedniče Smith. Želim Vam zahvaliti na održavanju ovih saslušanja. Ona su vrlo važna i pravodobna. Pred nama je danas težak, ali nuždan zadatak: saslušati svjedočanstva o genocidu, masovnim grobnicama, silovanjima, pogubljenjima – neizrecivim i neprihvatljivim zločinima.

Posljednjih četiriju godina u Bosni svjedoci smo povratka europske noćne more za koju smo

visit the sites of the alleged mass graves in eastern Bosnia and confirm their existence. He also had the unfortunate experience of being caught by the Serb militants and held by them for about 10 days. Mr. Rohde, therefore, has not only firsthand experience with the victims, but also with the perpetrators of crimes against humanity.

Finally, we will have testimony presented by Barbara Wolf, a forensic pathologist from Albany, New York, who has visited many mass grave sites in Bosnia and neighboring Croatia under the auspices of AmeriCares in order to help identify the remains. Press reports about this effort indicate the importance of this work for those families who may suspect, but do not know, the actual fate of their loved ones, not to mention for the possible prosecution, at the International Tribunal in the Hague, of those who created and filled these graves with victims.

I want to welcome our panelists to the Commission and thank them for the great work that they have done in documenting these heinous acts which have been perpetrated against civilians. I would also, at this point, like to yield to my very good friend, the ranking member of the Commission, Mr. Hoyer.

OPENING STATEMENT OF HON. STENY H. HOYER

Mr. HOYER. Thank you very much, Chairman Smith. I want to thank you for having these hearings. They are very important and they are very timely. It is a sad task before us today, but one that must be undertaken: the examination of testimony about genocide, mass graves, rape, executions—unspeakable and unacceptable atrocities.

PDF page 78

svi mislili da je okončana prije pedeset godina. Pred nama je panel uglednih svjedoka koji je iz prve ruke svjedočio posljedicama neobuzdane etničke mržnje. I samoga su gospodina Rohdea zarobili bosansko-srpski militanti, jer se usudio svijetu obznaniti istinu o jezivim poljima smrti Srebrenice, gdje je pogubljeno gotovo 8.000 muslimanskih muškaraca po kratkome postupku pošto je ta dotad sigurna zona opkoljena.

Ta polja smrti, naravno, nisu bila ograničena na Srebrenicu i Žepu, nego su se nalazila diljem hrvatskoga i bosanskoga prostora što su ga militanti zauzeli. Dana 16. listopada 1995., u članku u novinama USA Today, podrobno je opisana ekshumacija masovne grobnice u nedavno oslobođenoj hrvatskoj regiji Krajini, gdje je dr. Wolf obavila značajan dio svojega rada, o čemu ćemo uskoro čuti u njezinu svjedočenju.

U članku se navodi da su se desetci članova obitelji okupili u mrtvačnici Kliničkoga bolničkog centra Split kako bi pokušali identificirati posmrtne ostatke svojih najmilijih, uključujući satove, raspela i dijelove odjeće pronađenih uz tijela. U članku stoji, citiram: „Ključ automobila marke BMW pronađen uz tijelo broj 28

Naslovnica međunarodnog izdanja USA Today s člankom „Mass graves yield horror in Bosnia”, 16. listopada 1995. godine.

Cover of the international edition of USA Today featuring the article “Mass graves yield horror in Bosnia,” 16 October 1995.

Naslovnica međunarodnog izdanja USA Today s člankom „Mass graves yield horror in Bosnia”, 16. listopada 1995. godine.

Cover of the international edition of USA Today featuring the article “Mass graves yield horror in Bosnia,” 16 October 1995.

The past 4 years in Bosnia have seen the recurrence of a European nightmare that we all thought had ended 50 years ago. We have before us a distinguished panel of witnesses, all who have seen firsthand the results of unbridled ethnic hatred. Mr. Rohde himself was captured by the Bosnian Serb militants for daring to bare to the world the gruesome killing fields of Srebrenica, where as many as possibly 8,000 Muslim men were summarily executed following the overrunning of that safe haven.

Those killing fields were not limited, of course, to Srebrenica and Zepa, but are found throughout Croatian and Bosnian territory overrun by the militants. On October 16, 1995, a USA Today article detailed the exhumation of a mass grave in the recently liberated Krajina region

Mr. Chairman, we as members of the international community, not just as Americans, not just as members of Congress who are this Commission, but as members of the international community, must recommit ourselves to that haunting phrase. We must redouble our efforts to ensure that the goal of justice before vengeance enshrined in Nuremberg is, in fact, achieved.

Justice before vengeance. Now, many of us traveled throughout Europe and heard about things that needed to be redressed that occurred 50 years ago, 100 years ago, and 200, 300, 400 years and centuries ago. But those who feel aggrieved saw their grievances never redressed. Therefore, the cycle of vengeance and terror and atrocities and killings go on.

We have witnessed the conclusion of the long-awaited and strenuously-achieved peace agreement among the parties to the conflict in the former Yugoslavia. Plans to implement that agreement are underway. However, Mr. Chairman, I’m convinced that a lasting resolution of this conflict requires breaking the cycle of violence and vengeance that has racked this region, not just in this century, but as I said, in centuries past.

This goal can only be achieved through the administration of justice by an impartial International Tribunal, which is already moving forward with its work. The United States, Mr. Chairman—and I hope our Commission is in the forefront of urging it to do so—must continue to take the lead in strongly supporting the efforts of the War Crimes Tribunal. ISKAZ IVANA LUPISA, ISTRAŽIVAČA, HUMAN RIGHTS WATCH/HELSINKI

G. LUPIS. Naravno. Hvala Vam što ste održali ovo saslušanje i pozvali me svjedočiti. Zovem se Ivan Lupis. Istraživač sam u helsinškome odjelu organizacije Human Rights Watch, donedavno poznatoj kao Helsinki Watch. Sljedeće svjedočenje temelji se na istrazi koju smo proveli jedan konzultant i ja od 31. srpnja do 23. kolovoza 1995.

U njoj opisujemo događaje koji su prethodili padu Srebrenice, koju je UN proglasio zaštićenom zonom, kao i događaje tijekom samoga pada i neposredno nakon njega. Ti događaji obuhvaćaju teška kršenja humanitarnoga prava, kakva su do tada bila uobičajena u srpskome vojnom djelovanju. Pad grada Srebrenice i njegove okolice u ruke srpskih snaga, početkom srpnja 1995. godine, posve je obesmislio deklariranu obvezu međunarodne zajednice da zaštiti područja koja je proglasila sigurnim zonama.

Dužnosnici mirovnih snaga UN-a nisu htjeli udovoljiti zahtjevima vlastitih snaga stacioniranih unutar enklave, čime su srpskim snagama omogućili da je lako zauzmu i, bez uplitanja vojnika UN-a, provedu sustavna masovna pogubljenja stotina, možda tisuća civila, muškaraca i dječaka, te da maltretiraju, siluju, tuku, pogube, opljačkaju i na druge načine zlostavljaju civile koje su protjerivali s tog područja.

Nedavni mirovni plan iz Daytona i suzdržani optimizam koji je pratio taj prividni napredak ne bi smjeli zasjeniti činjenicu da nijedan mirovni sporazum ne će biti stabilan bez traženja pravde za kršenja ljudskih prava. Zločini opisani u ovome svjedočenju, kao i mnogi drugi koji su im prethodili na prostoru bivše Jugoslavije, zahtijevaju od međunarodne zajednice, a posebno od Sjedinjenih Država, da nastave s identifikacijom i vraćanjem posmrtnih

STATEMENT OF IVAN LUPIS, RESEARCHER, HUMAN RIGHTS WATCH/HELSINKI

Mr. LUPIS. Sure. Thank you for holding this hearing and inviting me to testify. My name is Ivan Lupis. I am a researcher at the Helsinki Division of Human Rights Watch, formerly known as Helsinki Watch. The following testimony is based on an investigation carried out by myself and a consultant from July 31 to August 23, 1995.

It describes the events leading up to, during, and immediately after the fall of the U.N.-designated safe area of Srebrenica, including gross violations of humanitarian law, as has been typical of Serbian military conduct to date. The fall of the town of Srebrenica and its environs to Serb forces in early July 1995 made a mockery of the international community’s professed commitment to safeguard regions it declared to be safe areas.

U.N. peacekeeping officials were unwilling to heed requests for support from their own forces stationed within the enclave, thus allowing Serb forces easily to overrun it and, without interference from U.N. soldiers, to carry out systematic mass executions of hundreds, possibly thousands, of civilian men and boys, and to terrorize, rape, beat, execute, rob, and otherwise abuse civilians being deported from the area.

The recent Dayton peace plan and the guarded optimism that has accompanied this apparent progress should not obscure the fact that no peace agreement will be stable without justice for human rights abuses. The atrocities described in this testimony, like the many others that have preceded them in the former Yugoslavia, require of the international community, and specifically the United States, a commitment to repatriation for victims and accountability for the perpetrators.

PDF page 83

Masovne grobnice i drugi zločini u Bosni / Mass Graves and other Atrocities in Bosnia

ostataka obiteljima te pozivanjem počinitelja na odgovornost.

Prije rata, u općini Srebrenica živjelo je približno 37.000 ljudi, 72 posto Muslimana i 25 posto Srba. Kada su snage bosanskih Srba, uz pomoć Jugoslavenske narodne armije, započele svoju brutalnu kampanju etničkoga čišćenja u istočnoj Bosni 1992. godine, većina područja brzo je pala pod srpsku kontrolu. Glavnina nesrpskih muškaraca ili je pobjegla, ili je odvedena u zatočeničke logore, ili je neselektivno ubijena.

Tisuće, uglavnom muslimanskih, izbjeglica iz drugih dijelova istočne Bosne slijevale su se u mjesta poput Žepe, Goražda i Srebrenice, gdje su postrojbe teritorijalne obrane uspješno odbijale srpske napade. Kao posljedica toga nagloga demografskog pomaka, stanovništvo Srebrenice naraslo je na procijenjenih 55.000 do 60.000 ljudi i bilo pod opsadom više od tri godine.

Dana 16. travnja 1993., Vijeće sigurnosti UN-a usvojilo je Rezoluciju 819, kojom je Srebrenica proglašena zaštićenom (sigurnom) zonom, a 17. travnja potpisano je primirje. No, u srpnju 1995. godine brojne su naznake upućivale na to da snage bosanskih Srba planiraju ljetnu ofenzivu na Srebrenicu. Pristup konvojima UN-a sve su više ograničavale snage bosanskih Srba, tako da je krajem veljače i početkom ožujka 1995. godine bio dopušten ulazak samo jednog konvoja mjesečno, koji je opskrbljivao hranom oko 39.000 ljudi preostalih u enklavi. Jedan dužnosnik UN-a u Tuzli rekao nam je da je to bila namjerna taktika kojom su se Srbi koristili kako bi oslabili stanovništvo enklave i kako bi pripremili područje za konačnu ofenzivu. Srbi su im tijekom dugoga razdoblja postupno uskraćivali hranu, vodu, električnu energiju i odgovarajuće medicinske potrepštine, što je već tada trebalo prepoznati kao stvarnu pripremnu fazu srpanjskoga napada na enklavu te

Before the war, approximately 37,000 people, 72 percent Muslim and 25 percent Serbs, lived in the Srebrenica municipality. When Bosnian Serb forces began their brutal campaign of ethnic cleansing with the help of the Yugoslav People’s Army in eastern Bosnia in 1992, most areas quickly fell under Serb control. Most of the non-Serb men either fled, were put into detention centers, or were indiscriminately killed.

Thousands of mostly Muslim refugees from other areas of eastern Bosnia flocked to places like Zepa, Gorazde, and Srebrenica, where territorial defense units succeeded in fending off Serb attacks. As a result of this sudden demographic shift, Srebrenica’s population swelled to an estimated 55,000 to 60,000 people and remained under siege for more than 3 years.

On April 16, 1993, the U.N. Security Council adopted Resolution 819, declaring Srebrenica a safe area; and a cease-fire was signed on April 17. But in July 1995, there were numerous indications that Bosnian Serb forces were planning a summer offensive against Srebrenica. Access to U.N. convoys was increasingly restricted by Bosnian Serb forces, so that by late February and early March 1995, only one convoy per month was being allowed into the area to feed the approximately 39,000 people left in the enclave.

A U.N. official in Tuzla told us that this was a deliberate tactic used by the Serbs to weaken the population of the enclave in order to prepare the area for a final offensive. The incremental denial of food, water, electricity, and proper medical supplies by the Serbs over a long period of time should actually have been viewed as the true preparatory stages of the July assault on the enclave and should have served as a warning signal to the international community that the so-called U.N. safe area of Srebrenica was in danger.

PDF page 84

kao upozorenje međunarodnoj zajednici da je takozvana sigurna zona UN-a Srebrenica ugrožena.

Prije ofenzive, snage bosanskih Srba također su ometale djelotvornost mirovnih snaga, kao i rotacije postrojba u enklavi. Dopustile su odlazak dviju rotacija nizozemskih vojnika stacioniranih u enklavi, ali su bosanski Srbi odbili ulazak njihovih zamjena. Tako su čitava enklava, i civili i postrojbe UNPROFOR-a, bili psihološki i fizički iscrpljeni tjednima prije ofenzive.

Samo dva dana prije napada, snage bosanskih Srba dopustile su ulazak jednoga konvoja koji je prevozio 100.000 litara dizelskoga goriva, dotad neviđenu količinu, u enklavu. To su gorivo zatim ponovno zaplijenile srpske snage kada je sigurna zona pala. S obzirom na embargo koji su nametnuli bosanski Srbi, kao i na njihovo prethodno onemogućivanje dovoza goriva u enklavu, ovaj iznenadni priljev goriva trebao je biti sumnjiv nizozemskim vojnicima UN-a. Bez toga goriva, snage bosanskih Srba poslije ne bi mogle autobusima prevesti desetke tisuća Muslimana na teritorij pod kontrolom bosanske vlade.

Do 5. srpnja oko 5.000 srpskih vojnika opkolilo je enklavu s 50 komada topničkog naoružanja i 15 do 20 borbenih vozila, a u 3:15 ujutro, 6. srpnja, pokrenuli su opsežnu ofenzivu na Srebrenicu. Granatiranje je bilo toliko snažno da je bilo nemoguće prebrojiti detonacije, kojih je, prema procjeni UN-a, bilo na tisuće. Srpske snage počele su preuzimati nadzor nad promatračnicama UN-a, jednom po jednom, i do završetka ofenzive, 55 vojnika UN-a bilo je zarobljeno i uzeto za taoce.

Nizozemski vojnici unutar enklave zatražili su neposrednu zračnu potporu od zapovjednika UN-a, ali je datum zahtjeva i dalje sporan. Dužnosnici UN-a s kojima smo razgovarali poriču da su nizozemske postrojbe u sigurnoj zoni zatražile neposrednu zračnu potporu prije 10. srpnja. Međutim, drugi dokazi upućuju na to da su nizo-

Prior to the offensive, Bosnian Serb forces also hampered peacekeeping effectiveness as well as troop rotations into the enclave. Two rotations of Dutch troops stationed in the enclave had been allowed to leave, but the Bosnian Serbs refused their replacements’ entry. Thus, the entire pocket, civilians and UNPROFOR troops alike, were psychologically and physically exhausted weeks prior to the offensive.

Just 2 days before the attack, Bosnian Serb forces allowed 1 convoy carrying 100,000 liters of diesel fuel, an unprecedented amount, into the pocket. This fuel was then recaptured when the safe area fell. Given the embargo of the Bosnian Serbs, as well as their refusal to allow fuel into the enclave on previous occasions, this sudden influx of fuel should have been suspicious to the Dutch U.N. soldiers. Without the fuel, Bosnian Serb forces would not have been able later to bus tens of thousands of Muslims to Bosnian Government-controlled territory.

By July 5, approximately 5,000 Serb troops had surrounded the enclave with 50 artillery pieces and 15 to 20 battle vehicles and launched a full-scale offensive on Srebrenica at 3:15 a.m. on July 6. The shelling was too heavy to count the number of detonations, but U.N. estimates were in the thousands. Serb troops began taking control of U.N. observation posts one by one, and by the time the offensive was over, 55 U.N. troops had been taken hostage.

Dutch soldiers within the enclave requested close air support from the U.N. commanders, but the date of the request remains disputed. U.N. officials interviewed by us deny that Dutch troops in the safe area requested close air support before July 10. Other evidence, however, suggests that Dutch troops in the enclave acted sooner and believe that close air support might have dissuaded Serb forces from pressing their offensive.

Masovne grobnice i drugi zločini u Bosni / Mass Graves and other Atrocities in Bosnia

zemske postrojbe u enklavi djelovale ranije i vjerovale da je neposredna zračna potpora mogla odvratiti srpske snage od nastavka ofenzive.

Prema Nizozemcima, bliska zračna potpora zatražena je 6. srpnja. Taj je zahtjev, kao i naknadno ponovljene zahtjeve, više puta odbijao zapovjednik mirovnih snaga UN-a u bivšoj Jugoslaviji, Bernard Janvier. Dana 10. srpnja, prema procjenama UNHCR-a, oko 30.000 ljudi počelo je napuštati Srebrenicu i povlačiti se u sjeverni dio enklave prema bazi UN-a u Potočarima, selu smještenome na pola puta između Srebrenice i Bratunca.

Na koncu, 11. srpnja, dva dana nakon što su srpske snage prodrle kroz Srebrenicu, četiri su borbena zrakoplova sudjelovala u napadu, pri čemu je uništen jedan srpski tenk. Zapovjednik bosanskih Srba, Ratko Mladić, zaprijetio je da će pucati na nizozemski kompleks i civilno stanovništvo Srebrenice te pogubiti zarobljene nizozemske pripadnike mirovnih snaga ako se zračni napadi nastave.

Zračni se napadi nisu ponovili i nastojanja UN-a da spasi UN-ovu sigurnu zonu Srebrenicu prešla su u fazu saniranja štete. Sada, zbog vremenskog ograničenja, moram preskočiti ono što se dogodilo ženama, djeci i starcima u Potočarima i prijeći na masovne pokolje, jer je to središnja tema ovoga svjedočenja. Ako bude pitanja o tome što su Srbi učinili u kompleksu u Potočarima, ona se mogu postaviti naknadno.

Dok je Srebrenica padala, velika većina muškaraca i dječaka vojničke dobi, uz manji broj žena i djece, okupili su se na više odvojenih mjesta kako bi se probili kroz područja pod kontrolom bosanskih Srba i stigli na teritorij pod kontrolom bosanske vlade. Većinu od 12.000 do 15.000 osoba u toj koloni činili su civili. Muškarci i dječaci s kojima smo razgovarali izjavili su da je samo između 3.000 i 4.000 njih bilo naoružano. Nakon što UN nije uspio

According to the Dutch, close air support was requested on July 6. That request and subsequent ones were repeatedly turned down by the commander of U.N. peace forces in former Yugoslavia, Bernard Janvier. On July 10, according to UNHCR estimates, approximately 30,000 people began to evacuate Srebrenica and move back to the northern part of the enclave toward the U.N. base in Potocari, a village located halfway between Srebrenica and Bratunac.

Finally, on July 11, 2 days after Serbian forces had driven through Srebrenica, four fighter planes took part in an attack which resulted in the destruction of one Serb tank. Bosnian Serb commander Ratko Mladic threatened to fire on the Dutch compound and the civilian population of Srebrenica and to execute Dutch peacekeeping hostages if more air strikes were carried out.

The air strikes were not repeated and the U.N. effort to save the U.N. safe area of Srebrenica shifted to damage control. Now, due to my limited amount of time, I have to skip over what happened with the women, children, and elderly people in Potocari and move on to the massacres because this is the focus of this testimony. If there are any questions about what the Serbs had done in the Potocari compound, questions could be raised after.

As Srebrenica was falling, the overwhelming majority of military-aged men and boys and a smattering of women and children gathered in separate locations in order to make the journey through Bosnian Serb-held areas to reach Bosnian Government-controlled territory. The majority of the persons in this group of 12,000 to 15,000 trekkers were civilians. Men and boys interviewed by us stated that only between 3,000 to 4,000 of them were armed. After the U.N. failed to defend the safe area of Srebrenica, the enclave’s military-aged men no longer trusted the UNPROFOR troops, nor did they believe their safety would be guaranteed

obraniti sigurnu zonu - Srebrenicu, muškarci vojničke dobi iz enklave više nisu vjerovali postrojbama UNPROFOR-a, niti su vjerovali da će im one moći jamčiti sigurnost. Oblikovali su kolonu dugu otprilike deset kilometara te su se kretali u nedovoljno zaštićenoj formaciji, jer su upozoreni da je područje minirano.

Tijekom puta, kolona je bila izložena brojnim napadima i zasjedama srpskih snaga, tijekom kojih je dolazilo do teških kršenja humanitarnoga prava. Jedan prognanik kojega sam intervjuirao živopisno je opisao užasan put kroz koji su muškarci i dječaci prošli. Naveo je sljedeće:

„Nakon otprilike tri kilometra naišli smo na prvu zasjedu kraj potoka. Sredinu naše kolone pogodile su protuzračne strojnice i minobacači. Oko 200 ljudi poginulo je samo od toga. Četnici“ – izraz kojim se mnogi koriste za nacionalistički nastrojene Srbe – „su zatim sišli s brda i oko 2.000 muškaraca iz sredine kolone našlo se usred pucnjave. Ljudi sa čela i s kraja kolone razbježali su se posvuda. Bio sam u sredini i vidio kako Srbi pucaju na nas i kolju nas bajunetama.“

„Nadalje, tijekom noći i za vrijeme zasjeda, srpski su se vojnici u civilnoj odjeći uspjeli infiltrirati u kolonu, širiti dezinformacije i pomutnju, davati krive upute, ubrizgavati muškarcima tvari za koje se vjerovalo da imaju halucinogeno djelovanje, odvlačiti skupine i pojedince od kolone te otvarati vatru i pogubljivati ljude u njoj.“

Kako su zasjede i infiltrirani Srbi nastavili „otkidati” dijelove kolone, muškarci i dječaci očajnički su pokušavali ponovno zbiti redove nakon zasjede. Kolona se postupno sve više osipala, a manje su skupine zaostajale i odvajale se od ostatka kolone. Mnogi muškarci i dječaci predali su se, a nekoliko svjedoka reklo nam je da su vidjeli kako nenaoružane muškarce ubijaju dok su se predavali.

by them. They formed a column which stretched for approximately ten kilometers and walked in a vulnerable formation because they had been warned of a mined terrain.

During the trek, the column was exposed to numerous attacks and ambushes by Serbian forces, during which violations of humanitarian law were committed. A displaced person I interviewed vividly described the horrific ordeal which the men and boys experienced. He mentioned:

“After about three kilometers, we encountered our first ambush at a stream. The center of our column was hit by anti-aircraft machine guns and mortars. Around 200 people died just from that. The Cetniks”—which is a term used by many to describe nationalist Serbs—”then came down from the hills, and about 2,000 men from the middle of the column got caught in the line of fire. The people at the front and back of the column scattered everywhere. I was in the middle and saw how the Serbs were shooting everyone and slaughtering us with bayonets.

“Furthermore, during the nighttime and during the ambushes, Serb soldiers in civilian clothing managed to infiltrate the column, spreading disinformation and confusion, giving wrong directions, injecting men with what was believed to have been hallucinatory drugs, drawing groups and individuals away from the column, and opening fire on and executing people from within the column.”

As the ambushes and infiltrating Serbs continued to pick away at the column, men and boys tried desperately to regroup after the ambush. The column eventually became smaller and smaller in number, and smaller groups were left behind and separated from the rest. Many men and boys surrendered, and several witnesses told us that they saw unarmed men shot in the process of surrendering.

PDF page 87

Masovne grobnice i drugi zločini u Bosni / Mass Graves and other Atrocities in Bosnia

Razgovarali smo sa svjedokom pokolja u području Nova Kasaba/Konjević Polje i s još četvoricom ljudi koji su bili odvedeni na masovna pogubljenja na dvama mjestima u području Karakaja, grada sjeverno od Zvornika na granici između Bosne i Srbije. Masovna pogubljenja po kratkome postupku počinjena su i na najmanje dvjema lokacijama u području Bratunca, a dokazi upućuju i na postojanje dviju lokacija u području Kravice.

Sustavna priroda operacije, koja je već opisana u ofenzivi i napadu osmišljenome da razbije kolonu muškaraca u bijegu, može se dodatno nazrijeti tijekom faze hvatanja raspršenih bjegunaca. Srpske snage strateški su se rasporedile uz glavne ceste i rijeke koje su muškarci morali prijeći kako bi stigli na teritorij pod kontrolom bosanske vlade. Srpske su snage očito nastojale zarobiti što je moguće više muškaraca prije nego što uspiju prijeći, kako bi ih mogle zadržati u logorima oko Nove Kasabe i Konjević Polja.

Kako je opisano u našim izvješćima, srpske su snage megafonima muškarcima upućivale naredbe i upute o tome kako i gdje se mogu predati prije nego što stignu do dviju cesta. Prema riječima jednoga prognanika, na tome su području počinjeni veliki pokolji. Ispričao nam je sljedeće:

„Mjesto je bilo prepuno četnika pa smo se skrili u visokoj travi i čekali. Muslimani su odasvud silazili na glavnu cestu i predavali se. Četnici su izdvajali Muslimane za koje su znali ili koje su poznavali, ispitivali ih, a zatim ih tjerali da kopaju jame koje su potom služile kao masovne grobnice.”

„Prvoga dana dok smo bili tamo, četnici su ubili oko 500 ljudi. Samo bi ih postrojili, strijeljali i ubacili u jame. Oko stotinu muškaraca, nakon ispitivanja, prisilili su na to da iskopaju masovne grobnice te ih potom zatrpaju. Na kraju dana naredili su im da iskopaju jamu

We conducted interviews with a witness to a massacre in the Nova Kasaba/Konjevic Polje area and with four other persons who were sent to mass executions at two sites in the Karakaj area, a town north of Zvornik on the Bosnian-Serbian border. Mass summary executions were also carried out at least two locations in the Bratunac area, and evidence points to the existence of two sites in the Kravice area as well.

The systematic nature of the operation, already described in the offensive, and the attack designed to break up the escaping column of men can be further adumbrated during the round-up phase of the dispersed trekkers. Serb forces strategically positioned themselves along major roads and rivers over which the men would have to cross in order to reach Bosnian Government territory. Serb forces apparently tried to capture as many men as possible before they could cross so that they could be detained at sites around Nova Kasaba and Konjevic Polje.

As described in our reports, Serb forces communicated orders and instructions to the men by megaphones on how and where to surrender before they could reach the two roads. According to a displaced person, large massacres were carried out in this area. He recounted:

“The place was full of Cetniks so we hid in some high grass and waited. Muslims were coming down on the main road from everywhere giving themselves up. The Cetniks picked out Muslims who they either knew about or knew, interrogated them, and then made them dig pits which would be used as mass graves.

“During our first day there, the Cetniks killed approximately 500 people. They would just line them up and shoot them into the pits. The approximately 100 men whom they interrogated and who had dug the mass graves then had to fill them in. At the end of the day, they were ordered to dig a pit for themselves and line

PDF page 88

za sebe i postroje se ispred nje. Zatim su ih, strojnicama M53, strijeljali i bacili u masovne grobnice.“

„U zoru je i dalje sve bilo isto. Stigao je bager i iskopao jamu dugu oko 30 metara i široku oko 15 metara te su ondje žive zakopali oko 400 muškaraca. Muškarce su opkolili četnici. Tko god bi pokušao pobjeći, bio bi strijeljan. Nakon toga su poravnali i sabili zemlju, tako da je izgledala gotovo kao netaknuta.“

Na tome su području mnogi muškarci i dječaci navodili da su vidjeli zapovjednika vojske bosanskih Srba Ratka Mladića, koji je nadgledao dijelove operacije koja se ondje odvijala. Uz to, izvijestili su da su vidjeli srpske vojnike odjevene u UN-ove uniforme kako se voze u bijelim oklopnim vozilima UN-a.

Činjenica da su četvorica preživjelih bila zatočena na području Nove Kasabe/Konjević Polja i potom prošla kroz njega te zatim autobusima preko Bratunca prevezena u Karakaj dodatno potvrđuje da je kampanja koju su provele srpske snage bila sustavna.

Brojni stanovnici Bratunca i okolnih sela govorili su o nasilnim smrtima velikoga broja muškaraca iz Srebrenice. Priče mještana bile su dosljedne u mnogim pojedinostima, uključujući mjesto i način pogubljenja. Jedna je žena iz Srbije navodno rekla da je upravo bila posjetila svog šurjaka, koji je bio vojnik u snagama bosanskih Srba.

„On i njegovi prijatelji prilično otvoreno govore o tome što se događa“, uskliknula je žena. „Oni ubijaju muslimanske vojnike. Rekli su da su ih samo jučer ubili 1.600 i procijenili da su ukupno ubili oko 4.000. Rekli su da im se žuri pa većinu samo strijeljaju.“

U području Karakaja muškarcima su naredili da iziđu iz kamiona u skupinama od pet ili deset

up in front of it. Then with M53 machine guns, they were shot into the mass graves.

“At dawn, it was still the same. A bulldozer arrived and dug up a pit which seemed to be about 30 meters long and about 15 meters wide, and they buried about 400 men alive. The men were encircled by Cetniks. Whoever tried to escape was shot. After that, they packed down the earth so it almost looked as good as new.”

In this area, many men and boys described the appearance of Bosnian Serb General Ratko Mladic, who oversaw parts of the operation being carried out. Moreover, they reported seeing Serb soldiers dressed in U.N. garb driving around in white U.N. armored personnel carriers.

The fact that four of the survivors were detained in and transited through the Nova Kasaba/Konjevic Polje area and were then bused to Karakaj via Bratunac further suggests that the campaign carried out by the Serb forces was systematic in nature.

A number of citizens of Bratunac and its surrounding villages told about the violent deaths of a large number of men from Srebrenica. The villagers’ accounts were consistent in many details, including the place and the method of execution. One woman, a resident of Serbia proper, reportedly said that she had just been to visit her brother-in-law, who was a Bosnian Serb soldier.

“He and his friends are quite open about what is going on,” the woman exclaimed. “They are killing Muslim soldiers. They said they killed 1,600 yesterday alone and estimated in all they had killed about 4,000. They said they were in a big hurry so they were shooting most of them.”

In the Karakaj area, men were ordered to get out of the trucks in groups of five or ten and line up in front of the Serb soldiers who

PDF page 89

Masovne grobnice i drugi zločini u Bosni / Mass Graves and other Atrocities in Bosnia

i postroje se ispred srpskih vojnika, koji su potom otvorili vatru na njih. Četiri preživjela iznijela su pojedinosti koje upućuju na to da su masovna pogubljenja bila dobro isplanirana i sustavno provedena. Primjerice, svi su primijetili da su tijekom duljih razdoblja kamioni pristizali na lokacije i iskrcavali zatočenike. Streljački vodovi pogubili bi nekoliko skupina, a zatim bi dobili naredbu da prolaze među tijelima kako bi se uvjerili da su svi doista mrtvi. Prisutnost bagera, koji su mrtva tijela gurali na traktorske prikolice, upućivala je na to da su se srpske vlasti pripremile na to da će se pogubiti mnogo ljudi na tim mjestima.

Jedan od dvojice koji su preživjeli masovno pogubljenje na livadi ispričao je: „Bilo nas je 12 u malome kamionu. Vozili su nas oko dvije do tri minute i, kad je kamion stao, naredili su nam da iziđemo. Vidio sam travu ispod poveza na očima. Moj rođak Haris uzeo me za ruku. Rekao je: ‘Pogubit će nas.’ Čim je to izgovorio, začuo sam pucnjavu s desne strane. Haris je pogođen i pao je prema meni, a ja sam pao s njim.“

„Netko im je naređivao da nas pojedinačno dokrajče. Taj se proces nastavljao cijeli dan. Tijekom dana također sam čuo kako kamioni neprestano dolaze na drugo mjesto, otprilike 100 metara dalje, i nakon toga bi ubrzo uslijedila pucnjava. Morala su postojati dva stratišta jedno pokraj drugoga. Također sam čuo kako u pozadini radi bager i užasnuo se. Najviše sam se bojao da ću biti živ zakopan.“

„Neprestano sam slušao ljude kako zapomažu i traže vode da ne umru žedni. Drugi su neprestano ponavljali: ‘Ubijte me. Samo me dokrajčite.’ Kasnije sam se probudio. Nisam bio siguran jesam li se onesvijestio ili zaspao, a lagano je rosilo. Bila je noć i vidio sam snopove svjetla s farova bagera. Još sam uvijek čuo iste zvukove kao i prije – kamione koji su dolazili,

fired on them. Four survivors disclosed details which indicate that the mass executions were well-planned and systematically carried out. For example, all noted that for extended periods of time, trucks pulled up to the sites and dropped off loads of prisoners. Firing squads would execute several groups and were then ordered to walk among the corpses to make sure everyone was dead. The presence of bulldozers, which pushed the dead bodies onto tractor-trailers, indicated that the Serb authorities had prepared for a large number of persons to be executed at the sites.

One of two survivors of a mass execution carried out on a meadow recounted:

“There were 12 of us in a small truck. We were driven for about 2 to 3 minutes, and when the truck stopped, we were ordered to get out. I saw grass underneath my blindfold. My cousin, Haris, took my hand. He said, ‘They’re going to execute us.’ As soon as he said that, I heard gunfire from the right side. Haris was hit and fell toward me, and I fell with him.

“Someone was ordering them to finish us off individually. This process continued all day. During the day, I also heard trucks continuously driving up to another area about 100 meters away and gunshots which would follow shortly thereafter. There must have been two execution sites right next to each other. I also heard a bulldozer working in the background and became horrified. My worst nightmare was that I would be buried alive.

“I kept hearing people gasping and asking for water so they wouldn’t die thirsty. Others kept on repeating, ‘Kill me. Just finish me off.’ Later I woke up. I wasn’t sure whether I blacked out or fell asleep, and it was drizzling. It was nighttime, and I saw light beams from a bulldozer’s headlights. I still heard the same noises as before—trucks driv-

PDF page 90

ljude koji izlaze i pucnjavu. Također se jasno sjećam starijega glasa kako viče: ‘Nemojte nas ubiti. Nismo vam ništa učinili.’, nakon čega je uslijedila pucnjava.“

„Čekao sam oko četiri ili pet minuta nakon što su svi Srbi otišli da budem siguran kako to nije kakva varka. Kad sam napokon odlučio ustati, nisam mogao. Cijelo mi je tijelo bilo utrnulo. Trebalo mi je nekoliko minuta da se priberem, ali kad sam ustao, vidio sam leševe razbacane po livadi veličine oko 150 metara puta 100 metara. Odjednom sam čuo nekoga kako pita: ‘Jesi li ranjen?’ Odgovorio sam da nisam. Bio je to šezdesetogodišnji muškarac.“

„Pokušao sam doći do njega, a da ne stanem na mrtve. Bilo je nemoguće, pa sam barem pokušao ne stati na prsa i trup, nego na ruke i šake. Vidjeli smo još dvojicu ranjenih muškaraca, obojicu u tridesetim godinama. Oba su bila pogođena u noge, a jedan i u kuk. Provjerili smo mogu li se kretati i shvatili smo da im nikako ne možemo pomoći. I oni su to shvatili i rekli su nam da što brže pobjegnemo.“

„Prije nego što smo otišli, čovjek koji je bio ranjen u noge rekao mi je da mu je hladno i zamolio me da uzmem košulju ili nešto s jednoga od mrtvih tijela kako bi se mogao pokriti. Posljednje što sam čuo da su rekli bilo je: ‘Bježite, braćo, spasite se.’“

Napad srpskih snaga u srpnju 1995. godine na UN-ovu sigurnu zonu Srebrenicu bio je pomno isplaniran, a zločini počinjeni nakon pada enklave bili su sustavni i dobro organizirani. Prema UNHCR-u, gotovo 8.000 muškaraca, uključujući dječake od samo 12 godina, i dalje se smatra nestalima; za mnoge se vjeruje da su ubijeni ili pogubljeni.

Iako su države članice UN-a i dužnosnici UN-a bili spremni osuditi ratne zločine i zločine protiv čovječnosti na prostoru bivše Ju-

ing up, people getting out, and gunshots. I also remember distinctly an older voice calling, ‘Don’t kill us. We didn’t do anything to you,’ followed by gunfire.

“I waited for about 4 or 5 minutes after all the Serbs had left to make sure that it wasn’t some kind of trick. When I finally decided to get up, I couldn’t. My whole body was numb. It took me a few minutes to get adjusted, but when I got up, I saw corpses littering a meadow about 150 meters by 100 meters. Suddenly I heard someone ask, ‘Are you wounded?’ I answered that I wasn’t. It was a 60-year-old man.

“I tried to make my way over to him without stepping on the dead. It was impossible, so I tried at least not to step on the chest and torsos, but onto the arms and hands instead. We saw two other wounded men both in their thirties. They were both shot in the legs and one was shot in the hip. We checked to see if they could move, and they realized there was no way we could help them. They realized this, too, and told us to run away as quickly as possible.

“Before we left, the man who was wounded in the legs told me he was cold, and asked me to take a shirt or something off one of the dead bodies so that he could cover himself. The last thing I heard them say was, ‘Run, brothers, save yourselves.’ “

The July 1995 attack on the U.N.-declared safe area of Srebrenica by Serb forces was planned well in advance, and abuses perpetrated after the fall of the enclave were systematic and well-organized. According to the UNHCR, up to 8,000 men, including boys as young as 12 years old, remain missing; and many are believed to have been killed or executed.

Although the U.N. member states and U.N. officials have been ready to condemn war crimes and crimes against humanity in the former Yu-

PDF page 91

Masovne grobnice i drugi zločini u Bosni / Mass Graves and other Atrocities in Bosnia

goslavije, učinjeno je vrlo malo kako bi se spriječili ili zaustavili takvi zločini. Između kolovoza i listopada 1995. godine, dok su Sjedinjene Države vodile aktivne pregovore sa srpskim čelnikom Slobodanom Miloševićem, nastavljalo se sustavno etničko čišćenje nad desetcima tisuća nesrpskih stanovnika u sjeverozapadnoj Bosni.

Dvije tisuće muškaraca, civila koji nikada nisu sudjelovali u oružanome otporu, nestalo je dok su njihove obitelji protjerivane na teritorij pod kontrolom bosanske vlade. Brojni svjedoci izvijestili su da su na tome području vidjeli specijalne postrojbe iz Srbije, Arkanove snage. Arkan je ratni nadimak Željka Ražnatovića, osumnjičenoga ratnog zločinca iz Srbije.

Štoviše, prikupili smo i nekoliko svjedočanstava i fotografske dokaze koji upućuju na masovno pogubljenje oko 150 civila, počinjeno krajem rujna 1995. godine.

Organizacija Human Rights Watch/Helsinki želi iskoristiti ovu prigodu da pozove međunarodnu zajednicu, a posebno vladu SAD-a, da ustraje na tome da se međunarodnim organizacijama hitno omogući pristup svim zatočenicima iz sigurne zone Srebrenice i zatraži da se zajamče njihova sigurnost i dobrobit, te da inzistira da vlasti bosanskih Srba omoguće neposredni pristup mjestima prijavljenih masakara tijekom ofenzive na Srebrenicu. Sudbina nestalih i prisilno odvedenih osoba mora se razjasniti.

Nadalje, ako to bude relevantno, Sjedinjene Države i međunarodna zajednica moraju objaviti sve raspoložive informacije, uključujući obavještajne podatke, koje upućuju na umiješanost Srbije u opskrbu, pomaganje ili zapovijedanje postrojbama bosanskih Srba, te također ojačati mehanizme za praćenje vanjske potpore snagama bosanskih Srba.

goslavia, little has been done to prevent or stop such abuses from taking place. Between August and October 1995, while the United States carried out active negotiations with Serbian leader Slobodan Milosevic, systematic ethnic cleansing continued to be carried out against tens of thousands of non-Serbs in northwestern Bosnia.

Two thousand men, civilians who had never engaged in armed resistance, disappeared as their families were expelled into Bosnian Government-controlled territory. Numerous witnesses reported seeing Serbian-based special forces of Arkan operating in the area. Arkan is the nom-de-guerre of Zeljko Raznatovic, a suspected war criminal from Serbia.

Moreover, we also obtained several testimonies and photographic evidence pointing to a mass execution of approximately 150 civilians, which took place in the end of September 1995.

Human Rights Watch/Helsinki would like to use this opportunity to call on the international community, and especially the U.S. Government, to insist on immediate international access to all detainees from the Srebrenica safe area and demand that their safety and well-being are ensured, and insist that the Bosnian Serb authorities provide immediate access to the sites of reported massacres during the Srebrenica offensive. The fate of the missing and disappeared must be disclosed.

Furthermore, if relevant, the United States and the international community must disclose all available information, including the intelligence, that implicates Serbia in supplying, assisting, or directing Bosnian Serb troops, and also strengthen the mechanisms for monitoring external support to Bosnian Serb forces.

Finally, the international community and the United States must ensure that the Dayton peace

ISKAZ DAVIDA ROHDEA, DOPISNIKA CHRISTIAN SCIENCE MONITORA

G. ROHDE. Hvala vam što ste me pozvali. Želio bih na početku reći da sam ovdje samo kako bih iznio dokaze do kojih sam došao na terenu, a o političkim pitanjima ne ću raspravljati. Ovdje sam, u biti, samo kako bih iznio dokaze. Ovdje sam kako bih, kao novinar, prenio javne informacije, i to je jedino što želim.

Htio bih samo dati kratku izjavu, a zatim ću stati sa strane i prikazati različite vizuale. Tijekom tromjesečne istrage, organizacija Christian Science Monitor uspjela je posjetiti četiri od šest mogućih masovnih grobnica koje je američka obavještajna služba na području Srebrenice identificirala; uspjela je pronaći devet vjerodostojnih osoba koje su preživjele masovna pogubljenja; i pronašla je kombinirane dokaze – iskaze očevidaca i dokaze pronađene na samim mjestima događaja – koji upućuju na to da su srpske snage u oko tjedan dana nakon pada Srebrenice, 11. srpnja, po kratkome postupku pogubile najmanje 2.000 do 3.000 civila.

Dokazi upućuju i na to da je Ratko Mladić bio na najmanje četiri stratišta nekoliko sati prije nego što je pucnjava započela, a postoje i dokazi da su snage iz Srbije, premda nije bilo jasno jesu li redovne ili paravojne, sudjelovale u napadu. Prema mišljenju stručnjaka s kojima sam razgovarao u regiji, predsjednik Srbije Slobodan Milošević, s obzirom na razmjere ovoga napada, pa i same operacije pogubljenja, vjerojatno je znao da se ta pogubljenja odvijaju, ali očito nije učinio ništa da ih spriječi. Sada ću prijeći s one strane.

Predsjednik SMITH. Dok budete išli tamo, g. Rohde, želim Vam reći da smo gospodin Hoyer i ja jako zadovoljni što ste pušteni.

STATEMENT OF DAVID ROHDE, CORRESPONDENT, CHRISTIAN SCIENCE MONITOR

Mr. ROHDE. Thank you for inviting me here. I want to say at the start that I am just here to present the evidence I found at the sites; as far as policy matters go, I will not be discussing them. I’m just here basically to present evidence. I’m here as a journalist to present what’s public information, and that’s all.

I would just like to make a short statement, and then I’ll step over to the side to use the different visuals. Basically, over the course of a 3-month investigation, the Christian Science Monitor was able to visit four of six possible mass graves identified by U.S. intelligence around Srebrenica; was able to find nine credible survivors of mass executions; and found the combined evidence, those eyewitness accounts, and also the evidence that was found on the ground at these sites that indicates that at least 2,000 to 3,000 civilians were summarily executed by the Bosnian Serbs in around a week after the July 11 fall of Srebrenica.

The evidence also indicates that Ratko Mladic was present at least at four of the execution sites hours before the shooting began, and there’s also evidence that forces from Serbia— whether they were regular military or irregulars is unclear—were involved in the attack. According to experts I’ve spoken to in the region, President Slobodan Milosevic of Serbia, due to the size of this attack and even the size of the operation involved in the executions, probably knew that these executions were going on, but apparently did nothing to stop them. I just want to step over to the side now.

Chairman SMITH. While you’re walking over, Mr. Hoyer and I are very pleased that you were released.

G. ROHDE. I ja sam.

G. ROHDE. I ja sam, i želio bih zahvaliti Vladi Sjedinjenih Američkih Država na trudu koji je uložila da me izvuče. Zahvalan sam na tome. Nadam se da će dokazi koje sam pronašao dovesti do toga da se pravda u ovome slučaju u cijelosti zadovolji.

Započet ću kronološki i pokušati sve sažeti. Ako me želite prekinuti da biste postavili pitanja, slobodno to učinite. Dopušten mi je ulazak na teritorij bosanskih Srba radi redovitoga izvještavanja i, srećom, suprotno od uobičajenoga, nisam dobio pratnju. Obično morate imati vodiča bosanskoga Srbina koji putuje s vama i kontrolira kamo idete. Ja ga nisam imao i uspio sam doći do tih lokacija.

To je stara svjedodžba. Prvo mjesto na koje sam otišao bio je ovaj grob, odnosno ovo područje ovdje. Našao sam ova dva dokumenta ovdje s moje desne strane. Mr. ROHDE. So am I.

Chairman SMITH. As you know, this Commission is probably one of the most bipartisan groups in the House and acted in that way, as we always do, asking for your release. I’m just very happy that you’re out unscathed.

Mr. ROHDE. I am, too, and I’d like to thank the U.S. Government for the efforts they made to get me out. I appreciate it. Hopefully, the evidence I found will lead to justice being done in this whole issue.

I’ll just start chronologically and try to keep this short. If you want to interrupt me for questions, that’s fine. The first thing I did was in August. It was August 16. It was about 10 days after these two satellite photos were released by the U.S. Government. [See Exhibits 1 and 2.] I was allowed into Bosnian Serb territory to do regular reporting and luckily was not, as is usually the case, given an escort. You have to have a Bosnian Serb guide travel with you and control where you go. I didn’t have that, and I was able to go to these sites.

I first went to the lower photograph, and I was able to find these two documents here on the bottom right. [See Exhibit 3]. One of them is an elementary school diploma that belongs to a young man named Smajic Murat. It’s an old diploma. The first place I went was this grave, this area right here. I found these two documents here on my right. The first document I found was this piece of paper here, notes from a town meeting that was held inside the Srebrenica enclave. It’s dated here—you can make out the numbers—14.03.1995, which would be March 14, 1995. The meeting took place in Potocari, a village inside Srebrenica where the U.N. base was.

I later found people who thought they had attended this meeting. The town meeting was about how to get some retarded children some help
