Masovne grobnice i drugi zločini u Bosni / Mass Graves and other Atrocities in Bosnia

mjesne zajednice odnosio se na to kako nekolicini djece s mentalnim poteškoćama osigurati pomoć međunarodnih humanitarnih organizacija, kao i na zaštitu civila. Bilješke sa sastanka pronašao sam upravo na tom mjestu. Ova je jama bila iskopana, ali ne i zatrpana. Papir je ležao točno na rubu. Ne znam je li ga možda neka od žrtava izvadila iz džepa i odbacila. To mi nije jasno.

Dalje niz padinu, bliže velikomu području svježe iskopane zemlje, pronašao sam svjedodžbu koja je pripadala Muratu Smajiću. Zatim sam uspio otići u izbjegličke kampove i, nažalost, ondje pronaći brata Smajića Murata. Zapravo je riječ o Muratu Smajiću; ime i prezime ondje su navedeni obrnutim redoslijedom. Njegov brat rekao mi je da su on, Murat Smajić, njihov drugi brat i otac zajedno hodali sa skupinom od 10.000 vojno sposobnih muškaraca o kojoj je govorio gospodin Lupis.

Došlo je do zasjede i tada su se razdvojili. U ovom trenutku Murat Smajić još se uvijek smatra nestalim. Članovi njegove obitelji pretpostavljali su da je mrtav. Ponovno ističem, važnost dokaza jest u tome što povezuje Muslimane iz Srebrenice s tim lokacijama.

Još sam nešto ondje pronašao. Ove donje grobnice udaljene su oko 100 do 200 metara od tog područja, a hodajući tim prostorom pronašao sam raspadnutu ljudsku nogu kako viri iz tla. To sam pronašao.

Ključna stvar za ovo mjesto jest veličina tih grobnica. Opisi koje su dali preživjeli, osobito čovjek s kojim je razgovarao Human Rights Watch, svi se podudaraju s veličinom tih jama. To je bilo uz asfaltiranu cestu, upravo ovdje.

Ta je cesta bila Muslimanima ključna za prijelaz, ako su htjeli stići do područja središnje Bosne pod kontrolom muslimanske vlade, i čini se da su upravo ovdje snage bosanskih Srba us-

from international aid agencies and about civil defense. I found the notes right here. This was a pit that had been dug but not filled in. The paper was sitting right on the edge. I don’t know if possibly one of the victims maybe threw it out of his pocket. It’s not clear to me.

Farther down here closer to the large area of fresh digging, I found the diploma, again, that belonged to Murat Smajic. I was able then to go to refugee camps and find, unfortunately, Smajic Murat’s brother. It’s actually Murat Smajic. The names are reversed there. His brother told me that he, Murat Smajic, his brother, and father had all been walking together among this group of 10,000 military-aged men that Mr. Lupis talked about.

There was an ambush, and they were separated. At this point, Murat Smajic is still missing. The reaction of his family members was that they assumed he was dead. Again, the importance of the evidence is that it linked Muslims from Srebrenica to these sites.

There was one other thing I found here. These bottom graves are about 100 to 200 yards from this area, and walking in this area, I found here, sticking out of this area, was a decomposed human leg. I found that.

The key thing about this site is the size of these graves. The descriptions given by the survivors, especially the man that Human Rights Watch spoke to, all fit the size of these pits. This was along this paved road right here.

The road was crucial for Muslims to cross if they wanted to make it to Muslim-held central Bosnia, and it seems it was here that the Bosnian Serb forces set up. They had APCs patrolling this road and another road in the area, and they set up basically a killing ground where any Muslims trying to cross this area would be caught and rounded up.

postavile svoje položaje. Njihova su oklopna vozila patrolirala ovom cestom i drugom cestom na tom području, te su uspostavili, u biti, polje smrti na kojemu bi sve Muslimane koji bi pokušali prijeći taj prostor uhvatili i opkolili.

I još jedan ključan dokaz: postojala je još jedna snimka, koju Sjedinjene Države nisu javno objavile, nogometnoga igrališta koje je bilo oko pola kilometra dalje. Otišao sam na to igralište. Dvojica su muškaraca, koji su preživjeli pogubljenje na drugim lokacijama, dovedena na to igralište. Oni su, a da im ja nisam rekao, točno opisali svaki detalj toga igrališta. Nimalo ne sumnjam da su ondje bili. Sve, od veličine igrališta, zgrada oko njega, do vrste drveća koje ga okružuje, ne ostavlja nikakvu sumnju da govore istinu.

Ispričali su mi da se Ratko Mladić obratio skupini od približno 1.000 do 2.000 zarobljenika na tom igralištu i rekao im da im se ništa neće dogoditi, da će biti razmijenjeni za zarobljenike i vraćeni svojim obiteljima. Ti su ljudi potom odvedeni. Pogledajte, ovdje je Srebrenica.

Ovo je područje o kojem sam govorio, ono što biste mogli nazvati poljem smrti. Ključno je bilo to da je svatko tko je htio stići na teritorij pod kontrolom Vlade morao proći kroz to područje, a glavna kolona muškaraca kretala se upravo ovuda. Postoje i drugi preživjeli s kojima sam razgovarao i koji su govorili o pokoljima na ovome području. Na ovome području postoje i druge grobnice. Ovdje i ovdje nalaze se grobnice koje je identificirala američka obavještajna služba, a ovo su one dvije koje sam posjetio u kolovozu.

Ljudi koji su bili okupljeni na tome nogometnom igralištu zatim su, kako je i gospodin Lupis spomenuo, autobusima prevezeni u Bratunac i ondje zadržani preko noći. To je važno jer upućuje na izravnu namjeru. Ondje su zadržani preko noći, a zatim su odvezeni dalje na sjever, ako možete vidjeti manju kartu, na područje Karakaja.

And one last crucial bit of evidence: there was another photograph that the United States did not release publicly of a soccer field that was about a half-mile away. I went to that soccer field. There are two men who were survivors of other executions that were taken to the soccer field. They, without my telling them, accurately described every detail of this field. There’s no doubt in my mind they were there. Everything from the size of the field, the buildings around it, to the kind of trees that surround it— there’s no question in my mind that they’re telling the truth.

What they told me was that Ratko Mladic addressed as many as 1,000 to 2,000 prisoners at that field and told them that they would not be harmed, that they were going to be exchanged for prisoners, and they’d be returned to their families. Those men were then taken away—here’s Srebrenica.

This is the area I was talking about, what you could call the killing ground. It was just crucial that anyone who wanted to make it into government territory had to cross through this area, and the main column of men moved up through here. There are other survivors I spoke to who talked of massacres in this area. In this area, there are other graves. There’s a grave here and another grave here that have been identified by U.S. intelligence, and again, these are the two that I visited in August.

The men that were gathered at this soccer field were then taken—and Mr. Lupis referred to this— to Bratunac by bus and held there overnight. This is important because it indicates premeditation. They were then held there overnight and then taken farther north, if you can see the smaller map, up to the Karakaj area.

Once they arrived in Karakaj, they said they were held in a school in the Karakaj area. I visited the Nova Kasaba site in August. I was

able to find nine survivors in September, and then on October 29 went back and was able to get to the Karakaj area, which appears to be one of the larger sites. This is where the men were taken. It was the evening of Friday, July 14, according to the survivors, when the executions were carried out at two sites there.

I was able to visit both sites around Karakaj and found evidence of executions at both places. The most damning evidence was near a village called Sahanici. Everything about this site fits the description that survivors gave. The account that Mr. Lupis read—the young man who talked about two execution sites, firing going on about 100 yards away, and digging going on at both sites—well, I found two areas here and here of fresh digging. This is just a graphic that the Monitor put together.

The survivors all described driving 2 or 3 minutes. Two or 3 minutes away, I found a school that exactly fit the descriptions of the survivors. I actually have a sketch that one of them drew for me of the layout of the school. This is a notebook I used at the time. I found this school. It’s a distinctive school in that it has a room added to the side and a concrete playground just outside of it.

The prisoners were taken to that room and blindfolded there and then loaded onto trucks in the parking lot. There’s a house next to it where they described a woman watching them. This is also the school where Ratko Mladic was seen addressing the troops. A young boy actually watched me take pictures of this school, the same house, and later on, when my film was developed by the Bosnian Serb police, they told me that this school was a military installation and that I should not have been taking pictures of it. It appeared to be beat up and not being used. Again, the fact they say themselves it’s a military installation adds credibility to the survivors’ accounts.

trošno i nekorišteno. A opet, činjenica da oni sami kažu da je to vojni objekt povećava vjerodostojnost iskaza preživjelih.

Jedan od preživjelih otamo rekao mi je i to da su muškarci koji su ondje dovedeni bili stariji muškarci koji su od UN-a u Srebrenici zatražili pomoć, ali su ih umjesto toga odveli vojnici bosanskih Srba. UN-ove mirovne snage nisu učinile ništa da ih zaustave.

Na tome području, sa svježim iskopima, pronašao sam cipele, čarape i raznu civilnu odjeću razasutu naokolo. Pruga razdvaja ta dva mjesta. Preživjeli su opisali da se lokacija nalazi uz prugu. A najčvršći dokaz koji sam pronašao nalazio se ovdje u šumi, oko 15 metara dalje, hrpa odjeće. Ondje sam pronašao najmanje 100 civilnih jakni.

U džepovima jakni pronašao sam dvije osobne iskaznice. Jedna je imala muslimansko ime, a jedna je bila iz Srebrenice. Mislim da su tri štapa i jedna štaka koje sam ondje pronašao bili najjeziviji dokaz, jer potvrđuju iskaz preživjelog koji je rekao da je tamo doveden zajedno sa starcima iz Potočara. Taj čovjek jedva hoda. Teško je ozlijedio nogu u nesreći.

Također, preživjeli su opisali kako su bili prisiljeni skinuti jakne prije pogubljenja u školi. Sve zajedno, to stvara jezivu sliku. Postojali su tragovi teških vozila koja su se više puta kretala tim područjem. Jednostavno, sve u svezi s tom lokacijom savršeno odgovara onomu što su mi svjedoci opisali. Nadalje, Ratko Mladić, zapovjednik vojske bosanskih Srba, prema iskazima trojice preživjelih koji su mi sve točno opisali, viđen je u toj školi. Obratio se zarobljenicima i rekao im da im se ništa ne će dogoditi. Njegov se automobil zaustavio upravo ovdje, blizu željezničke pruge, i on je gledao kako se pogubljenja izvršavaju. Još jednom, sve ostalo što su mi ti preživjeli rekli savršeno se poklapa s onim što sam pro-

One of the survivors from there also told me that the men who were taken here were the elderly men who went to the U.N. in Srebrenica and asked for help, but were instead marched away by the Bosnian Serb soldiers. The U.N.— the peacekeepers—did nothing to stop them.

In this area, along with the fresh digging, I found shoes, socks, and different civilian clothes spread across the areas of fresh digging. There’s a railroad track separating the two. The survivors described its being adjacent to a railroad track. And the most damning evidence I found was here in the woods, about 50 feet away. It was a pile of clothing. I found at least 100 civilian jackets there.

Inside the pockets of the jackets I found two IDs. One had a Muslim name on it, and one was from Srebrenica. I think the most chilling evidence was in that pile: I also found three canes and one crutch, and that corroborates the account of a survivor who said he was taken there along with the old men from Potocari. This is a man who can barely walk. He badly damaged his leg in an accident.

Also, survivors described being forced to take their jackets off before they were executed at the school. So all together, it paints a grim picture. There were tracks of heavy vehicles repeatedly coming up to the area. Just everything about this site exactly fits what the describers told me. Again, Ratko Mladic, the Bosnian Serb military commander, was seen at this school by these three survivors who described everything to me accurately. He spoke to prisoners and told them they would not be harmed. His car pulled up right here near the railroad tracks, and he watched these executions carried out. Again, everything else these survivors told me matched perfectly to what I found. I have a couple of other things, and then I will just sit back down.

našao. Još nešto moram napomenuti, a zatim ću se vratiti na svoje mjesto.

Oprostite, zaboravio sam još nešto spomenuti, a to je da sam uspio napraviti fotografije, ali su mi film oduzeli. Fotografirao sam to područje, masovnu grobnicu ondje. Ona je dovoljno velika. Prema riječima analitičara američke obavještajne službe, dvije grobnice u Novoj Kasabi dovoljno su velike da u njih stane oko 600 tijela.

Grobnica koju sam pronašao u blizini Karakaja bila je nešto veća. Procjenjujem da u nju može stati oko 800 tijela, a uspio sam iznijeti ove predmete koje sam pronašao u džepovima jakni. Svojim zarobiteljima, bosanskim Srbima, rekao sam da su to moji rupčići i češljevi. Slobodno pogledajte ove predmete. Nažalost, nisu osobito snažni dokazi, ali bilo je to jedino što sam uspio iznijeti. Sve ostalo su mi oduzeli.

Postoji još samo nekoliko posljednjih točaka koje sam želio iznijeti. Postoje dokazi da bosanski Srbi iskapaju barem jednu od šest lokacija za koje Sjedinjene Države znaju, a na kojoj nisam bio. Američka obavještajna služba to je objavila prošloga mjeseca. Imaju zračne snimke rovokopača koji se nalaze na tom području i iskapaju te vade neku vrstu materijala, moguće tijela, a moguće je i da bosanski Srbi polijevaju tijela kiselinom i uništavaju dokaze.

Prema važećemu mirovnom sporazumu, istražitelji za ljudska prava, a posebice istražitelji Suda za ratne zločine, imaju pristup svim tim područjima, a američke snage imaju pravo uporabiti silu kako bi ušli na ta područja. Još bih nešto želio reći o tome kako mi je bilo kad su me bosanski Srbi zarobili: neki od mojih srpskih čuvara bili su prema meni vrlo ljubazni, neki nisu. Jednu mi noć nisu dopustili da spavam i bili su uvjereni da sam špijun. Drugi su bili vrlo susretljivi.

I’m sorry. One thing I forgot to mention was that I was able to get pictures, but my film was seized. I took pictures of that area, the mass grave there. It appears large enough. According to U.S. intelligence analysts, the two graves at Nova Kasaba are large enough to hold approximately 600 bodies.

The grave I found near Karakaj was slightly larger. I would guess it can hold 800 bodies, and I was able to carry out these items I found in the jackets. I told my Bosnian Serb captors that these were my handkerchiefs and combs. You’re free to look at these. Unfortunately, they’re not very damning evidence, but it was all I was able to bring out. They took everything else from me.

There are just a couple last points I wanted to make. There is evidence that at least one of the six sites that the United States knows about is one I have not visited, that the Bosnian Serbs are digging it up. U.S. intelligence said this last month. They have aerial photos of backhoes being in the area digging it up, taking out some kind of material, which could be bodies, and there’s a possibility the Bosnian Serbs are pouring acid onto the bodies and destroying evidence.

According to the current peace deal, human rights investigators and especially investigators from the War Crimes Tribunal have access to all these areas, and U.S. troops have the right to use force to go into these areas. One last thing I’d like to say about being held by the Bosnian Serbs: some of my Serb captors were very kind to me, some were not. One night I was not allowed to sleep, and they were convinced I was a spy. Others were very supportive.

It was very clear to me from the prisoners and the guards I talked to that most Serbs don’t believe that these massacres happened. They believe the line of the Bosnian Serb authori-

PDF page 100

Bilo mi je vrlo jasno iz razgovora sa zatvorenicima i čuvarima da većina Srba ne vjeruje da su se ti pokolji dogodili. Oni vjeruju verziji vlasti bosanskih Srba da je riječ o vojnicima koji su poginuli. Nisam vidio nikakve dokaze da su se na tim područjima vodile borbe, a osobito zato što je lokacija u Karakaju udaljena 15 do 25 km od glavnoga pravca bijega Muslimana iz Srebrenice. Stoga, ne postoji nikakvo objašnjenje za postojanje tih grobnica na tom području.

Mislim da je ključno dokazati što se ondje dogodilo, na ovaj ili onaj način. Bosanski Srbi možda su u pravu i možda se doista radi o vojnicima, ali dokazi upućuju na suprotno. Mirovni sporazum predviđa jamstva da se mogu provoditi daljnje istrage i nadam se da će dokazi koje sam pronašao dovesti do toga da točno saznamo što se dogodilo u Srebrenici.

Predsjednik SMITH. Gospodine Rohde, hvala Vam puno na Vašemu svjedočenju i iznimnoj hrabrosti da odete na ta područja, prikupite te informacije i zatim ih objavite uz veliki rizik za vlastiti život, što se jasno potvrdilo kada su vas uhitili.

Želio bih samo zabilježiti da se našem panelu pridružio gospodin Zeliff; kolega iz Komisije, gospodin Porter; predsjednik Odbora za međunarodne odnose Ben Gilma; i gospodin Moran. Zamolio bih našu treću i posljednju svjedokinju, dr. Wolf, da iznese svoje svjedočenje prije nego što prijeđemo na pitanja.

ties that these are soldiers that were killed. Again, I saw no evidence of any fighting going on in these areas, and especially the site in Karakaj is 10 to 15 miles from the main escape route Muslims would have used from Srebrenica. So there’s no explanation for these graves existing in that area.

I think it’s crucial that what happened there be proven one way or another. The Bosnian Serbs may be right, and these may be soldiers, but the evidence indicates otherwise. With the peace agreement, there are guarantees that more investigations can go on, and I hope that the evidence I found will hopefully lead us to find out exactly what happened in Srebrenica.

Chairman SMITH. Mr. Rohde, thank you very much for your testimony and for your obvious bravery in going out to those areas, recovering that information, and then making it known at great risk to yourself, as the situation certainly proved when you were arrested.

I’d like just to note that Mr. Zeliff has joined our panel; fellow Commissioner Mr. Porter; chairman of the International Relations Committee Ben Gilman; and Mr. Moran. I’d like to ask our third and final witness, Dr. Wolf, if she would present her testimony before we go to questions.

ISKAZ DR. BARBARE C. WOLF, FORENZIČKE PATOLOGINJE

Dr. WOLF. Hvala Vam, gospodine Smith. Svakako je čast što ste me pozvali da danas budem ovdje s vama i da s vama podijelim opažanja koja smo moji kolege i ja zabilježili kada smo prije dva mjeseca posjetili određene gradove u Bosni i Hrvatskoj. Točnije, želim s vama podijeliti naše iskustvo rada s civilnim liječnicima iz tih zemalja na lokacijama niza masovnih grobnica.

Mislim da sada možemo malo prigušiti svjetla. Da, to je odlično. Hvala Vam.

Između 8. i 13. listopada 1995. godine pridružila sam se skupini američkih liječnika-znanstvenika na putovanju u Hrvatsku i Bosnu pod pokroviteljstvom organizacije AmeriCares. AmeriCares je međunarodna humanitarna organizacija sa sjedištem u New Canaanu, u saveznoj državi Connecticut. To je privatna, neprofitna organizacija za pomoć u katastrofama koja pruža trenutačan odgovor na hitne medicinske potrebe, a također podupire dugoročne zdravstvene programe diljem svijeta.

Skupina s kojom sam putovala sastojala se od nekoliko drugih forenzičkih stručnjaka: dr. Michaela Badena, suvoditelja Jedinice za forenzičke znanosti Državne policije New Yorka; dr. Henryja Leeja, glavnoga kriminalista Državne policije Connecticuta; dr. Mosesa Schanfielda, stručnjaka za DNK iz Denvera; i dr. Davida Rowea, profesora pedijatrije sa Sveučilišta u Connecticutu.

Kupres, Bosna i Hercegovina, 1995. godine (foto: Phil Farnsworth, uz dopuštenje).

Kupres, Bosnia and Herzegovina, 1995 (photo: Phil Farnsworth, used with permission).

STATEMENT OF DR. BARBARA C. WOLF, FORENSIC PATHOLOGIST

Dr. WOLF. Thank you, Mr. Smith. It’s certainly an honor to be asked to be here with you today and to share with you the observations of my colleagues and myself when we visited certain towns in Bosnia and Croatia 2 months ago. More specifically, I want to share with you our experience in working with our civilian physician colleagues from those nations at the sites of a series of mass human graves.

I think we can probably put the lights down a little bit. Yes, that’s great. Thank you.

Between October 8 and 13, 1995, I joined a group of American physician-scientists on a trip to Croatia and Bosnia under the sponsorship of AmeriCares. [See Slide 1.] AmeriCares is an international humanitarian aid group based in New Canaan, Connecticut. It is a private, non-profit disaster relief organization providing immediate response to emergency medical needs, and also supporting long-term health care programs around the world.

The group with whom I traveled consisted of several other forensic specialists: Dr. Michael Baden, the co-director of the Forensic Sciences Unit of the New York State Police; Dr. Henry Lee, the chief criminalist for the Connecticut State

PDF page 102

Otišli smo u Hrvatsku i Bosnu kako bismo surađivali s forenzičkim timom Kliničke bolnice Split u Splitu i pružili im potporu. Dok smo bili ondje, pomagali smo pri ekshumaciji tijela iz masovnih grobnica, sudjelovali u obdukcijama te, potom, s hrvatskim forenzičkim timom radili na susretima s obiteljima kako bismo im pomogli u identificiranju tijelâ.

Također smo radili s hrvatskim znanstvenicima u njihovu DNK laboratoriju, koji su osnovali prije godinu dana, uz određene upute dr. Leeja, kako bi se bavili slučajevima u kojima se ne identifikacija ne može provesti tradicionalnim metodama.

Posjetili smo tri novootkrivene masovne grobnice koje su sadržavale tijela Hrvata ubijenih 1991. i 1992. godine. Dvije grobnice, jedna s 34 tijela, a druga s tri, nalazile su se u blizini Kupresa u Bosni. Treća masovna grobnica koju smo posjetili bila je u Petrinji, u Hrvatskoj, udaljenoj oko dva sata helikopterskoga leta od Kupresa. Svrha toga posjeta bila je pomoći u identifikaciji tijela pronađenih u masovnim grobnicama koje se otkrivaju na područjima koja su prije bila pod kontrolom suprotstavljenih vojski.

Naš cilj bio je identificirati mrtve i time pomoći živima u zacjeljivanju rana. U mnogim slučajevima radilo se o obiteljima koje su tijekom proteklih dviju ili triju godina gajile nadu da su njihovi najmiliji možda još uvijek živi. Nekima je rečeno da su im članovi obitelji odvedeni kao zarobljenici. Naš je zadatak bio pomoći u identifikaciji tih tijela kako bismo obiteljima omogućili neku vrstu zaključka, zatvaranja toga poglavlja.

Nije mi danas cilj, dijeljenjem ovoga iskustva, komentirati ni iznositi mišljenja o mirovnom procesu. Isto tako, nisam ovdje da tražim kazne za bilo koga ni da utvrđujem krivnju za ove smrti. To nije bio cilj putovanja. Nismo pregledavali ta tijela radi dokaza o mučenju, pa čak ni radi utvrđivanja točnoga uzroka smrti. To je

Police; Dr. Moses Schanfield, a DNA specialist from Denver; and Dr. David Rowe, a Professor of Pediatrics from the University of Connecticut.

We went to Croatia and Bosnia to work with and lend our support to the forensic team from the Split Clinical Hospital in Split, Croatia. While there, we assisted in the excavation of bodies at mass graves, assisted in autopsies, and then worked with the Croatian forensic team in meeting with families to assist them in identifying the bodies.

We also worked with the Croatian scientists in their DNA laboratory, which they had set up a year ago with some guidance from Dr. Lee to deal with those cases that could not be identified by traditional means. [See Slide 2.]

We visited three newly-found mass graves which contained the bodies of Croatians killed in 1991 and 1992. Two of the graves, one containing 34 bodies and the other, 3, were located near Kupres, Bosnia. The third mass grave we visited was in Petrinja, Croatia, a 2-hour helicopter flight from Kupres. The purpose of this visit was to assist in the identification of those bodies found in the mass graves being uncovered in areas previously controlled by opposing armies.

Our goal was to identify the dead and thereby help heal the living. In many instances, these were families who, for the past 2 or 3 years, had been holding out the hope that their loved ones might still be alive. Some had been told that their relatives had been taken prisoner. Our task was to help identify these bodies, to allow the families some kind of closure.

My purpose in sharing these experiences today is not to make any comments or give opinions regarding the peace process. Likewise, I’m not here to seek penalties for anyone or to assess blame for these deaths. This was not the goal of the trip. We did not examine these bodies for

PDF page 103

Masovne grobnice i drugi zločini u Bosni / Mass Graves and other Atrocities in Bosnia

bila politička odluka donesena prije našeg putovanja. Naš je posao bio isključivo raditi na identifikaciji tih tijela i vratiti ih njihovim obiteljima radi dostojnoga ukopa.

Ako zvučim kao da me ovo iskustvo nije potreslo, dopustite mi kazati da, unatoč desetljeću rada u forenzičkoj patologiji i suočavanja sa smrću, nisam bila spremna na mnoge prizore. U jednome je slučaju u jednoj od masovnih grobnica bilo tijelo 82-godišnje slijepe žene. Također, pomagao nam je vojnik koji je, dok je iskapao jednu od grobnica, pronašao tijelo svoga nestaloga brata, civila.

Cijelo je putovanje bilo vrlo dojmljivo, i jedno je od onih koje, mislim, većina ljudi u ovoj zemlji ne bi mogla istinski shvatiti ni razumjeti, niti bi itko tko nikada nije proživio iskustvo rata to mogao i zamisliti. Moj je cilj danas prikazati kliničku percepciju problema ljudi u Bosni i Hrvatskoj u poslijeratnom razdoblju.

Mirovni proces zapravo je tek početak dijela posla koji predstoji za ove ljude. U ovome trenutku još uvijek postoje tisuće nestalih vojnika i civila. Kako mir bude dolazio u regiju, otkrivat će se sve više i više grobnica. Vrlo je vjerojatno da će se ovaj proces identifikacije nastaviti u narednim godinama, kao što i danas identificiramo vojnike iz vremena Vijetnama. Sam broj nestalih i nedostatak resursa znače da je pred tim procesom još dug put.

Sada bih vam željela pokazati neke prizore s tih posjeta. Ova je fotografija snimljena prvoga dana putovanja dok smo se približavali Kupresu, gradu u jugozapadnoj Bosni koji su 1992. godine Srbi osvojili. Prve linije bojišnice nedavno su potisnute s toga područja i otkrivene su brojne masovne grobnice.

Jedna od grobnica s 34 tijela ekshumirana je tjedan dana prije našeg dolaska. Koliko razumijem iz medijskih izvješća, tih 34 ljudi bilo je

evidence of torture or even for specific causes of death. This was a policy decision made prior to our journey. Our job was purely to work toward the identification of these bodies and to return them to their families for proper burial.

In case I sound unmoved by this experience, let me say that despite a decade in forensic pathology and dealing with death, there were many sights that I was unprepared for. In one instance, there was the body of an 82-year-old blind woman in one of the mass graves. Also, we were being assisted by a soldier who, while excavating one of the graves, had found the body of his missing civilian brother.

The entire trip was a very moving one, and one that I think certainly most people in this country could not truly comprehend or understand, and that one who has never lived through the experience of war could envision. My purpose today is to give a clinical perception of the problems of the people of Bosnia and Croatia in the aftermath of war.

The peace process is really just a beginning of some of the work to be done for these people. At this point, there are many thousands of soldiers and civilians still missing. As peace arrives in the region, more and more of these graves will be found. It is quite probable that this identification will be going on for many years, as we are still identifying soldiers from the Vietnam era. The sheer number of the missing and the lack of resources dictates a long road for this process. [See Slide 3.]

I’d now like to show you some of the sights from these visits. This shot was taken on the first day of the trip as we approached Kupres, Bosnia, which is a town in southwestern Bosnia that had been captured by the Serbs from the Croatians in 1992. The front lines had recently pulled back from this area, and several mass graves had been discovered.

pogubljeno dok su članovi njihovih obitelji to promatrali. Kad smo došli u Kupres, bilo je jasno da je cijela regija, sve te kuće, bila granatirana, a zgrade praktički sravnjene sa zemljom.

Iako se činilo da se neki ljudi pokušavaju vratiti svojim domovima, praktički su sve građevine bile uništene. Ovo je gradonačelnik Kupresa u razgovoru s dr. Leejem i dr. Schanfieldom. Utvrdili smo da su ljudi u Kupresu, kao i ljudi u svim područjima koja smo posjetili, bili vrlo gostoljubivi i iznimno zahvalni na našoj pomoći. Gradonačelnik Kupresa bio nam je toliko zahvalan na svemu da su nam usred ratne zone poslužili objed od janjetine i domaćih vina.

One of the graves containing 34 bodies had been excavated the previous week. It is my understanding from media reports that the 34 had been ordered executed while their relatives watched. [See Slide 4.] As we came to the town of Kupres, it was apparent that all of the region, all the houses, had been shelled, with the buildings being essentially in ruin. [See Slide 5.]

Although some of the people appeared to be attempting to get back to their homes, basically all the structures had been destroyed. This is the mayor of Kupres talking with Dr. Lee and Dr. Schanfield. [See Slide 6.] We found that the people of Kupres, as well as the people in all the areas that we visited, were very hospitable and extremely grateful for our help. Such was the appreciation of the mayor of Kupres that we were served a luncheon of lamb and local wines in the middle of a war zone.

This is the site of the grave that had been excavated the week prior to our arrival at the edge of Kupres. [See Slide 7.] This was the grave that had contained 34 bodies. A family member of one of the deceased had marked the grave with this cross. The bodies had been excavated, preliminarily autopsied at the site, and then moved to the Split Clinical Hospital where we would later work with the forensic team on these bodies.

The make-up of those in this grave cut across many lines: soldier and civilian, man and woman, adult and child, although there was a predominance of young males because of the soldiers.

Forensic team with the mayor of Kupres, Ivan Ivić, at the site of a mass grave near Kupres. The photograph includes Henry C. While we were not looking for specific causes of death, it was apparent that most of these people had died from multiple wounds, including gunshots and bayoneting. A conclusive determination of cause of death would also have been difficult because many of the bodies had been bulldozed and attempts had been made to burn the bodies.

Dok smo obilazili ovu prvu lokaciju masovne grobnice u Kupresu, stigla je vijest da su srpske vlasti tijekom nedavne razmjene zarobljenika označile mjesto još jedne grobnice, za koju se poslije utvrdilo da sadržava tijela trojice vojnika. Otišli smo na to mjesto, na samoj lokaciji se presvukli u medicinske kute i pomagali u ekshumaciji tih tijela.

Treba istaknuti da ta tijela ni na koji način nisu bila opsežno obdukcijski obrađena, onako kako bismo to učinili u našoj zemlji. Nedavno sam sudjelovala u izvlačenju jednog tijela u kaznenome djelu na sjeveru države New York,

traditional forensic pathology methods as well as DNA technology was available, the primary process used in the identification was largely direct visual examination by the families.

Most of these people came from towns that had been largely destroyed, so in most cases no dental or other medical records existed to work with. In many cases, we actually had to show family members a remnant of clothing to see if they could recognize it. [See Slide 9.]

Kao što sam prije spomenula, naš je posjet bio vrlo zapažen i cijenjen. Posvećen nam je i znatan medijski prostor. U lokalnim je novinama idućega dana nakon našega posjeta Kupresu objavljen članak s fotografijom koja prikazuje kako pomažemo u ekshumaciji.

Kasnije toga poslijepodneva vojni nas je helikopter prebacio u Petrinju, grad u središnjoj Hrvatskoj, sjeverno od granice s Bosnom, gdje je otkrivena još jedna velika masovna grobnica. U Petrinju smo stigli na kukuruzno polje u nedavno oslobođenoj regiji, gdje su lokalni vojnici identificirali novu lokaciju masovne grobnice. Iako nismo ostali do kraja ekshumacije, poslije je utvrđeno da je na toj lokaciji bilo gotovo 100 tijela.

Rečeno mi je da su mjesto grobnice odredila dvojica hrvatskih vojnika koji su bili srpski ratni zarobljenici i koji su bili prisiljeni

While we were visiting this first mass grave site in Kupres, word came that another grave, which was later found to contain the bodies of three soldiers, had been pinpointed by Serb officials during a recent prisoner exchange. We went to that site, changed into scrub suits at the scene, and assisted in the excavation of these bodies.

I should take the opportunity to note that these bodies were not in any way extensively autopsied as we would do in this country. I recently participated in the recovery of one body in a criminal case in upstate New York where the removal of the body alone took 12 hours and the autopsy another day. This kind of precision was not available in Bosnia and Croatia simply because of the enormity of the numbers.

As I mentioned earlier, our visit was greatly noted and appreciated. There was a great deal of media attention given to our visit. An article appeared the next day following our visit to Kupres in the local paper, showing us at the grave site assisting in the excavation. [See Slide 10.]

PDF page 108

pomagati u prikupljanju i pokapanju tijela na tome mjestu. Kada smo stigli, forenzički tim iz Zagreba, najbližega forenzičkog centra sjeverno od Petrinje, radio je na ekshumaciji tijela. U toj su grobnici pronađeni vojnici i civili svih dobi i obaju spolova.

Stigli smo baš u pravi trenutak. Rečeno nam je da je prije našeg dolaska pas na tom mjestu nastradao od eksplozije mine, a medijski izvještaji bilježe da su samo nekoliko sati prije toga na lokaciji pronađene tri protuoklopne granate, vjerojatno postavljene kako bi se spriječilo bilo kakvo iskapanje tijela. Tijela u toj grobnici isto su bila iz 1991. godine. Bila su u vrlo lošemu stanju, zbog raspadanja, ali i zbog samog načina pokapanja, te su se uglavnom sastojala od skeletiziranih ostataka ili djelomično očuvanih kostura.

Budući da su zubi relativno otporni na razgradnju, bili su nam najkorisnije sredstvo za identifikaciju. Iako stomatološki kartoni praktički nisu postojali, katkad bi se članovi obitelji mogli prisjetiti, primjerice, karakterističnoga prednjeg zuba svojih najmilijih i tako prepoznati osobu. U jednome je slučaju stomatologinja koja je radila na zubima nekolicine nestalih došla u obdukcijsku salu i prepoznla vlastite stomatološke zahvate na nekim zubima.

Šimun Anđelinović, Henry C. Lee, stomatologinja i članovi obitelji tijekom identifikacije žrtve u Kliničkome bolničkom centru Split (foto: Phil Farnsworth, uz dopuštenje).

Šimun Anđelinović, Henry C. Lee, a dentist, and family members during the identification of the victim at the University Hospital of Split (photo: Phil Farnsworth, used with permission).

Later that afternoon, we were taken by military helicopter to Petrinja, a town in north-central Croatia over the border from Bosnia where another large mass grave had been discovered. [See Slide 11.] At Petrinja, we arrived in a cornfield in the recently liberated region where local soldiers had identified another mass grave site. Although we did not stay for the entire excavation, it was later determined that the site contained nearly 100 bodies. [See Slide 12.]

I was told that the location of the grave had been determined by two Croatian soldiers who had been Serb prisoners of war and who had been forced to help collect and bury the bodies at the site. [See Slide 13.] When we arrived, a forensic team from Zagreb, the nearest forensic center located to the north of Petrinja, was working to excavate the bodies. Again, the makeup of the bodies in the grave included soldiers and civilians of all ages and both sexes.

Our timing was good. We were told that before our arrival, a dog had been blown up at the site by a landmine, and media reports noted that just hours before, three anti-tank grenades were recovered at the site, presumably put there to prevent anyone from excavating the bodies. The bodies in this grave were also from 1991. They were in very bad shape, both because of decom-

gornjemu sloju. Dvije stvari koje su me posebno pogodile kad smo prvi put došli do ove grobnice bili su kaotičan način na koji su ta tijela bila nabacana jedno na drugo i smrad gotovo 100 tijela nakon četiri godine raspadanja. Mene je to podsjetilo na filmove o koncentracijskim logorima iz Drugoga svjetskog rata, sa slojevima i slojevima tijela nabacanih u grobnicu.

Sljedećega dana vratili smo se u Kliničku bolnicu Split u Hrvatskoj kako bismo s njihovim forenzičkim timom radili na 34 tijela ekshumi-

Hladnjača s posmrtnim ostatcima žrtava ekshumiranih iz masovnih grobnica u Kliničkome bolničkom centru Split (izvor: arhiv Kliničkoga zavoda za patologiju, sudsku medicinu i citologiju KBC-a Split).

Refrigerated truck containing the remains of victims exhumed from mass graves at the University Hospital of Split (source: Archive of the Clinical Department of Pathology, Forensic Medicine, and Cytology, University Hospital of Split).

position and because of the burial process, and consisted predominantly of skeletonized remains or partial skeletons.

Because teeth tend to be relatively resistant to decomposition, they were our most useful tool in identifying these bodies. Although dental records were essentially nonexistent, sometimes members of a family might remember, for example, a characteristic front tooth of a loved one and could recognize the person that way. In one case, a dentist who had worked on several of the missing people came to the autopsy room and was able to identify his own dental work on some of the bodies. [See Slide 14.]

This is closer to the first layer of the mass of bodies in this grave. You can see the outline of several of the bodies in the top layer at the upper center of the slide. The two things that really struck me as we first encountered this grave was the jumbled fashion in which these bodies were stacked one on top of the other, and the stench of almost 100 bodies with 4 years of decay. It seemed, at least to me, reminiscent of movies of the World War II concentration camps with layers and layers of bodies jumbled in a grave.

The following day, we returned to the Clinical Hospital in Split, Croatia, to work with their forensic team on the 34 bodies recovered the previous week in Kupres. Officials had notified

rana tjedan dana prije u Kupresu. Institucije su obavijestile članove obitelji iz toga grada koji su imali nestalu rodbinu da mogu doći u Split i pokušati identificirati svoje nestale najmilije.

Masovne grobnice i drugi zločini u Bosni / Mass Graves and other Atrocities in Bosnia

Da se ovo događalo u Sjedinjenim Državama, taj bi posao, po meni, trajao mjesec dana. U Splitu je to, zapravo, bio posao od jednoga jutra ili jednoga dana. Nije to bilo zbog nedostatka brige ili nedostatka stručnosti forenzičkoga tima, nego isključivo zato da se, s obzirom na golemi broj ljudi, identificira što je više moguće žrtava, bez potpunoga obduciranja svakoga tijela.

Ovo je obdukcijska sala Kliničke bolnice Split. U prostoriji je istovremeno bilo možda pet ili šest tijela na stolovima. Ovo su članovi našeg tima: ja s desne strane, dr. Lee i dr. Baden u sredini, kako radimo s dr. Šimunom Anđelinovićem, glavnim forenzičkim patologom u Splitu, u pripremama za identifikaciju jednoga od tijela.

Tijela smo rasporedili po stolovima u obdukcijskoj sali na način za koji smo se nadali da će obiteljima biti najmanje potresan. Ovo je tijelo poginulog hrvatskoga vojnika. Njegova kaciga nalazi se na prsima, a iako se ovdje ne vidi jasno, na kacigi je velika rupa od metka. Ovo je prikaz istoga vojnika s izraženim ozljedama lica.

Kao što sam spomenula, u mnogim je slučajevima identifikacija naposljetku ovisila o tome da član obitelji prepozna komad nakita ili odjeće. U ovom je slučaju supruga ovog hrvatskog vojnika, prikazanoga ovdje od stopala prema glavi, uspjela identificirati svoga muža jednostavno prepoznajući njegove čarape marke Nike.

Dok smo pregledavali ta raspadnuta tijela stara nekoliko godina, istodobno smo bili svjesni nasilja koje je još uvijek trajalo u blizini. Susjedna obdukcijska sala bila je mjesto obdukcije vojnika poginulih u aktualnim borbama. Ovaj je vojnik ubijen prethodnoga dana s višestrukim ranama od gelera.

Kao forenzička patologinja naviknula sam da obitelji dolaze u obdukcijsku salu kako bi pogledale ili identificirale članove svojih obite-

If this were the United States, this would have been a month’s work for me. At Split, this turned out to be essentially a morning or a day’s work. This is not because of lack of caring of the forensic team or lack of sophistication. It was just that given the enormous number of people to identify as many people as possible without specifically autopsying each body.

This is the autopsy room in the Clinical Hospital in Split. [See Slide 17.] There were probably five or six bodies at a time on tables in this room. These are the members of our team, myself on the right; Dr. Lee and Dr. Baden in the middle working with Dr. Simun Andelinovic, who was the chief forensic pathologist in Split, preparing to identify one of the bodies. [See Slide 18.]

We arranged the bodies on tables in the autopsy room in a way we hoped would be the least grotesque to family members. [See Slide 19.] This is the body of a dead Croatian soldier. [See Slide 20.] His helmet is on his chest, and although it really doesn’t show well here, there’s a large bullet hole in the helmet. This is a shot of the same soldier showing substantial facial trauma. [See Slide 21.]

As I mentioned, in many of these cases, identification came down to a family member just recognizing a piece of jewelry or a piece of clothing. In this case, the wife of this Croatian soldier who’s shown here feet first was able to identify her husband just by recognizing his Nike socks. [See Slide 22, 23.]

While we were examining these decomposing bodies from several years earlier, we were also reminded of the continued ongoing violence nearby. The adjacent autopsy room was the site of the autopsies of soldiers being killed in the current fighting. This was a soldier who had been killed the day before with multiple shrapnel wounds. [See Slide 24.]

PDF page 112

lji, ali situacija s pet ili šest tijela na stolovima i desetcima obitelji koje se izmjenjuju u obdukcijskoj sali bila je iskustvo za koje nisam bila pripremljena. Bilo je puno teže i zbog jezične barijere. Dr. Anđelinović, koji je u sredini fotografije, pokušavao je za nas obavljati ulogu prevoditelja, ali uz šest tijela i mnogo obitelji, to je bilo praktički nemoguće.

Obiteljima smo pokazivali sve što smo mogli pronaći kako bismo pomogli u procesu identifikacije — često zube preminulih. Najteže je bilo to što, zbog jezične barijere, nismo mogli pružiti nikakvu utjehu članovima obitelji. Unatoč groznomu stanju tijela pred njima, članovi obitelji jako su se željeli zadržati uz svoje identificirane najmilije.

Treba napomenuti da je misija u ovome slučaju uvelike bila uspješna. Do kraja je dana identificirano 27 od 34 tijela iz prve masovne grobnice i sva tri vojnika iz druge grobnice. DNK testiranja na preostalim, još neidentificiranim tijelima, i dalje se nastavljaju.

Ljudi u Bosni i Hrvatskoj prošli su kroz jedan od najgorih ratova stoljeća, s okrutnošću i neljudskim ponašanjem čovjeka prema čovjeku koji podsjećaju na Drugi svjetski rat. Apelirala bih na ovu Komisiju i na sve vlade svijeta da shvate težak položaj tih ljudi i pomognu osigurati sredstva koja će im vjerojatno godinama trebati za rad na identifikaciji tih žrtava. Hvala vam.

As a forensic pathologist, I’m used to families coming to an autopsy room to examine or identify their relatives, but having five or six bodies on tables and dozens of families rotating through an autopsy room was an experience I wasn’t prepared for. It was much more difficult because of the language barrier. Dr. Andelinovic, who is in the center of the photograph, tried to serve as an interpreter for us, but with six bodies and many families, it was essentially impossible. [See Slide 25.]

We showed families whatever we could find to help in the identification process—frequently, teeth of the deceased. The thing that was most difficult was that because of the language barrier, we couldn’t offer any comfort to the family members. [See Slide 26.] Despite the grisly nature of the bodies before them, the family members wanted very much to spend time with their identified loved ones.

I should note that the mission in this case was largely a success. Before the end of the day, 27 of the 34 bodies in the first mass grave, and all 3 of the soldiers from the second grave, were identified. DNA testing is continuing on the remaining bodies yet unidentified.

The people in Bosnia and Croatia have been through one of the worst wars of the century with cruelty and man’s inhumanity to man reminiscent of World War II. I would appeal to this Commission and to all the governments of the world to understand the plight of these people and to help make the resources available to them that will be needed probably for years to come in working with and identifying these victims. Thank you.

PITANJA I ODGOVORI

Predsjednik SMITH. Dr. Wolf, hvala Vam na tom vrlo uznemirujućem svjedočenju. Podsjetili ste nas o kakvim se zločinima zapravo radi kada ih vidimo crno na bijelo i u boji. Počeo bih s pitanjima, a zatim bih prepustio riječ svojim kolegama za njihova eventualna pitanja. Gospodine Rohde, prije ste spomenuli da su neki od vaših čuvara, s kojima ste bili u kontaktu dok su vas bosanski Srbi držali u zatočeništvu, bili očito pristojniji od drugih. Nisu bili skloni zastrašivanju. Čak su Vam dali naslutiti da bi Vaša sudbina ipak mogla imati dobar ishod. Iznova slušamo da se svi bosanski Srbi u ovom ratu ne ponašaju kao da su lišeni svake kaznene odgovornosti te da među njima ima i onih koji doista vjeruju da se ti zločini ne događaju. Hitler je imao svoje SS postrojbe, svoje jurišne odrede i ljude koji su zločine činili kao dio svoje svakodnevice. General Mladić, a gospodine Lupis, možda i Vi želite nešto reći o tome, mora imati neku vrlo elitnu skupinu ili korpus ljudi sklonih takvim zločinima, koji će bespogovorno slijediti zapovijedi, do zadnjega slova, i činiti ovakve strašne stvari kakve ovdje vidimo. Postoje li dokazi da se utvrđuje identitet tim ljudima? Znam da je protiv nekolicine podignuta optužnica, uključujući Mladića. Prikupljamo li dokaze koji upućuju na to da postoji jezgra bosanskih Srba koji su počinili većinu ovih zločina? Gospodine Lupis, biste li Vi željeli prvi odgovoriti?

G. LUPIS. Da. Zapravo, tijekom ove istrage pokušali smo sastaviti zapovjedni lanac koji povezuje vojnike na terenu koji su počinili ove zločine i koji vodi sve do Mladića. U ovome slučaju, kada je riječ o ofenzivi na Srebrenicu, većina toga zahtjevnog posla već je obavljena, jer je general Mladić viđen na mnogim od tih stratišta. Pravi je problem

QUESTIONS AND ANSWERS

Chairman SMITH. Dr. Wolf, thank you for that very disturbing testimony. You have reminded us what kind of atrocities we are actually talking about when we see it in black and white and in color. I’d like to begin the questioning and then yield to my colleagues for any questions they might have. Mr. Rohde, you mentioned earlier that, when you were held by the Bosnian Serbs, some of your guards with whom you had contact were obviously more decent than others. They weren’t into intimidation. They even gave you a headsup that things might turn out well for you. We’ve heard over and over that all of the Bosnian Serbs are not acting with the same kind of impunity in this war, and that there are some who really believe that these atrocities are not taking place. Now, Hitler had his SS, he had his storm troopers, and he had people who committed atrocities as a matter of course. General Mladic—and Mr. Lupis, you might want to speak to this as well—must have a very elite group or corps of people who are given to these kinds of atrocities, who will follow orders to the letter, dotting the i’s and crossing the t’s, and doing the kinds of terrible things that we see here. Is there evidence that those people are being identified? I know there have been a few names handed down, including Mladic, in terms of indictments. Are we gathering evidence that indicates there is a core group of Bosnian Serbs that committed the bulk of these atrocities? Mr. Lupis, would you want to start on that?

MR LUPIS. Yes. As a matter of fact, during this investigation, we’ve been trying to piece together a chain of command linking the soldiers in the field who committed these crimes and continue up the ladder until we get to Mladic. In this case, for the Srebrenica offensive, most of the hard work has been cut out for us because General Mladic

PDF page 114

sada pokušati uspostaviti vezu između pješaštva i Mladića. Human Rights Watch proteklih je dana uspio pribaviti informacije, imena zapovjednika bosanskih Srba koji su djelovali tijekom ove ofenzive i koji su izdavali zapovijedi vojnicima. Teže je, u ovome trenutku, sada utvrditi tko je iz same Srbije, koji su vojnici i vojni dužnosnici iz Srbije bili uključeni, jer postoje snažni dokazi da su u ovoj ofenzivi sudjelovale i snage iz Srbije. Prikupili smo mnoge iskaze. Ljudi su govorili da su vidjeli srpske vojnike u srpskim uniformama, prepoznatljive ponajprije po naglasku. Srpski je naglasak vrlo različit od bosanskoga. Također, izvješća UN-a koja je objavio Mazowiecki sadržavaju svjedočanstva ljudi koji su vidjeli srpske vojnike uključene u ovu ofenzivu. Dakle, sada imamo prilično jasnu sliku o tome tko je uključen sa strane bosanskih Srba. Puno je teže pitanje možemo li utvrditi tko je sudjelovao iz same Srbije.

Predsjednik SMITH. Gospodine Rohde?

G. ROHDE. Od preživjelih sam čuo isto to u svezi sa srpskim naglaskom i slično. Stručnjaci s kojima sam razgovarao ondje, dužnosnici UN-a i razni vojni dužnosnici, smatraju da predsjednik Srbije Slobodan Milošević ima jednu od najboljih obavještajnih službi u bivšoj Jugoslaviji. On je vrlo dobro umrežen. Srbijanci se lako mogu uvući na teritorij bosanskih Srba, tako da je vrlo vjerojatno da je znao da se ta pogubljenja događaju, ali nije učinio ništa da ih zaustavi.

Predsjednik SMITH. S obzirom na navode o masovnim pogubljenjima o kojima je danas bilo riječi, a i prije smo ih čuli, je li bilo ikakvih pokušaja Ujedinjenih naroda, osobito u svezi sa sigurnom zonom Srebrenice, da se istraže ta masovna pogubljenja dok su se događala?

was witnessed, was seen at many of these massacre sites. The real problem now is to try to get the chain established between the infantrymen and Mladic. Human Rights Watch, in the last few days, has been successful in obtaining information, names of Bosnian Serb commanders who operated during this offensive and who gave the orders to the men. The harder thing right now is to establish precisely who from Serbia proper—what military people from Serbia proper, what soldiers from Serbia proper—were involved, because there’s strong evidence to support that troops from Serbia proper were also used in this offensive. We collected many testimonies. People said they’d seen Serbian troops in Serbian uniforms, distinguishable mainly by accents. The Serbian accent is very different from the Bosnian accent. Also, the U.N. reports that Mazowiecki published also collected testimonies of people seeing Serbian troops involved in this offensive. So right now, we have a pretty good picture of who’s involved from the Bosnian Serb side. The more difficult question now is to see if we can determine who was involved in Serbia proper.

Chairman SMITH. Mr. Rohde?

Mr. ROHDE. I heard the same thing from survivors regarding Serbian accents and that kind of thing. And experts I’ve talked with over there—U.N. officials, various military officials—feel that President Milosevic of Serbia has one of the best intelligence organizations in the former Yugoslavia. He has very close ties. Serbians are able to slip into Bosnian Serb territory, so it’s very possible that he knew these executions were going on but did nothing to stop them.

Chairman SMITH. Given the allegations of mass executions which have been spoken about today—and we’ve heard about previously—was there any attempt by the United Nations,

G. LUPIS. Pa, uloga Ujedinjenih naroda je problematična, jer je nizozemski bataljun Ujedinjenih naroda u Srebrenici bio u vrlo teškoj situaciji: s premalo ljudi i nesposoban zaštititi enklavu od ovog napada. Problem koji Human Rights Watch ima s ulogom Ujedinjenih naroda jest to što, čak i ako nisu mogli to spriječiti, nisu pravodobno objavili ključne informacije. Nizozemski su mirotvorci svjedočili nekima od ovih zločina, a umjesto da su ih odmah u stvarnome vremenu dojavili radio vezom, ovi navodi i priče počeli su izlaziti tek nekoliko dana nakon pada Srebrenice. Sada je Mazowiecki, posebni izvjestitelj UN-a za ljudska prava, sastavio izvješće temeljeno na iskazima ljudi koji su istraživali ulogu snaga Ujedinjenih naroda ondje, i ono je izazvalo buru u Nizozemskoj. Čini se da je nizozemska vlada zatajila informacije, odnosno popis muškaraca predanih Srbima. Ako želite saznati više o tome, naveli smo to u svome izvješću, ali sada UN doista pokušava sagledati što može učiniti kako bi objasnio taj izostanak djelovanja. Zbog sloma u Srebrenici, gospodin Mazowiecki podnio je ostavku na svoju dužnost, mislim potkraj kolovoza ili početkom rujna.

G. ROHDE. Još samo nešto. Otišao sam i u Nizozemsku te razgovarao s nekim mirotvorcima koji su i sami bili u enklavi. Oni su mi rekli da su bili duboko frustrirani time što su bili brojčano nadjačani, slabije naoružani i što im je praktički bila povjerena nemoguća zadaća. Konkretno, radilo se o popisu, vjerujem, od 142 muškarca, oprostite, 239, koji su se nalazili unutar zone UN-a i kojima su nizozemske snage naredile da odu. Dok su izlazili, odvajali su ih od obitelji i odvodili. Svi se ti muškarci sada vode nestalima, a jedan od preživjelih s kojima sam razgovarao rekao mi je da je i on bio u Potočarima, što me navodi na zaključak da bi ti muškarci vrlo vjerojatno mogli biti u grobnici koju sam pronašao u blizini sela Čajniče.

particularly as related to the safe haven Srebrenica, to investigate those mass executions as they were occurring?

Mr. LUPIS. Well, the role of the United Nations is problematic in the fact that the United Nations Dutch battalion in Srebrenica was in a very difficult situation: undermanned and unable to protect the enclave from this attack. The problem that Human Rights Watch has with the United Nations’ role is that even if they were helpless in stopping this from happening, there was crucial information which was not released in a timely fashion. The Dutch peacekeepers witnessed some of these atrocities taking place, and instead of radioing it out real-time, these allegations and stories started coming out a few days after the fall of Srebrenica. Now, Mazowiecki, the U.N. human rights rapporteur, had written up a report based on testimonies taken from people investigating the role of the U.N. troops there; and it has caused waves in Holland. The Dutch Government has apparently suppressed information, a list of men who were turned over to the Serbs. If you would like to read about this more in detail, it’s in our report, but right now, the U.N. is really trying to feel out what it can do to explain this lack of action taken by it, and as a result of the Srebrenica debacle, Mr. Mazowiecki resigned his post in, I think, late August, early September.

Mr. ROHDE. There’s just one thing. I also went to Holland and spoke to some of the peacekeepers who were in the enclave themselves, and they spoke of being very frustrated about being outmanned, outgunned, and almost being given an impossible mission to do. And the specific thing was that there was a list of, I believe, 142 men—239, sorry—who were inside the U.N. enclave and ordered by the Dutch to leave.

Vjerujem da IFOR, provedbene snage, imaju nesmetan pristup bilo kojemu mjestu u Bosni i Hercegovini. Ovo bi bila dobra prilika da se organizacije za ljudska prava, kao i forenzički stručnjaci, dovedu na mjesta pokolja s pripadnicima IFOR-a. Vjerujem da istočna Bosna, gdje se nalazi Srebrenica, potpada pod američku jurisdikciju, pa bi pravodobna mjera bilo upućivanje forenzičkoga tima i tima za ljudska prava pod pratnjom američkih vojnika na ta mjesta, čim Daytonski mirovni sporazum bude potpisan. Mislim da bi moglo biti osobito opasno ono što je David prije spomenuo: kad je bio zarobljen, oduzeli su mu karte s označenim lokacijama, a razvili su mu i fotografije. Na to treba reagirati žurno; u protivnom bi bosanski Srbi mogli iskopati te grobnice i ukloniti tijela. Vjerujem da su već na jednom mjestu počeli.

As they left, they were separated from their families and taken away. All of those men are missing, and one of the survivors that I spoke to said he was also at Potocari, which leads me to believe that those men may very well be in the grave that I found near the village of Cajnice.

Chairman SMITH. Let me ask one final question before yielding to Mr. Hoyer. Mr. Lupis, I think this issue will especially apply to you as a researcher for a human rights organization. Mr. Rohde and Dr. Wolf, you also may want to go back to some of the places that you have visited. Do you believe that you will have access, unfettered access, to the sites where suspected mass graves and other kind of atrocities may have been held by all three sides?

Mr. ROHDE. I believe, according to the peace agreement, journalists do not have unfettered access to Bosnian Serb territory.

Mr. LUPIS. Yes, and this would be a good opportunity to call the Serbs’ bluff and promise access to these graves under the Dayton peace agreement. I believe IFOR, the Implementation Force, has unrestricted access to any place in Bosnia-Herzegovina. This would be a good opportunity to bring human rights groups, as well as forensic experts, along with IFOR troops, to the massacre sites. I believe eastern Bosnia, where Srebrenica is, falls under U.S. jurisdiction, so a timely action would be to deliver a forensic team and a human rights team to be escorted to these sites by U.S. troops as soon as the Dayton peace agreement is signed. One thing I think David said before, which may be dangerous, is that when he was captured, his maps of the sites were confiscated from him and, as well, his pictures were developed. Something like this should be done in a hurry; otherwise, the Bosnian Serbs

ma ili je to nešto što bi se radilo neovisno? Kako biste to organizirali? Dr. Wolf, kako ste Vi organizirali svoj dio?

Dr. WOLF. Naše je putovanje bilo pod okriljem AmeriCaresa i u suradnji s Kliničkim bolničkim centrom u Splitu. Na terenu djeluje nekoliko forenzičkih timova, tim u Splitu i tim u Zagrebu. Oni raspolažu svim potrebnim znanjima i tehnikama za provedbu identifikacijskih postupaka, uključujući i DNK tehnologiju, ali u osnovi govorimo o samo nekoliko forenzičkih patologa i stomatologa. Dakle, mislim da bi nam trebali i resursi, ponajprije financijska sredstva, ali i u vidu pomoći drugih forenzičkih stručnjaka. Oni bi zasigurno bili vrlo zahvalni na svakoj forenzičkoj pomoći u tom pogledu.

Predsjednik SMITH. Gospodine Lupis, kako informacije koje prikupite dolaze do tužitelja Goldstonea? Radite li izravno s njim ili mu ih jednostavno prosljeđujete?

G. LUPIS. Ne, mi radimo neovisno. Oni imaju svoje istražitelje, a mi svoje. Ali, radimo usporedno. Sve što otkrijemo ili utvrdimo odmah im šaljemo kako bismo im pomogli u sastavljanju predmeta za optužnice. Ipak, sustav funkcionira dobro. Do sada je sve što smo objavili od 1991. do 1992. godine o ratu predano Međunarodnomu kaznenom sudu, tako da je to učinkovito. Mislim da bi oni mogli obaviti dobar posao kad bi cijeli postupak proveli do kraja.

G. ROHDE. Samo želim istaknuti da ja ne radim usporedno sa Sudom za ratne zločine. Moj je cilj samo iznijeti informacije u javnost i učiniti ih javno dostupnima. Ipak, bilo bi dobro na to predloženo putovanje, o kojemu ste govorili, povesti i novinare, kako bi i oni mogli ići, jer mi nemamo nesmetan pristup; ali siguran sam da bi bilo novinara koji bi se dobrovoljno pridružili kada bi postojala odgovarajuća sigurnosna jamstva.

would be able to dig up these graves and remove the bodies. I believe they’ve started in one place already.

Chairman SMITH. If I could follow up, is there something that should be coordinated with Justice Goldstone and his prosecutors, or is it something that would be done independently? How would you work that? Dr. Wolf, how did you work yours?

Dr. WOLF. Well, our trip was under the auspices of AmeriCares and in collaboration with the Split Medical Center. There are several forensic teams in place, a team in Split, a team in Zagreb. They have all of the knowledge and techniques, including DNA technology, to do the identification process, but basically we are talking about just a few forensic pathologists and dentists. So I think we would need both the resources, in terms of monetary resources, as well as assistance from other forensic experts. They are certainly very appreciative of any forensic help in that regard.

Chairman SMITH. Mr. Lupis, how does your information make its way to Justice Goldstone? Do you work with him, or do you feed them information?

Mr. LUPIS. No, we work independently. They have their own investigators, and we have ours. But we work in a parallel fashion. Whatever we uncover or discover we send immediately to them just to help them build the cases for the indictments. But it’s a solid system. So far, everything we’ve published since 1991-1992 about the war has been handed over to the International Criminal Tribunal, so it’s effective. I think they could do a good job if they fully carried it out.

Mr. ROHDE. I just want to state that I don’t work in a parallel fashion with the War Crimes Tribunal. I just try to get the information

Dr. WOLF. Very much so, and I think this was really an example of science crossing polit-

mogu pomoći pri identifikaciji. Forenzički timovi obavili su golem posao prikupljanja svih dostupnih podataka o nestalim osobama. Imaju bazu podataka i prikupili su sve dostupne dentalne i zdravstvene podatke o tim nestalima, ali u mnogo slučajeva, s obzirom na to da su cijeli gradovi razoreni, vrlo je malo toga što se može iskoristiti. Uobičajeno je da se pri identifikaciji raspadnutoga tijela koristimo stomatološkim kartonima i sličnim podatcima. U mnogim slučajevima takvi zapisi ondje nisu dostupni. Zbog toga je potreba za analizom DNK-a veća. Laboratorij u Splitu radi već oko godinu dana i sada proširuje rad izvan tradicionalnih metoda analize DNK-a, nadamo se u smjeru sofisticiranijih tehnika analize mitohondrijskoga DNK-a. Mislim da će to postati vrlo važno u ovome procesu. Tako uzorci članova obitelji mogu poslužiti za identifikaciju nekih nestalih osoba kada druge metode identifikacije nisu dostupne. Hrvatski su znanstvenici dolazili u ovu zemlju i radili s tim tehnologijama. Naši stručnjaci za DNK, dr. Lee, dr. Schanfield i drugi, pregledali su njihov rad i tehnologiju. Kompetencije i znanje zasigurno imaju. Potrebni su im resursi i ljudi.

G. HOYER. Doktorice, govorili ste o identifikaciji ubijenih osoba. Očigledno Vaš posao nije toliko usmjeren na samu identifikaciju osoba, koliko na utvrđivanje zašto su ubijene, posebice kada se pokušava uspostaviti veza s kaznenim djelom, u ovom slučaju ratnim zločinom. Jeste li uvjereni da možemo provesti forenzičku obradu kakvu Haag zahtijeva?

Dr. WOLF. Kao što ste rekli, obično kao forenzička patologinja radim na kaznenim predmetima. U ovome slučaju, na ovome konkretnom putovanju, donesena je odluka da se ne traže dokazi o zločinima, iako mislim da, očito, masovne grobnice same po sebi, i ljudi u njima, donekle govore sami za sebe. Tijela

ical lines. The techniques that we use in the United States were very much the same techniques that they were using there, including the DNA laboratory in Split.

Mr. HOYER. The problem, I take it then, is the volume of work to be done and the scarcity of numbers there to do the work?

Dr. WOLF. The volume of people to be identified, and the lack of available information which aids in identification. The forensic teams have done a tremendous amount of work in gathering whatever information is available about the missing persons. They have a data bank and have gathered whatever dental or other medical information about these missing persons, but in many cases, because the entire towns are destroyed, there is very little to use. Traditionally with identification of a decomposed body, we work with dental records, that sort of thing. In many cases, those records aren’t available there. So that puts a greater need for DNA techniques. The laboratory in Split has been up and running for about a year and they’re now expanding beyond traditional DNA, hopefully into the more sophisticated mitochondrial DNA techniques. I think that’s going to become very important in this process. In that way, samples from relatives can be used to identify some of these missing people when other methods of identification aren’t available. Croatian scientists have come to this country and spent time working with the technologies. Our DNA experts, Dr. Lee, Dr. Schanfield, and others, have looked at their work and the technology. The capabilities are certainly there. It’s resources and people that are needed.

Mr. HOYER. Doctor, you have reflected upon the identification of individuals who have been killed. Obviously your job is not so

much about identification, but to determine why they were killed, particularly in trying to make some connection with a criminal act, in this case, a war crime. Are you confident that we’re going to be providing the kind of forensics work that will be needed in the Hague?

Mr. HOYER. Thank you. Mr. Rohde, you mentioned on a couple of occasions the gravesites which you had identified, took pictures of, and talked to survivors. Now, I wanted to follow up at that time and did not. That may or may not be relevant. Obviously you can’t kill soldiers that have been captured and are unarmed and are no longer combatants. It is a war crime to murder them and put them in a mass grave as much as it is any other individual. At that point in time, they’re essentially subject to the same protections that civilians are, as I understand it. Was there a contention that these soldiers were killed in battle? Is that the defense?

Mr. ROHDE. That is the Bosnian Serbs’ explanation. They say these were all soldiers killed in combat, and for sanitary reasons, the bodies were collected from the areas and put in these mass graves. But again, the evidence I found contradicts that in terms of civilian clothes, and all the evidence I found has buttressed the accounts of the nine survivors who say civilians were executed.

Mr. HOYER. OK.

Mr. ROHDE. It’s also important to point out that I believe only a third of the men who fled the enclave were armed. So the idea of combat going on is also difficult to prove from that.

He had a soldier cut the pig’s throat and told the Dutch, “you have to be able to watch this before we can talk.” He then told the Dutch he would shell the compound if they resisted any efforts his troops made to take away the men at the site. Mr. ROHDE. Yes.

Mr. HOYER. And represented to them they were going to be released.

Mr. ROHDE. Consistently on at least four different locations.

Mr. HOYER. Now, I’m wondering whether or not you had discussions with folks who were there or who have analyzed that situation in trying to establish this chain of command and the connection between Mladic and Milosevic, and those who actually inflicted the death blows or death shots or however the death was brought.

Mr. ROHDE. He was only seen at the one site that I visited. According to one survivor, he got out of the car and watched as the executions were going on. At other locations, he spoke to prisoners a few hours before the shooting started. Again, everything else that survivor told me matched perfectly in terms of the description of the site and everything I found.

Mr. HOYER. And we have the names of those people? You talked to one witness who saw Mladic observing the killing go on?

Mr. ROHDE. Yes. Others put him at the site addressing prisoners hours before they were executed.

Mr. HOYER. Mr. Lupis, how many witnesses do we have that fall in that category in number?

Mr. LUPIS. How many witnesses who have seen Mladic?

Mr. HOYER. Yes.

Mr. LUPIS. I believe we talked to about six survivors of the massacres, I think of which four had witnessed Mladic at different sites. Two of them saw Mladic in the Karakaj area

Mr. HOYER. Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Last question, and I’ve already taken more time than I should have and I apologize to Mr. Porter who’s waiting patiently to ask his questions. Could you comment on the importance of bringing to justice those who have perpetrated the acts which you have witnessed and investigated?

Mr. LUPIS. I think that is the fundamental principle which must be carried out to the end. Many of these atrocities which have been committed during this war have come about as a result of unhealed wounds from World War II when nationalists slaughtered various ethnic groups. When Tito came into power, he just basically suppressed any talk or any doings of resolving these issues. Many people we talked to, especially Serbs, often refer to crimes committed against them. The Muslims of eastern Bosnia have suffered many massacres by the Serbs over the years,

It was very clear that the rationalization in the heads of these execution squads was that they were carrying out revenge for World War II, 50 years later. The Serbs did suffer a tremendous amount in World War II. So again, there’s anecdotal evidence of the possibility of what you’re talking about.

To answer the question that you’re asking, I think, clearly what we have seen is that it will happen again, it can happen again. But my own experience there was not to look at that question.

Again, the Bosnian Serbs had denied access to anyone to the Srebrenica area for 3 months, and I felt that action was warranted to see what had really happened there.

Mr. PORTER. Mr. Lupis, did you go in there also? I didn’t hear any of your testimony unfortunately.

Mr. LUPIS. Unfortunately, Human Rights Watch, and I think other human rights groups, have consistently been denied access to Bosnian Serb-controlled territories. So our job basically, the best we could do, was to travel to the Tuzla area where many of these refugees were crossing over. The people who trekked through the mountains crossed over into Tuzla where most of the men, elderly, women, and children were bused to. From there, we basically went to the refugee camps and attempted to interview people, trying to get their accounts. It was a traumatic experience because many of these people had just crossed over and they were still in shock about what had happened. The one important thing is, I think, in order for something like the Dayton peace plan to work, is that all territories in Bosnia-Herzegovina should be accessible to human rights groups, to IFOR troops, in order to be able to find out exactly what happened to everyone in the territories. Mr. LUPIS. I’d like to answer your question in a more general sense. Everywhere, from the media, from television shots to the United Nations to Western diplomats running in and out of the country. In Bosnia-Herzegovina, the tragic thing is it’s been happening in front of our eyes and we keep on using the United Nations as the vehicle by which we pass resolutions and we do nothing about them. This Srebrenica case is one of the greatest examples of having Western presence right in the middle of it—in the form of a United Nations safe area, and it was overrun. Resolutions were passed, condemnations, those who have committed crimes against humanity and
