G. LUPIS. Mislim da je najvažnije da se prestane prebacivati odgovornost na Ujedinjene narode. Međunarodna bi zajednica trebala prestati oslanjati se na taj birokratski stroj za donošenje rezolucija iza kojih ne slijedi djelovanje. Vjerujem da NATO, kao legitimni vojni akter u ovome poslijehladnoratovskom razdoblju, treba formulirati jasnu i preciznu misiju kako bi pokušao privesti ratne zločince Sudu. Sada više ne govorimo o tome da IFOR ne može uhititi te zločince. To sve više počinje nalikovati još jednoj misiji Ujedinjenih naroda. Sud za ratne zločine treba podržati svim sredstvima kako bi se ti ratni zločinci mogli procesuirati i optužiti, i kako bi ljudi koji su izgubili svoje obitelji mogli dobiti neku vrstu pravde. Time bi se dao primjer drugim zemljama, drugim situacijama, gdje bi Međunarodni kazneni sud za ratne zločine imao stvarnu i ozbiljnu težinu.

G. ROHDE. Govori se, a vjerujem da Sjedinjene Države to podržavaju, o osnivanju stalnoga suda za ratne zločine koji bi trajno postojao i koji bi se bavio ovakvim situacijama. Doista nemam odgovor na to i ne osjećam se pozvanim o tome suditi, ali mogu vam samo iz iskustva reći da je teško kvantificirati učinak odvraćanja. Moji zarobitelji bosanski Srbi bili su vrlo iznenađeni što su me zatekli tako duboko na svome teritoriju i što sam uspio proći njihove kontrolne točke. Mislim da ih je šokiralo kada su Sjedinjene Države javno objavile te satelitske snimke. Postoji argument u prilog odvraćanju. Mislim da je jedan od razloga zašto su bili uvjereni da sam špijun taj što su i sami morali priznati da samo špijun može učiniti nešto takvo. Mislim da su bili vrlo potreseni mjestom na kojemu su me uhvatili, tim fotografijama, i općenito, čini se da je to utjecalo na njih i možda ih navelo da obuzdaju neke svoje postupke.

that is proceeding, although my understanding is that the NATO forces will not have authority to apprehend or arrest those accused of war crimes. So that is taken off the table. What can we do in a broader sense about this, not just Bosnia, but all of this genocide that goes on? What can we do to change the apparent acceptance of it by the world? And I don’t mean that in an accusatory sense at all. I think that every American has been deeply disturbed about what they have seen and understood about what has happened in Bosnia. But what do we do beyond this? Do we pursue this judicial direction only? Does Mr. Rohde write a book that reaches to our soul? Does the U.S. Congress pass another resolution? What are your thoughts?

Mr. LUPIS. Again, I think the most important thing is to stop passing the buck to the United Nations. The international community should stop relying on this convenient bureaucratic machine to pass resolutions and not act. I believe NATO, now being the legitimate military leader in this post-cold war era, should formulate a concise and clear mission in order to try to bring these war criminals to trial. Now it’s off the table that IFOR can’t apprehend these criminals. It’s starting to sound like another United Nations mission. The War Crimes Tribunal should be supported by all means so at least these war criminals can be tried and accused and the people who have lost families will be able to receive some kind of justice. Therefore, this would set an example for other countries, other situations where the International War Crimes Tribunal would have some formidable stature.

Mr. ROHDE. There is some talk, and I believe the United States does support the formation of a permanent war crimes tribunal that would

PDF page 132

Čini se da od kolovoza, otkako su te stvari objavljene, nisu zabilježena pogubljenja takvih razmjera.

G. PORTER. Čini mi se, i najsnažnije sam to osjetio u vrijeme kada je nigerijska vlada pogubila ogonsku devetoricu, uključujući Kena Saro-Wiwu, da bi svijet, kad bi mogao snažno i jednoglasno reagirati u takvoj situaciji, tako što bi prekinuo – a to se ne odnosi nuždno na Bosnu jer je riječ o drugačijoj situaciji – svaku diplomatsku, političku i ekonomsku interakciju s takvim društvom dok se vlada ne promijeni, kada bismo mogli progovoriti jedinstvenim glasom od Europe, preko Azije do Sjeverne i Južne Amerike i izraziti svoje ogorčenje tako da ta država bude potpuno izolirana… To smo činili, naravno, tijekom mnogo duljeg razdoblja u Južnoj Africi i naposljetku se isplatilo. No, kada bismo tako mogli govoriti o ovakvim strašnim zločinima, koji nas sve duboko potresaju, tada bi se poslala poruka svima drugima koji bi htjeli nešto slično počiniti. Ako to ne učinimo, ako Shell Oil Company kaže nigerijskoj vladi: „Ne brinite se za Svjetsku banku. Nadoknadit ćemo tih 100 milijuna dolara i projekt će ići dalje. Nije nas briga što ste ubili devet ljudi. Pa što?” – čini mi se da je to upravo ono što ohrabruje ovakvo ponašanje. Mislim da smo dosegli, ili smo odavno trebali doseći, razinu razmjene informacija i zajedničke čovječnosti na kojoj bismo trebali naučiti govoriti jednim glasom; i možda Ujedinjeni narodi nisu pravo mjesto za to, ali nekako svi moramo ustati s takvim pravednim ogorčenjem pred takvim zločinima da se oni više ne ponove; da se učini sve što je moguće kako bi se spriječili. A nažalost, svijet to jednostavno nije učinio. U Sjedinjenim Državama možemo govoriti koliko god želimo o tome da nam je stalo do ljudskih prava, ali znamo da se danas naše

exist permanently to address these kind of situations. I really don’t have an answer to that and don’t feel qualified to comment on it, but I can just tell you anecdotally that the power of deterrence is tough to measure. My Bosnian Serb captors were very surprised to find me so far into their territory and that I was able to get through their checkpoints. I think they were shocked when the United States publicly released these satellite photos. There’s a case to be made in terms of deterrence. I think one of the reasons they were convinced I was a spy was because they themselves had to say only a spy could do such a thing. So I just think they were very shook up by where they found me, by these photos, and just anecdotally, it seemed to have an effect on them and maybe made them curtail some of their behavior. It appears that executions of this size have not occurred since these things were made public in August.

Mr. PORTER. It seems to me, and I thought this most strongly at the time of the Nigerian Government’s execution of the Agani 9, including Ken Saro-Wiwa, that if the world can react strongly and overwhelmingly at a situation like that one to cut off—and this does not necessarily apply to Bosnia because it’s a different situation—but to cut off all diplomatic, political, economic intercourse with such a society until the government is changed, if we could speak in a unified voice from Europe to Asia to North America and South America and express our outrage in such a way that the country is completely isolated. We did this, of course, over a much longer period of time in South Africa, and it finally proved its worth. But if we could speak in that voice about these kinds of horrible atrocities that shake all of us so much that there would be a message to all others who would perpetrate them.

Gospodin Rohde i ja prije smo razgovarali o tome da se odmah nakon potpisivanja Daytonskoga mirovnog sporazuma prozre blef Srba i da se na ta mjesta masovnih grobnica dovede forenzički tim s predstavnicima organizacija za ljudska prava i novinarima. Dakle, Daytonski mirovni plan nudi priliku da se preokrene ono što se događalo posljednjih četiriju godina.

G. PORTER. Hvala Vam, gospodine predsjedniče.

Predsjednik SMITH. Hvala Vam, gospodine Porter. Prije nego što zaključimo, želio bih postaviti još jedno završno pitanje. Jedna od mojih briga, a to moji suradnici i ja prepoznajemo već mjesecima, jest ta da neke osobe unutar Ujedinjenih naroda i neki sudionici ovoga procesa u međunarodnoj zajednici zapravo i

To the extent that we do not do that, to the extent that Shell Oil Company says to the Nigerian Government, “Don’t worry about the World Bank. We’ll make up the $100 million and the project will go ahead. We don’t care that you killed nine people. So what?” It seems that is exactly the kind of thing that encourages this kind of conduct. I think we’ve reached—we should have reached long since, but we have reached a level of information-sharing in a level of common humanity that we ought to learn how to speak in one voice; and perhaps the United Nations isn’t the place to do it, but somehow we have to all rise up in such righteous indignation about these things that they can’t happen again; that everything is brought to bear to prevent them. And unfortunately, the world has just not done that. We can talk all we want in the United States about our caring about human rights; but we know today that our weapons supplied to the Turkish Government are used to kill Kurds without trial, people who simply disappear, whose homes are plowed under or they’re driven from them; and yet we don’t put that at high enough priority. I don’t know if you want to comment on that. It’s more of a statement than a question, but feel free if you’d like.

Mr. LUPIS. In terms of Bosnia, the Dayton peace plan seems to be the perfect opportunity to reverse the trend of the last 4 years because with Rwanda and Bosnia, I think, leaders around the world—nationalists who are thinking of carrying out some similar campaigns as have been carried out in the aforementioned countries—are feeling pretty comfortable because so far, no international action has been taken up that really changed the tide of these conflicts. Mr. Rohde and I had spoken earlier about, as soon as the Dayton peace agreement is signed, immediately calling the Serbs’ bluff

ne žele da Sud za ratne zločine u velikoj mjeri uspije. Možda nekoliko optužnica, nekoliko pokaznih slučajeva i to je to; a zločine ostavimo za sobom i krenemo dalje. IFOR je ovlašten uhititi bilo kojega optuženog ratnog zločinca na kojega naiđe ili koji se umiješa u njegovu misiju, ali ne će aktivno tragati za njima.” Pregledom teksta Daytonskog sporazuma, njegovih aneksa, dodataka i popratnih pismena, nigdje nismo pronašli odredbu ili odredbe koje IFOR-u daju „ovlast da uhićuje bilo

and bringing a forensic team with human rights people and journalists to these grave sites. Exposing these graves sites would, I think, help start turning the mechanisms for the International War Crimes Tribunal to issue more indictments and just get the ball moving with the War Crimes Tribunal in general. So the Dayton peace plan, I think, offers an opportunity where we can reverse the last 4 years.

Mr. PORTER. Thank you, Mr. Chairman.

Chairman SMITH. Thank you, Mr. Porter. Before we conclude, I’d like to ask one final question. Mr. Lupis, I noticed that you used the word “calling their bluff” on two occasions, and I heartily agree. One of the concerns that I have—and this has been picked up at least by my staff and myself for months now—that there are some within the United Nations and some who are part of this process in the international community who really don’t want the War Crimes Tribunal to succeed all that much. Perhaps a few indictments, some show cases, and that’s it, put the atrocities behind us and move on. I think that would be a travesty if that were the case. That’s why I think there are some at the United Nations particularly who have made it very hard for Justice Goldstone to proceed. From my perch as chairman of the International Operations Subcommittee, we have tried to pressure the United Nations, over which our committee has jurisdiction, as well as the administration, really to be more aggressive, to make sure sufficient funds are allocated in a timely fashion. You know very well how damning the Shell study was in terms of what evidence was being lost. I met with administration officials earlier this week who told me they don’t have one shred of evidence on Milosevic in terms of committing war crimes. I was astounded

that this has not been an ongoing fact-finding accumulative process, and I was very disappointed, frankly, when the administration official told me this. Dugogodišnje prijateljstvo i znanstvena suradnja s prof. dr. sc. Mitchellom Hollandom doveli su do imenovanja profesorom i aktivnog uključivanja prof. dr. sc. Dragana Primorca u nastavu forenzičkog programa Sveučilišta Penn State, a potom i do njegova imenovanja titulom Global Penn State Ambassador, po prvi put u povijesti toga sveučilišta osnovanog 1855. godine. Tu su titulu prof. dr. sc. Draganu Primorcu 2016. godine svečano uručili rektor Sveučilišta Penn State prof. dr. sc. Nicholas P. Jones i prorektor prof. dr. sc. Michael Adewumi.

The long-standing friendship and scientific collaboration with Mitchell Holland led to the appointment of Dragan Primorac as a professor and to his active involvement in the forensic science program at Pennsylvania State University. This collaboration subsequently resulted in his appointment as a Global Penn State Ambassador, the first such title awarded in the history of the university, founded in 1855. The title was formally conferred upon Professor Dragan Primorac in 2016 by Nicholas P. Jones, President of Pennsylvania State University, and Michael Adewumi, Vice Provost for Global Programs.

PDF page 141

Prijateljstvo i profesionalna suradnja prof. dr. sc. Henryja C. Leeja i prof. dr. sc. Dragana Primorca rezultirali su time da je prof. dr. sc. Dragan Primorac od samih početaka bio imenovan profesorom i aktivno uključen u nastavni i znanstveni rad na University of New Haven, na Henry C. Lee College of Criminal Justice and Forensic Sciences, kao i u djelovanje The Henry C. Lee Institute of Forensic Sciences od njegova osnutka 1998. godine. Kao priznanje za izniman doprinos razvoju forenzičkih znanosti i međunarodnoj afirmaciji Instituta, prof. dr. Henry C. Lee osobno je prof. dr. Primorcu dodijelio priznanje Lifetime Achievement Award, a s predsjednikom toga Sveučilišta, prof. dr. sc. Stevenom Kaplanom i prestižnu International Distinguished Service Award 2010. godine. Dugogodišnja i intenzivna međunarodna suradnja rezultirala je i potpisivanjem sporazuma o suradnji između University of New Haven i Sveučilišta u Splitu, kao i između Penn State University i Sveučilišta u Splitu, koji se uspješno provode i danas. Kao posebno priznanje za izniman doprinos razvoju hrvatske forenzike te identifikaciji žrtava Domovinskog rata, na prijedlog prof. dr. sc. Dragana Primorca i prof. dr. sc. Šimuna Anđelinovića, prof. dr. sc. Henry C. Lee 2005. je godine odlikovan Redom Danice hrvatske s likom Katarine Zrinske, dok mu je Sveučilište u Splitu 2010. godine, odlukom tadašnjeg rektora prof. dr. sc. Ivana Pavića, dodijelilo počasni doktorat.

The friendship and professional collaboration between Henry C. Lee and Dragan Primorac resulted in Professor Dragan Primorac being appointed, from the very beginning, as a professor actively involved in both the teaching and scientific activities at the University of New Haven, within the Henry C. Lee College of Criminal Justice and Forensic Sciences, as well as in the work of the Henry C. Lee Institute of Forensic Sciences since its establishment in 1998. In recognition of his exceptional contribution to the advancement of forensic sciences and to the international reputation of the Institute, Henry C. Lee personally presented Professor Dragan Primorac with the Lifetime Achievement Award, and together with Steven Kaplan, President of the University of New Haven, also conferred upon him the prestigious International Distinguished Service Award in 2010. The long-standing and intensive international collaboration also led to the signing of cooperation agreements between the University of New Haven and the University of Split, as well as between Pennsylvania State University and the University of Split, partnerships that continue to be successfully maintained to this day. In recognition of his exceptional contribution to the development of Croatian forensic science and to the identification of victims of the Croatian War of Independence, Henry C. Lee was, upon the proposal of Professors Dragan Primorac and Šimun Anđelinović, awarded the Order of the Croatian Star with the Effigy of Katarina Zrinska in 2005. In 2010, the University of Split, by decision of its then Rector professor Ivan Pavić, conferred upon him the degree of Doctor Honoris Causa.

30. obljetnica objave prvih rezultata primjene tehnologije DNK u identifikaciji žrtava iz masovnih grobnica 30th Anniversary of the Publication of the First Paper on DNA Identification of Victims from Mass Graves

PDF page 149

Ovaj je tekst, uz dopuštenje uredništva, preuzet i prilagođen iz Liječničkih novina, broj 240/2026.

30. obljetnica primjene tehnologije DNK u identifikaciji žrtava Domovinskoga rata

Piše: dr. Vinko Bubić, specijalist kliničke radiologije KB Merkur, Zagreb, Hrvatska

Kad osvane taj dan u zemlji ranjenoj biser sja na dlanu grad i moj i tvoj

Kad raziđe se dim – Vice Vukov (stihovi: Krste Juras)

U vedrome i sunčanome Splitu na ugodnih 20 stupnjeva 27. travnja 2026., u Velikoj dvorani KBC-a Split, na Firulama, održana je 30. obljetnica objave prvih rezultata primjene tehnologije DNK u identifikaciji žrtava iz masovnih grobnica. To je metoda koja je upravo u Hrvatskoj prvi put primijenjena u praksi, i to u ratnim okolnostima gdje je mlada država bila suočena s velikim brojem često neprepoznatljivih posmrtnih ostataka. Splitski stručnjaci prof. dr. sc. Šimun Anđelinović i prof. dr. sc. Dragan Primorac razvili su model koji je spojio forenzičku genetiku s klasičnim metodama i omogućio identifikaciju i u najtežim slučajevima. „Bili su to teški uvjeti analize DNK iz bakterijski kontaminiranih uzoraka oštećenih

This article was translated and adapted with minor modifications from the original Croatian version published in Liječničke novine, issue No. 240/2026.

30th Anniversary of the Publication of the First Paper on DNA Identification of Victims from Mass Graves

Written and translated by: Vinko Bubić, MD, Specialist in Clinical Radiology, University Hospital Merkur, Zagreb, Croatia PhD Candidate, University of Split School of Medicine, Croatia

When parts away the smoke When first heartache is gone We’ll sit around the table In our ancient home.

When parts away the smoke – Vice Vukov (stihovi: Krste Juras)

In the bright and sunny city of Split, Croatia, on April 27, 2026, under clear spring skies and mild weather, the Great Hall of the University Hospital of Split at Firule hosted the 30th anniversary of the publication of the first results on the application of DNA technology in the identification of victims from mass graves. It was precisely in Croatia that this method was first implemented in practice, under wartime circumstances in which young Croatian state was faced with many, often unrecognizable, human remains.

Croatian experts Professor Šimun Anđelinović, MD, PhD, and Professor Dragan Primorac, MD, PhD, developed a model that combined forensic genet-

PDF page 150

Otvaranje promocije monografije i izložbe, uvodno obraćanje ravnatelja KBC-a izv. prof. dr. sc. Krešimira Dolića (izvor: arhiv KBC-a Split).

Opening of the monograph and exhibition presentation, introductory address by the Director of the University Hospital of Split, Associate Professor Krešimir Dolić, MD, PhD (source: archives of the University Hospital of Split).

teškim metalima, gdje tim tada nije imao nikakvih iskustava”, istaknuo je prof. dr. sc. Dragan Primorac. Za medije je naš znanstvenik i forenzički genetičar osobito istaknuo rad, publiciran prije 30 g., koji DNK tehnologiju stavlja na pijedestal korištenja za identifikaciju tijela, što je promijenilo percepciju cijeloga svijeta prema istini koja je – samo jedna, kako je istaknuo.

Upravo prijateljstvo njega i predstojnika Patologije prof. dr. sc. Šimuna Anđelinovića nadilazilo je samu znanost jer su bili povezani i taekwondo klubom Kocunar u Splitu, koji je osnovao prof. Primorac, a njihov daljnji život i karijeru duboko su obilježili u Domovinskome ratu gubitak njihove šestorice prijatelja i učenika, koje i dandanas s ponosom spominju. To im je bio velik motiv da uspiju u projektu identifikacije. Prof. Primorac upravo je u laboratoriju u Connecticutu (SAD) s glasovitim

ics with standard forensic methods, enabling identification even in the most difficult cases.

“These were extremely challenging conditions for DNA extraction, amplification, and analysis from skeletal remains often contaminated with bacterial DNA and heavy metals, while the greatest difficulty was that no one had prior experience regarding which protocols should be applied in such cases,” emphasized Professor Dragan Primorac.

Speaking to the media, the Croatian scientist and forensic geneticist particularly highlighted the paper published in the Journal of Forensic Sciences thirty years ago, which placed DNA technology at the fore of body identification methods, fundamentally changing the world’s perception toward the truth – since, as he stated, “there is only one truth.”

The friendship between Professor Primorac and the Head of Pathology, Professor Šimun Anđelinović, transcended science itself, as they were also connected through the Taekwondo Club Kocunar in Split, founded by Professor Primorac, in whose leadership Professor Anđelinović also participated. Their lives and careers were deeply marked during the Croatian War of Independence by the loss of six of their friends and students, who had joined Professor Primorac on January 14, 1991, in the defense of Croatia, and whom they still proudly remember today. This became a powerful motivator for the success of the identification project.

At the laboratory in Connecticut, USA, together with the renowned Dr. Henry Lee, Professor Primorac worked even after regular hours to isolate DNA from degraded samples, which were subsequently amplified using the polymerase chain reaction (PCR). The connection with the humanitarian organization AmeriCares was facilitated by Stanimir Vuk-Pavlović, a distinguished Croatian American scientist at the

PDF page 151

30. obljetnica primjene tehnologije DNK u identifikaciji žrtava Domovinskoga rata / 30th Anniversary of the Publication of the First Paper on DNA Identification of Victims from Mass Graves

dr. Henryjem Leejem nakon redovitih obveza radio na izolaciji DNK-a iz degradiranih uzoraka, koje su potom umnažali lančanom reakcijom polimeraze (PCR, engl. polymerase chain reaction). Vezu s humanitarnom organizacijom AmeriCares osigurao je Stanimir Vuk-Pavlović, istaknuti hrvatsko-američki znanstvenik iz bolnice Mayo Clinic, koja je rezultirala otvaranjem laboratorija 16. ožujka 1994. Već u prosincu 1994. na poziv dr. Leeja predstavljen je „hrvatski model” identifikacije žrtava iz masovnih grobnica primjenom DNK tehnologije na međunarodnoj konferenciji u Taipeiju (Tajvan), koja rezultira i člankom objavljenim 1996. u uglednom časopisu Journal of Forensic Sciences.

Domaćin ovoga važnog susreta bio je izv. prof. dr. sc. Krešimir Dolić, specijalist radiologije i ravnatelj KBC-a Split. Tom je prigodom za medije iznio da je u jeku rata splitski tim radio na osjetljivom području s američkim timom i da je taj model preuzeo danas čitav svijet,

Prvo izdanje monografije Imena u tišini (izvor: arhiv KBC-a Split).

First edition of the monograph Names in the Silence (source: archives of the University Hospital of Split).

Mayo Clinic, which led to the opening of the laboratory on March 16, 1994.

Already in December 1994, at Dr. Lee’s invitation, Professor Primorac presented the “Croatian model” for DNA-based identification of mass grave victims at an international conference in Taipei, Taiwan, which later led to the publication of the landmark article in the prestigious Journal of Forensic Sciences in 1996.

The host of this important gathering was Associate Professor Krešimir Dolić, MD, PhD, specialist radiologist and Director of the University Hospital of Split. Speaking to the media, he emphasized that in the height of the war, the Split team worked in an extremely sensitive field alongside the American team, and that today the entire world has adopted this pioneering model, of which the hospital remains deeply proud. In an interview for Slobodna Dalmacija, he stated that Croatia had become internationally recognized as an example of good practice in this highly sensitive and demanding field.

The ceremony was attended by Croatian Deputy Prime Minister and Minister of Croatian Veterans Tomo Medved, Deputy Mayor of Split Ivo Bilić, Split-Dalmatia County Deputy Prefect Stipe Čogelja, Member of the Parliament and the former County Prefect Ante Sanader, MSc, and Ante Mihanović, PhD. Both the Deputy Prefect and the Deputy Mayor openly expressed their pride and continued providing support to this successful team.

Minister Medved stated to the media that a total of 150 mass graves had been discovered in Croatia. To date, more than 80% of approximately 5,300 victims have been identified, while the search for 1,740 missing people continues. The Croatian identification model, developed in Split, is now internationally recognized and applied in numerous similar

PDF page 152

i da je bolnica na taj pionirski zahvat ponosna. Za Slobodnu Dalmaciju izjavio je da je Hrvatska postala prepoznata kao primjer dobre prakse u osjetljivome i zahtjevnome području.

Svečanosti su prisustvovali i ministar hrvatskih branitelja i potpredsjednik Vlade Tomo Medved, Ivo Bilić, kao zamjenik gradonačelnika Splita, splitski dožupan Stipe Čogelja, saborski zastupnik i bivši župan mr. sc. Ante Sanader i dr. sc. Ante Mihanović. Dožupan i zamjenik gradonačelnika Splita nisu krili ponos i podršku koju i dalje pružaju ovomu uspješnom timu.

Ministar Medved za medije je izjavio da je u Hrvatskoj nađeno sveukupno 150 masovnih grobnica. Do danas je identificirano više od 80 % od približno 5 300 žrtava, dok se potraga za 1 740 nestalih nastavlja. Hrvatski model identifika-

Otvaranje promocije monografije i izložbe, uvodno obraćanje ministra hrvatskih branitelja i podpredsjednika Vlade RH Tome Medveda (izvor: arhiv KBC-a Split).

Opening of the monograph and exhibition presentation, introductory address by Tomo Medved, Croatian Deputy Prime Minister and Minister of Croatian Veterans (source: archives of the University Hospital of Split).

situations worldwide, its foundation remaining an interdisciplinary approach and a lasting commitment to truth.

During the gathering, the monograph Names in the Silence was presented, edited by Professors Anđelinović, Bašić, Jerković, Kružić, and Primorac. The image on the cover depicts a doorway — a boundary between the unknown and the known. The monograph was presented by Professor Ana Marušić, Professor Željana Bašić, and Ante Gugić, MSc, retired brigadier general and representative of the Croatian Generals’ Association.

As written in the foreword itself, “It is a testimony to the encounter between humanity, science, and the world in the service of the highest values — the dignity of the victim and the family’s right to the truth.”

During her presentation, Professor Ana Marušić emphasized a simple but powerful thought:

“If something is not written down, it is as if it never happened.”

highlighting the importance of the first supplement published 35 years ago in the Croatian Medical Journal concerning the medical horrors of war.

Professor Željana Bašić emphasized that the monograph should represent the story of every victim, every family, every individual involved in exhumations, and every person who communicated identification results to relatives. She described the publication as a record of what was achieved in science and by the “coxless pair” — Professor Šimun Anđelinović and Professor Dragan Primorac.

Professor Bašić highlighted the extraordinary enthusiasm that could have carried the duo anywhere, while also stressing the historical

Promotori monografije prof. dr. sc. Ana Marušić, prof. dr. sc. Željana Bašić i mr. Ante Gugić (izvor: osobni album Ante Ivanović / Vinko Bubić).

Presenters of the monograph: Professor Ana Marušić, PhD; Professor Željana Bašić, PhD; and MSc Ante Gugić (source: Ante Ivanović / Vinko Bubić personal archive).

Na skupu je prezentirana monografija Imena u tišini urednika Anđelinovića, Bašić, Jerkovića, Kružić i Primorca. Ona na naslovnici predstavlja vrata – granicu između nepoznatoga i poznatoga. Predstavili su je prof. dr. sc. Ana Marušić, prof. dr. sc. Željana Bašić i mr. Ante Gugić, brigadni general u miru i predstavnik generalskoga zbora. Kako piše u samom predgovoru, „Ona je svjedočanstvo o susretu čovjeka, znanosti i svijeta u službi najviše vrijednosti – dostojanstva žrtve i prava obitelji na istinu.” Prof. Ana Marušić pri predstavljanju je istaknula jednu jednostavnu misao: „Ako nemate

reality that Croatia had only just achieved statehood and simultaneously faced the enormous challenge of identifying war victims. She recalled how the then-young Professor Primorac managed, within ten minutes, to establish a crucial collaboration with the father of modern forensic science, the late Professor Henry Lee (1938–2026), while also building relationships with Professor Barbara Wolf, molecular biologist Professor Moses Schanfield, Professor Mitch Holland, Professor Bruce Budowle, and others.

She also emphasized Professor Lee’s own statement that meeting the victims’ families changed his life perspective forever. As a lesser-known historical detail, she noted that Professor Lee temporarily left the O. J. Simpson trial in the United States to assist the Croatian team directly in Croatia.

Professor Bašić also underlined the importance of the opening words in the U.S. Congress, characterizing events as crimes against humanity — heinous crimes against civilians, genocide, unspeakable and unacceptable crimes.

This represented an important American message to the world at a time when many global powers were not inclined toward Croatia for various political reasons.

She further emphasized that Professor Anđelinović personally delivered the truth to nearly 500 families – to thousands of mothers, sisters, and parents – communicating identification results under emotionally devastating circumstances. She noted how difficult such conversations are, even for physicians in other specialties, when delivering bad news. As Viktor Frankl once wrote, “In some ways suffering ceases to be suffering at the moment it finds a meaning, such as the meaning of a sacrifice.” For many families, identification did not erase the pain; however, it did transform uncertainty into truth and absence into memory.

Special recognition was also given to the logistical and editorial efforts of Professor Ana Marušić and Professor Matko Marušić, who promoted Croatian scientists under the principle of “publish or perish”, primarily to ensure that history itself would not disappear.

Long ago, the father of Croatian archaeology famously stated, phrasing it into the words also known in Vienna:

“From stone comes history, and from history — consciousness.”

bilo kuda sa svojim dvojcem, ali je i naglasila povijesnu činjenicu da je naša zemlja ostvarila svoju državu i zatekla se u izazovima identifikacije tijela, gdje tada mladi prof. Primorac pred ocem svjetske forenzike u deset minuta uspijeva ostvariti bitni kontakt i suradnju s nedavno preminulim Henryjem Leejem (1938. – 2026.), ali i stvara kontakte s Barbarom Wolf, molekularnim biologom Mosesom Schanfieldom, dr. Mitchellom Hollandom itd. Ističe da je sam dr. Henry Lee rekao da je susret s obiteljima promijenio njegovu životnu perspektivu. Kao manje poznatu činjenicu naglašava da dr. Lee ostavlja suđenje O. J.-u Simpsonu u SAD-u i dolazi pomagati raditi u Hrvatsku. Istaknula je važnima i prve rečenice otvaranja američkog kongresa koje karakterizira zločine – zločin protiv čovječnosti, gnusni zločini nad civilima, genocid, neizrecivi i neprihvatljivi zločini. Bila je to američka poruka svijetu kada svjetske sile i nisu bile sklone Hrvatskoj zbog višestrukih razloga. Naglasila je da je prof. Anđelinović iznio istinu gotovo 500 obitelji i to tisućama ljudi (majkama, sestrama, roditeljima); kada to obitelj treba prihvatiti dajući svoju bol i olakšanje, što je osobito teško kada se zna koliko je liječnicima drugih specijalnosti emotivno zahtjevno kada trebaju reći pacijentima lošu dijagnozu u svom radu. Osobito je istaknula logistiku prof. Ane Marušić i prof. Matka Marušića, koji su protežirali hrvatske znanstvenike pod egidom „publiciraj ili nestani”, ponajprije misleći da ne nestane povijest. Odavno je to ustvrdio i Otac hrvatske arheologije u svojoj glasovitoj rečenici poznatoj i u Beču: „Iz kamena povijest, a iz povijesti – svijest.”

Program je pjesmom uveličala i policijska klapa „Sv. Mihovil“ osobito dirljivom obradom pjesme „Mojoj lijepoj“ od Daleke obale. Sve je to izrečeno uoči rođendana IV. gardijske brigade Pauci, čijim je predstavnicima mr. Ante Gugić uz pljesak uzvanika i osobito čestitao.

The event was further enriched by a performance from the Croatian police klapa choir “St. Michael”, particularly their emotional rendition of the song Mojoj lijepoj (To my beautiful) by Daleka Obala. The gathering was held on the eve of the 4th Guards Brigade Pauci anniversary, and its representatives were especially congratulated by Ante Gugić to the applause of the attendees.

A particularly moving aspect of the event was the exhibition displayed throughout the underground corridors of the University Hospital of Split, accompanied by the song Kad raziđe se dim (When parts away the smoke) by Vice Vukov — the voice of the unspoken silence after the Croatian Spring movement, but also the voice of the Croatian dignity and national revival.

The black-and-white exhibition by the American war photographer Phillip Farnsworth, displayed in dimly lit corridors, documents the processes of the exhumation and identification over the past three decades, gradually illuminating them more clearly each day, alongside the words of the Deputy Prime Minister:

“We will not give up.”

The exhibition, open to visitors in the hospital corridors, was presented by Associate Professor Ivan Jerković, the author of the lyrics and music for the song Imena u tišini (Names in the Silence), which emphasizes the restoration of dignity through every identification that symbolically returns people from silence.

Although chronologically the youngest member of the organizing team, often one of those who carry the greatest burden, he emphasized the legacy and foundations of forensic studies, the importance of “thinking outside the box” as Professor Anđelinović’s guiding principle, and proudly stated that the Croatian team has now developed capacities which were

30. obljetnica primjene tehnologije DNK u identifikaciji žrtava Domovinskoga rata / 30th Anniversary of the Publication of the First Paper on DNA Identification of Victims from Mass Graves

Posebnost je ovog događaja izložba u podzemnim hodnicima KBC-a Split u kojima su postavljene fotografije uz pjesmu Kad raziđe se dim Vice Vukova, toga glasa neprevedene šutnje nakon Hrvatskog proljeća, ali i glasa hrvatskoga dostojanstva i povratka oživjele Hrvatske.

Izložba fotografija američkoga ratnog fotografa Phillipa Farnswortha u crno-bijeloj tehnici u tamnim hodnicima pod prigušenim svjetlom dokumentira procese ekshumacija i identifikacija tijekom proteklih triju desetljeća i rasvjetljava ih svakim danom sve više uz rečenicu potpredsjednika vlade: „Ne ćemo odustati.” O izložbi koja se može razgledati u hodnicima KBC-a govorio je doc. dr. sc. Ivan Jerković, autor teksta i glazbe pjesme „Imena u tišini”, koja naglašava povratak dostojanstva svakom identifikacijom tijela koja vraća ljude iz tišine. Premda je kronološki najmlađi član organizacijskog tima (oni koji obično najviše i potegnu), naglasio je ostavštinu i temelje forenzičkih studija, razmišljanje „izvan okvira” kao krilaticu prof. Šimuna Anđelinovića te s ponosom ustvrdio da su razvili kapacitete da sada predstavljaju i svoja rješenja u SAD-u da im se na neki način „vrati dug”, o čemu svjedoči i posjet prof. Željane Bašić i njega osobno laboratoriju američke vojske u Nebraski, Defense POW/MIA Accounting Agency (DPAA).

Nakon energične izvedbe Ane Banovac s patinom jadranskih diva Kesovije-Šverko-Novak, uzvanici su se zaputili pregledati potresne fotografije ekshumacija. Pjesma je snimljena u suradnji s aranžerom Matom Šimunićem i prevedena na engleski jezik, čime monografija završava, i to ne slučajno. Time je znanstveno preneseno u ljudsko i engleskom auditoriju jer priča nije samo hrvatska u „pobratimstvu lica u svemiru“, kako kaže veliki Tin.

strong enough to present their own solutions in the United States, in a way “repaying the debt,” as evidenced by the visit of Professor Željana Bašić and himself to the U.S. Army laboratory, the Defense POW/MIA Accounting Agency (DPAA) in Nebraska.

After a powerful performance by Ana Banovac, whose voice carried the spirit of the Adriatic divas such as Kesovija, Šverko, and Novak, the guests viewed deeply emotional photographs of the exhumations. The song, recorded in collaboration with arranger Mate Šimunić, was translated into English and placed at the very end of the monograph, which was not done by pure coincidence. In this way, the scientific was transformed into the profoundly human and conveyed to the international audience, since this story is not exclusively Croatian, but universal, in the spirit of “the brotherhood of faces in the universe,” as expressed by the great Croatian poet Tin Ujević in Hrvatska revija (Croatian Review) 1932.

“O universe! I live and die in all beings; nameless, I endure within my brothers.”

Brotherhood of Faces in the Universe* Tin Ujević

Završni tekstovi / Closing Texts

Imena u tišini autor: Ivan Jerković

U zemlji bez glasova, bez lica i riječi, gdje šutnja je dublja od svake noći, ležali su brojevi, nečiji životi, bez imena, bez doma, u pustoj samoći.

I došli su ljudi, bez sumnje i straha, sa znanjem što svijet tek počinje znat, iz kostiju hladnih, iz pepela rata, tražili su trag da mir im mogu dat’.

Jer istina šuti, al’ nikad ne nestane, u svakoj kapi krvi ime ostane...

I kad sve izgori, i kad vrijeme izbriše, ostane nešto što ne može tiše, u svakoj niti što pamti tko smo bili — imena u tišini, oni su oživjeli.

Pod svjetlom bez sjene, tiho i sporo, gradila se nada iz najmanjih tragova, majke su čekale, godine nosile, da istina vrati ono što skrilo je grob.

I svijet je na trenutak zastao i gledao, kako istina dolazi iz zemlje i bola, bez granica, bez jezika, bez zastava, samo čovjek koji čovjeku ime dariva.

I kad sve izgori, i kad vrijeme izbriše, ostane nešto što ne može tiše, u svakoj niti što pamti tko smo bili — imena u tišini, oni su oživjeli.

Nije to bilo samo znanje… to je bio povratak dostojanstva.

I kad sve izgori, i kad vrijeme izbriše, ostane nešto što ne može tiše, i kroz godine, kroz bol i daljine — vratili su ljude… iz tišine.

„Imena u tišini” — izvedba istoimene pjesme uživo tijekom obilježavanja 30. obljetnice prvih DNK identifikacija žrtava iz masovnih grobnica u Hrvatskoj, uz kadrove izložbe i događanja (izvedba: Ana Banovac | video: Ante Ivanović). “Names in the Silence” — live performance of the song during the commemoration of the 30th anniversary of the first DNA identifications of victims from mass graves in Croatia, accompanied by footage from the exhibition and the event (Performance: Ana Banovac | Video by: Ante Ivanović).

PDF page 166

Names in the silence author: Ivan Jerković translated by; Ana Banovac

In a land with no faces, no voices, no names, Where silence runs deeper than words can contain, Numbers lying there, but their lives once were real, And all of their loved ones, they needed to heal.

And then came the people, the ones without doubts and fears, With wisdom, the world had yet to hear, From cold and broken bones, from the ashes of war, Tracing the path to give them peace once more.

For truth may be silent, but never fades away, In every drop of blood, a name will remain...

Yes, all shall burn away, and time will be our foe, Some things never change; they’re hidden below, In every fragile thread that tells us of the past — Names lost to silence are living at last.

In light without shadows, slowly and quietly, In faintest of traces, the hope was growing strong, Mothers were waiting, and all their prayers were sent For names to return and their longing to end.

And then, for a moment, the world stood still to see, The truth was rising from earth, from pain and debris, No borders, no language, no flags to divide, Just one human giving another their name back with pride.

Yes, all shall burn away, and time will be our foe, Some things never change; they’re hidden below, In every fragile thread that tells us of the past, Names lost to silence are living at last.

It was never just the knowledge… It was dignity returned.

Yes, all shall burn away, and time will be our foe, Some things never change; they’re hidden below, And through the years that passed, through all of pain unseen, From the deepest silence, their names are now free.

PDF page 167

ISBN 978-953-7288-06-8 Drugo, dopunjeno izdanje

Jer istina šuti, al nikad ne nestane, u svakoj kapi krvi ime ostane...

Pod svjetlom bez sjene, tiho i sporo, gradila se nada iz najmanjih tragova, majke su čekale, godine nosile, da istina vrati ono što skrilo je grob.

For truth may be silent, but never fades away, In every drop of blood, a name will remain...

In light without shadows, slowly and quietly, In faintest of traces, the hope was growing strong, Mothers were waiting, and all their prayers were sent For names to return and their longing to end.
